# ExecPlan 0026 — M7 agent-driven main paper decisions and bounded adaptation

**M7 is closed as of 2026-10-19 by the owner's decision, without the decision probes and the first live decision cycle, which are follow-ups ([§18](#the-boundary-m7-b-and-m7s-close-2026-10-19), [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-19--m7-closes-without-the-decision-probes-and-the-first-live-decision)).**

**Status: APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION by the owner on 2026-10-09, with the recommendation of every decision in §3.3 ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-09--execplan-0026-approved-for-implementation-with-the-recommended-decisions)). NOT IMPLEMENTED: nothing below is built, run or measured unless its line says `OBSERVED`.** **B0 is recorded in §18 as of 2026-10-10: contracts reconciled, schema v3 previewed, §2 read again (§2.8), the epoch hold tried as a script check on PostgreSQL 16 and the harness profile sized. Its PostgreSQL 17 gate run, the schema probe through the CLI and the latency read are `NOT RUN`; on 2026-10-10 the owner left them to the implementer, who decided that each runs on the owner's PC, and approved the harness profile ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-10--m7-b0-the-owner-leaves-the-latency-read-and-the-two-small-runs-to-the-implementer-and-approves-the-harness-profile)). B1 is next.** A section marked `PROPOSED` describes the approved design until its implementation evidence is recorded. Second draft of 2026-10-09, revised after two independent reviews (§17.1). Owner request of 2026-10-09 in chat, after asking whether M7 can be started while the session of the performance pull request is still at work: "start the M7 plan" ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-09--m7-plan-requested-execplan-0026)). The plan follows [ROADMAP](../../ROADMAP.md) M7 as written and treats M8 only as an interface.

**How this draft was made.** Read-only:
- No application file was edited, and no build, test, migration, install, login or model call was run.
- No Docker or Compose command addressed the normal `adaptive-spot-lab` project. Before the plan was requested, one `docker ps` listing limited to names beginning `asl-` answered the owner's question about the other session; it showed that session's isolated run only. One container without a network ran the link check on the documents.
- `.env`, `secrets/` and `codex_home` were not read.
- The contracts in `docs/` and the code of `main` at `cb4bbd4` were read in a worktree of their own. Five read-only searches mapped the execution path, the agent runtime, research with approvals and evaluation, the API with the web and the harness, and the contracts outside the authority documents. The author then read the files that §2.1, §2.2 and §2.6 cite. The facts of §2.3 to §2.5 come from the searches; where the design rests on one, its line was checked by a search of the file.
- One function of the pilot workers' build (`2f7973a`) was read from Git (§2.6).
- Five official pages were read on 2026-10-09 (§6).
- Two independent read-only reviews checked the first draft against the code and the contracts. They found two blockers and about twenty major points. Each was checked and changed the design; §17.1 lists them. A narrow second check of the revised text then found gaps in how two of the fixes were written; they are changed too, and those last changes have not been read by anyone else.

Labels: `OBSERVED` (seen in code, documents or command output), `INFERRED` (reasoned from observations), `PROPOSED`, `IMPLEMENTED`, `VERIFIED`, `NOT RUN`.

## 0. Summary

M6 gave the application research: four roles, approved evidence, isolated experiments, shadows, and promotion requests that stay pending. It gave no trading authority. M7 lets the coordinator's decisions reach the paper broker.

**What M7 delivers**
- **An operating policy the owner approves once.** `AGENT_POLICY_V1` states what the coordinator may do, from which evidence, within which finite choices, how often, and what happens when something fails. Its exact hash is approved in the UI, as the pilot's strategy was.
- **Trade intents.** The coordinator drafts an intent to enter, exit, cancel an unfilled entry or tighten a stop. An entry needs the evaluator's review of that exact draft. Hold and abstain are recorded decisions and create no intent.
- **One financial path.** An intent is a request row. The execution worker reads it inside its fenced step and runs the risk gate, sizing, reservation and fill code a strategy signal runs today. The model chooses from approved finite sets. It never supplies a price, a quantity, a stop level, a fee or a balance.
- **Authority checked until the fill.** Every step that takes, decides, retries or fills an agent's entry checks again that the permission, the policy, the bar and the price still hold. A Disable or a revocation cancels what is not yet filled.
- **Position management under the original terms.** Early exit, and a stop that only moves up, each only where the position's own terms allow it. Protection never waits for a model.
- **Triggers.** Finalized hourly bars on a declared cadence, position events and reviewed assessments start bounded workflows, with deduplication, coalescing and expiry.
- **Feedback.** Each closed position gets an outcome record computed by code and an evaluator's outcome review that later decisions may read.
- **Promotion approval.** The owner can approve a pending promotion. An approved candidate becomes a basis for an entry that code checks (decision M7-OD4).
- **Decision, policy and approval pages.**

**What it does not deliver:** partial reduction of a position (M7-OD7); a selector policy on the main account, live shadow accounts or forward ablations (M7-OD5); any real trading.

**Delivery, in three parts**
- **M7-A (implementer).** Build it, pass an isolated acceptance on fixtures with scripted models, deploy it inert beside the pilot.
- **The boundary (owner-instructed, one sitting).** After the owner has stopped the pilot: deactivate it with the existing tool, build its workers from the M7 commit and install the policy definition.
- **M7-B (owner-gated).** Approve the policy by hash; create, activate and start the new experiment; run the decision probes; one live walk-through.

**The constraint that shapes all of it** is the running pilot. It holds the main account's one experiment slot, and its workers and M1–M4 tables are not touched. M7-A therefore changes nothing the pilot uses, and agent-driven trading on the main account starts only after the owner has ended the pilot.

**Eleven decisions for the owner are in §3.3**, each with a recommendation. On 2026-10-09 the owner took every recommendation.

## 1. Goal and observable outcome (PROPOSED)

**M7-A — capability, shown in isolated stacks**
- A COORDINATOR workflow, started by a trigger, reads its portfolio snapshot and cited evidence, drafts an entry without any technical signal, obtains the evaluator's `ACCEPTABLE` review of that draft and submits it. The execution engine sizes it under `PAPER_PILOT_V1`, reserves, and fills it on a later quote. No owner action occurs between the trigger and the fill.
- The same role exits early, cancels an unfilled entry and tightens a stop where the position's terms allow, and is refused where they do not.
- Every negative case leaves the financial state unchanged, except a valid protective exit: an unreviewed entry, a stale or edited review, a choice outside the policy's sets, a price in free text, a second entry on one bar, two intents for one headroom, an intent after a revocation (§4).
- An entry that is accepted and not yet filled is canceled when the owner disables the runtime.
- A position opened by an intent is closed by its stop while the model runtime is disabled.
- A closed position has an outcome record and an outcome review; a later decision cites them.
- The owner approves a promotion; an entry on that candidate is accepted only while the candidate's own entry condition holds.
- The reference strategy's forward statements and its portable and M3 fingerprints are what they were.

**On the normal app after M7-A**
- Schema `0009`; `web`, `api` and `agent-worker` on M7 images; the pilot's containers, images, lease epoch and segment unchanged; no M7 row in any M1–M4 table.
- The policy is readable and cannot be approved: its definition is installed at the boundary.
- The agent runtime needs a new profile approval, because the definitions changed (§5.6).

**After the boundary and M7-B**
- All services on one commit; `AGENT_POLICY_V1` and `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` approved by hash; an `AGENT_DRIVEN` experiment `RUNNING`; the decision probes passed; one decision cycle recorded with real inputs (§13.4).

## 2. Current repository facts (OBSERVED at `cb4bbd4`)

Paths are relative to `services/engine/` unless they begin with `docs/`, `tools/` or `apps/`. Migrations are named by number.

### 2.1 The execution path (read by the author)

- **The forward engine runs the reference only.** The worker builds one engine per lease epoch and passes no strategy (`app/execution/worker.py:166`), so the engine takes `NativeReference` (`app/execution/engine.py:192`). The one other type it accepts is a research candidate, and only under replay in a research database (`engine.py:193-201`, `237-240`).
- **A step is one fenced transaction.** `store.fence`, fifteen phases in a fixed order, `store.recheck` before COMMIT (`engine.py:504-509`, `908-924`): load, owner requests, fills, expiries, protection, flatten, evaluations, exit orders, valuation, capture, start and resume, entries, housekeeping, run state, persist. Equity is known only after the valuation phase.
- **What forces a step.** Once per loop the engine reads four markers of what other identities wrote: owner requests, posted top-ups, owner approvals, final reports (`engine.py:315-335`). Agent rows are not among them.
- **An outside request already has a model: the owner's controls.** The API writes a request row, the engine applies it in the fenced step and records the application once (`engine.py:983-1021`; unique index `0006:123-125`). That table accepts the actors `OWNER` and `SYSTEM` and fixed kinds (`0006:105-118`), from `asl_api` and `asl_execution` only (`0006:242-250`).
- **An entry begins as a signal row.** The bar evaluation writes one `strategy_evaluations` row for every evaluated bar (`engine.py:1280`). `entries()` takes the bound strategy's rows with result `ENTRY_SIGNAL` and no outcome (`app/execution/store.py:215-220`). `_decide_entry` reads the reference price (`close`), the stop and the ATR from the row's `features`, applies the blockers, calls `size_entry`, and writes the mandate, the position, the order and the reservation (`engine.py:1784-1912`). A signal carries no size.
- **A signal can wait.** A transient reason records `DEFER` and leaves the signal pending (`engine.py:1859-1863`); an unfilled order can be tried again up to the strategy's attempt limit (`engine.py:1169`). A signal lives for the strategy's validity after its bar closes, a value read in six places.
- **Sizing is one function.** `size_entry` takes the smallest of the risk, aggregate, per-asset, total, cash, liquidity, lot and position limits, rounds down and rechecks five of them with the rounded quantity (`app/execution/risk.py:138-180`).
- **An exit is one obligation for the whole position.** `_obligate` writes one `exit_obligations` row and moves the position to `CLOSING`; a later, different reason is recorded as suppressed (`engine.py:1253-1264`). `exit_orders()` then sells `floor_to_step(min(position quantity, free))` (`engine.py:1327`). A sell order has no fill deadline (`engine.py:1365`).
- **The stop is the mandate's.** The protection phase, planned risk, the scheduling summary and a buy's fill all read `position_mandates.stop_price` (`store.py:145-148`; `engine.py:1220-1223`, `1771`, `359`, `1108`). *Corrected in §2.8:* a buy's fill reads `orders.stop_price`.
- **Not present:** a stop that moves, a reduction by decision, a cancellation at an agent's request. `_cancel` serves latches and owner requests (`engine.py:1143-1152`); a buy that waits for its fill is canceled when a blocker has appeared (`engine.py:1085-1087`).
- **Approvals are read again in every step.** A missing one opens the cause `APPROVAL_REVOKED`, cancels unfilled entries and leaves exits alone (`engine.py:1024-1027`).
- **Cost scenario H1:** arrival delay 1 s, fill window 5 s, an envelope of 20 basis points over the reference price, participation 0.5 (`app/execution/definitions.py:122-126`). The reference's signal is valid for 600 s after its bar closes (`definitions.py:82`).
- **Code identity** is a hash over every file under `app/` (`app/experiments/code_identity.py:17-33`). A difference rejects a Start; after activation it starts a new evaluation segment at the next lease.

### 2.2 What the M3 and M4 tables require (read by the author)

| Table | Constraint | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `strategy_evaluations` | `strategy_id` references `execution_definitions`; `bar_open_ms` is an hour boundary; `bar_observation_id` is required; one row per mode, strategy, symbol and bar; five result values | `0005:115-130` |
| `position_mandates` | one per evaluation, `evaluation_id` required; `stop_price < entry_reference`; `atr > 0`; append-only, no version column | `0005:156-178` |
| `positions` | one live position per mode and symbol | `0005:202-203` |
| `exit_obligations` | one per position; reason is `PROTECTIVE_STOP`, `OWNER_FLATTEN`, `TIME_EXIT` or `TREND_EXIT` | `0005:213-225`, `0006:201-204` |
| `orders` | origin is `ENTRY` or one of those four; a BUY is an `ENTRY` and nothing else is; one open order per symbol | `0005:226-271`, `0006:205-208` |
| `execution_definitions` | eight kinds, among them `STRATEGY` and `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL`; status `PROPOSED` or `FIXTURE_ONLY`; append-only | `0006:55-59`, `0005:61` |
| `experiments` | one experiment per mode in `DRAFT`, `READY`, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` or `STOPPING` | `0006:100` |
| `experiment_events` | actors `OWNER` and `SYSTEM`; five causes | `0006:31-38`, `102-107` |

Migrations 0007 and 0008 alter no M1–M4 table and reference none by foreign key; every reference in 0008 is to an `agent_`, `source_` or `research_` table.

### 2.3 The agent runtime (from the search)

- **Tools.** Catalog v2 has twelve tools and six effect classes. `request_review`, `draft_trade_intent`, `submit_trade_intent` and `request_entry_pause` are named as absent (`app/agents/definitions_v2.py:27`), and a generation's registry refuses a catalog that holds one of them.
- **Two tests draw today's line.** No generation-2 effect name may contain `ORDER`, `LEDGER`, `TRADE`, `APPROV` or `FUND` (`tests/test_m6_agent_units.py:115`). No tool handler, dispatcher, workflow or orchestrator module may import the accounting module, the owner module, the API module, the replay package, the experiment approvals or controls, or the execution package beyond three leaves: definitions, eligibility and numbers (`tests/test_m6_agent_units.py:416-425`).
- **Generations.** A `Generation` object and a lookup by template exist (`app/agents/generations.py`). Beside them the two generations are still two-way branches (`is_v2`, `G2`) in the orchestrator, the workflows, the dispatcher, the prompt composer, the artifacts, the profile, the views, the probes and the worker.
- **Children and waits.** A child workflow is a review and has a parent (a CHECK, `0008:448`). A parent in `WAITING_REVIEW` can only end (`0008` workflow guard), and a finished child ends it (`app/agents/orchestrator.py:224-232`). A child's ID follows from its parent and template, so a parent has one child per template (`app/agents/workflows.py:639`). A workflow that must go on after a wait uses `WAITING_TOOL` and one completion row.
- **The coordinator** has one template, `PROMOTION_V1`, which SYSTEM starts for each `EVIDENCE_READY` candidate (`workflows.py:1100-1111`). It reads market data, artifacts and experiments and can finish with a promotion request.
- **No account data reaches a model.** `get_snapshot` refuses every view but `MARKET` (`app/agents/tools/snapshot.py:83`); the profile has one data scope for all generations, which excludes `ACCOUNT`, `LEDGER`, `OWNER` and `PILOT_RECORDS` (`app/agents/profile.py:24`); `asl_agent` holds no privilege on any M1–M4 relation.
- **Triggers.** Three families (`0008:661`). The scheduler passes every 15 s (`workflows.py:62`) and refuses a trigger older than six hours as `STALE` (`workflows.py:1047`); a workflow it queues may wait six hours. The investigation template's hashed document names its starters, the owner and a source trigger (`definitions_v2.py:122`). No bar, position or review family exists.
- **Quiet window.** No model step starts between HH:59:30 and HH:06:00 UTC (`orchestrator.py:62-63`, `432-433`).
- **Probes.** One owner-started run holds every probe, CP-01 to CP-20, needs the state `PROBE_ONLY`, and any failure fails the run and latches the runtime. The research probes need an approved envelope and a sealed dataset (`app/agents/probes.py:1-27`).
- **Profile.** It binds the hash of every agent definition. A new or changed definition returns the runtime to `LOGIN_REQUIRED` until the owner approves a new profile and a probe run passes.
- **Budgets v2.** A parent has 12 model steps and 10 tool calls, a child 4 and 3; 24 parents a UTC day, counted in one statement over all parents that are not probes (`orchestrator.py:393`); 60 probe calls. There is no token budget, and no code assigns the quota state.
- **Reviews.** A review is bound to its target's hash when it is recorded. It has no validity period in code; an assessment's `expiry_hours` is stored and read by nothing. An artifact may cite only what its own workflow was given.
- **Authority.** The execution identity has no grant on any agent object. The permission epoch is one row that every agent commit holds `FOR KEY SHARE`; the research identity reads it through a function. A workflow's cutoff (time and publication watermark) is fixed when it starts. An owner's cancel of a workflow and a workflow's deadline raise no epoch.
- **Step schema v2:** 111 properties at nesting depth 9 ([INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE](../contracts/INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE.md#m6-b6-agentstep-schema-v2-and-queries--2026-10-05)). No real model has been sent it yet.

### 2.4 Research, promotions, approvals and evaluation (from the search)

- **Promotions cannot be approved.** A promotion is `PENDING`, `REJECTED` or `WITHDRAWN` (`0008:771`); the approval guard refuses every action but `REJECT` (`0008:807`); the candidate guard begins with "MAIN_APPROVED is unreachable in M6" (`0008:825`). A request's hashed document says `requested_scope: OWNER_CONSIDERATION_ONLY`. Nothing writes `WITHDRAWN`.
- **A candidate runs only in replay.** Its definition is `FIXTURE_ONLY`, refused in the main database, and the forward manifest's strategy slot is fixed to the reference (`app/experiments/manifest.py:18-20`, `60-61`). A candidate's stop multiple may be 1.0 to 5.0, its ATR period 5 to 50 and its hold 6 to 168 hours.
- **Two approval stores.** `owner_approvals` holds definitions and experiment manifests; the execution worker reads it in every step. `agent_approvals` holds the runtime profile, the research envelope, source versions and promotion rejections; a revocation there raises the permission epoch.
- **A manifest's definitions** must each be an `execution_definitions` row with an owner approval; Start compares each with the stored row.
- **Evaluation** compares a run with five buy-and-hold baselines on matched flows. It has no statistic between two policies. A segment starts only when the code identity changes, and the approved `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V1` states that rule and its list of measures. A closed position's exit is labeled by the origin of its first sell fill (`app/evaluation/metrics.py:122`).
- **Fingerprints** are taken over fixed table sets, one key a table (`app/replay/fingerprint.py:21-108`). Adding a table to a set changes every fingerprint.
- **Replay** takes timed owner controls as its only outside input; its driver runs timed callables. Research stores migrate to the newest revision (`app/research/migrate.py:16`), as the Compose `migrate` service does (`compose.yaml:36`).
- **Latches** are kept per mode, not per run. An unresolved one reopens as a cause when the next run is activated; a drawdown latch on a flat account stops that run.

### 2.5 API, web, harness and tests (from the search)

- **API.** 72 operations, all in `app/main.py`. No route for an intent, a policy or a decision. The schema revision `0008_m6_research` is named in sixteen files of the engine, its tests and the tools, and compared by name in, among others, `app/db.py`, `app/agents/views.py`, `app/research/identity.py` and `tools/research-activate.ps1:227`.
- **Web.** Nineteen route files, none for decisions; eighteen shadcn primitives; same-origin server functions behind the framework's CSRF check; no frame policy on any page ([ExecPlan 0023 §19](0023-m6-research-investigations.md#19-open-risks)).
- **Harness.** `tools/m6-acceptance.ps1` with fourteen modules: a run root, one Compose project per kind, test windows from HH:07 to HH:58 UTC, one heavy lock, attempt records, a report that judges a run. Phase names, budgets, pinned images and the fixture's schema revision are M6's.
- **Owner tools.** `tools/pilot-deactivate.ps1` ends a finished run: both workers stopped, both identities without login, both flags `0`. `tools/pilot-activate.ps1` enables the identities for a `READY` or `RUNNING` experiment and starts the workers ([OPERATIONS](../OPERATIONS_M3_M7.md#m4-pilot-operations-execplan-0015); `app/execution/identity.py:91`).
- **Tests.** 48 engine test files; agent tests script a model as a function over the job context through the in-memory recorded provider. Pull-request CI does not start on this repository ([TESTING](../TESTING.md)).

### 2.6 The pilot and the installation

- **The pilot.** Experiment `d779fbf4-e1be-444c-ab55-7b61292eaa87` has been `RUNNING` since 2026-09-30 on execution and evaluation workers built from `2f7973a` ([ExecPlan 0015 §16](0015-evaluation-fixed-pilot.md#16-completion-evidence)). It is open-ended; the owner named 2026-11-25 as its checkpoint, and M4 is complete once that report is recorded ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-09-30--m4-pilot-approvals-activation-and-checkpoint-execplan-0015)). No recorded decision ends it. It is the only forward run of the fixed reference.
- **The rule around it.** "Never rebuild/recreate/restart/stop or reconfigure the pilot's PostgreSQL, market, execution, evaluation or pilot-admin services; never alter their M1–M4 tables" ([OPERATIONS](../OPERATIONS_M3_M7.md#m6-research-operations-approved-contract-execplan-0023)).
- **The installation** ([STATUS](../../STATUS.md)): schema `0008`; research and sources off; the agent runtime `LOGIN_REQUIRED`; M6-B not begun; the live token-refresh check of M5 still open.
- **What the pilot's worker does with a definition it does not know (read from `2f7973a`).** Its `_load_definitions` checks each stored row against the row's own hash, and each ID the code knows against the code's hash; a row with an unknown ID passes. `INFERRED`: a new definition row would not stop that worker. This plan still installs none before the boundary (§5.1).

## 3. Scope and exclusions

### 3.1 In scope (PROPOSED)
- The operating policy `AGENT_POLICY_V1` and `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2`: documents, validation, installation, approval, revocation, and their place in the experiment manifest.
- Trade intents and their hand-off to the execution engine; the actions enter, exit, cancel and tighten; a discretionary entry pause.
- Position terms and stop updates beside the immutable mandate.
- Definitions generation 3: tools, grants, templates, step schema, budgets, prompts and probes for decisions.
- Trigger families for bars, position events and reviewed assessments.
- A portfolio snapshot for the coordinator and the intent reviewer.
- Outcome records and outcome reviews.
- Approval of a promotion, and an approved candidate as an entry basis.
- A comparison document between two isolated runs, a single-coordinator policy variant for fixtures, and a deferred replay of the reference over the agent run's window.
- Owner API routes and the decision, policy and approval pages; a frame policy for every page.
- Migration `0009_m7_agent_decisions` (additive) and a guarded command that installs the two definitions at the boundary.
- The M7 profile of the acceptance harness, `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` and `tools/m7-boundary.ps1`.
- What M6-B needs to stay possible on this build: the places that compare the schema revision by name, and its rehearsal.

### 3.2 Excluded
- **Real trading of any kind**, Testnet, Demo, exchange credentials, shorting, leverage.
- **The pilot:** no rebuild, recreate, restart or reconfiguration of its services and no change to an M1–M4 table before the boundary.
- **Partial reduction** (M7-OD7). The tool contract's `REDUCE` is refused as outside the policy's action domain.
- **A selector policy on the main account, live shadow accounts, a multi-account ledger and forward ablations** (M7-OD5).
- **A deterministic strategy that trades by itself on the main account under this policy.** An approved candidate is an entry basis (M7-OD4).
- **A fallback policy.** With no model, reviewer or required evidence there is no new exposure.
- **Resting limit orders, pyramiding, more than one position per symbol, a 4h policy, an event stream** (pages poll, as today).
- **A labeled test of the reviewer's wrong verdicts.** With scripted models it would measure the script. It belongs to the forward evidence of M8.
- **A new escalation artifact.** The promotion request is the one structured escalation; a `PortfolioDecision` may record that the coordinator asks for the owner's review, which grants nothing.
- **Any claim of profitability.** Fixture success is functional evidence only.

### 3.3 Decisions for the owner

| ID | Decision | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| M7-OD1 | **When M7's implementation may start.** The rule is one milestone at a time, and M6 is complete only with its live walk-through. (a) M7-A starts once the performance pull request is merged, on isolated stacks, while M6-B stays the owner's. (b) M7 waits until M6 is complete. | **(a).** M7-A needs M6-A's code and nothing M6-B switches on. M7's test runs yield the one heavy slot to the M6-B gate runs. M5 began the same way while M4 waits for its checkpoint. |
| M7-OD2 | **The pilot.** Agent-driven trading needs the main account's one experiment slot, so it starts only after the pilot is stopped. Stopping the pilot ends the only forward run of the fixed reference. (a) Stop it after the checkpoint report of 2026-11-25; M7's activation then waits until that date. (b) Stop it earlier, once M7-A is deployed and probed; the checkpoint report is then written at that earlier date. (c) Keep the pilot and do not activate M7 on the main account until a second account exists. | **Decide when M7-A is deployed; nothing before that depends on it.** If M7-A is ready before 2026-11-25, (b) saves the wait at the price of a shorter control run. With (a) or (b) the reference continues only as a deferred replay (M7-OD5). |
| M7-OD3 | **The values of `AGENT_POLICY_V1`** (§5.2) and of its work budgets and cadence (§5.7), among them a daily count for decision work that is kept apart from the 24 parents a day of M6. The owner approves the exact hashes of the policy and of `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` in the UI in M7-B. | **Build the proposed values in.** No measurement stands behind them and none is claimed optimal; they are chosen to keep a first run slow. Validity and cadence are compared with measured model latency before the boundary; a changed value is a new version. |
| M7-OD4 | **What approving a promotion gives.** (a) The candidate becomes an entry basis: the coordinator may enter on it, code checks that its entry condition holds, and the stop and hold stay inside the policy's own sets. The candidate's exit rule is not run. (b) The candidate trades by itself on the main account, with its exit rule run by the engine for each position. (c) Nothing yet: promotions stay reject-only. | **(a).** It gives approval an effect inside the agent-driven policy without widening it. (b) is the selector kind and needs a strategy per position in the forward engine. (c) contradicts M6-OD5. |
| M7-OD5 | **Selector, live shadows and forward ablations.** (a) M7 builds the comparison between isolated runs, a single-coordinator variant on fixtures, and a deferred replay of the reference over the agent run's window as the forward control (it needs research active). A selector, live shadow accounts and forward ablations get a plan of their own after M7-B; the roadmap gives M8 no build scope, so that plan has to be placed. (b) Build them in M7. | **(a).** The roadmap calls the selector optional. Forward variants need accounts the single-account schema does not have. What stays open is said here: the roadmap's comparison of the four variants exists only on fixtures after M7. |
| M7-OD6 | **Portfolio data in model input.** The coordinator and the intent reviewer see the paper account of the policy's own run: equity, free cash, positions, stops, pending orders, headroom and limits. It leaves the PC to the model provider, as market data and evidence extracts do. (a) Allow it, bound by the runtime profile's data scope, which the owner approves by hash. (b) Show coarse states only. | **(a).** A coordinator that cannot see a position cannot manage it. |
| M7-OD7 | **Partial reduction.** (a) Build `REDUCE` in M7-A. (b) Not in M7: the first policy enters, exits, cancels and tightens; reduction gets a small plan of its own before M8 closes acceptance row A21. | **(b).** The review found that a reduction can delay a protective exit, outlive its intent and close a position as dust unless three shared paths of the engine change. A full exit covers the need meanwhile. |
| M7-OD8 | **When M7 is complete.** (a) M7-A deployed, the boundary done, the policy approved, the experiment `RUNNING`, the decision probes passed (a live draft and its live review, with no submission), and one live decision cycle recorded, for which an abstention is enough. The first live submission, fill and outcome review are follow-ups. (b) Only after a first live submission with the engine's answer. (c) Only after a first live fill and its outcome review. | **(a).** (b) and (c) wait for a model to want a trade. With (a) the gate clause "end-to-end loop" has a live draft and review, and a live order only as a follow-up. |
| M7-OD9 | **Acceptance rules.** Carry over M6's: the quiet window, the rerun rule, one heavy stack with the pilot watch. Scope M7-A's acceptance from the start to what the inert deployment and the boundary switch on; load and live measures are conditions of the activation (§10). For `m7-g1e`'s phases after the owner's stop of the pilot on 2026-10-18 there is no watch ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-pilot-stops-before-the-rest-of-the-m7-gate-which-runs-without-the-pilot-watch)). | **Yes.** It is the scope M6-A ended with after its long run was stopped. |
| M7-OD10 | **The order of M6-B and M7-A.** (a) M6-B first, on the M6 build, when its gate is met. M7-A's deployment later stops model work until a second profile approval and probe run, and M7-A must show that research results are unchanged before it is deployed to active research services. (b) M6-B after M7-A is deployed: one profile approval, but M6's completion waits for M7-A, and the M6-B gate phases must pass on the M7 build. In either order M7-B needs M6-B's research activation first, because the capability run holds the research probes. | **(a).** M6 is then complete without waiting for M7, and a real model has seen the generation-2 schema before generation 3 is frozen. |
| M7-OD11 | **What the policy's hash binds.** The contracts ask one operating policy to bind the model, prompt, tool and source versions as well as the trading rules. (a) The policy binds every value of §5.2, its cadence, and the hashes of the generation-3 tools, schema, templates, work budgets and prompts. The model is bound by the runtime profile, which the owner approves separately; each submission records the profile it was made under, and a changed profile starts a segment. (b) The owner composes the policy in the UI from discovered models, as the profile is composed. | **(a).** A model changes more often than the trading rules, and the profile already has its own approval, probes and drift checks. (b) is a policy editor and a second way to create definitions. |

**The owner's answer, in chat on 2026-10-09: "approve with your recommendations, merge it".** Each decision is taken as recommended:

| ID | Decided | What still gates it |

| M7-OD1 | (a): M7-A may start once the performance pull request is merged, on isolated stacks | That merge. M7's test runs yield the heavy slot to the M6-B gate runs |

Until then the pilot runs as it does, and nothing of M7-A touches it. On 2026-10-18 the owner moved the stop earlier, before the rest of the M7 gate and the application update; the pilot's workers keep running until the boundary ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-pilot-stops-before-the-rest-of-the-m7-gate-which-runs-without-the-pilot-watch)) |

| M7-OD4 | (a): an approved candidate is an entry basis inside the policy's own sets | The owner approves each promotion by exact hash |

Not approved by this answer: any step of the boundary or of M7-B, any command against the normal application, any approval of a policy, protocol, profile or promotion, and any step of M6-B. The M7-A inert deployment is asked for before it runs, as M5's and M6's were.

### 3.4 Where the documents disagree, and what B0 writes

Each is settled in the contracts in B0, before code. None adds authority.

| Point | Documents | This plan |
|---|---|---|
| The policy's name | ROADMAP "AgentPolicyVersion"; [DATA_CONTRACTS](../contracts/DATA_CONTRACTS.md#agent-driven-records-and-ownership) "OperatingPolicyVersion" | One record: the operating policy of kind `AGENT_DRIVEN`, first version `AGENT_POLICY_V1` |
| What it binds | [AUTONOMY_MATRIX](../contracts/AUTONOMY_MATRIX.md#operating-envelope): model, prompt, tool and source versions too | M7-OD11 |
| Where it is approved | [API_CONTRACTS](../contracts/API_CONTRACTS.md#agent-driven-operator-resources): the existing exact-hash semantics; also a separate agent store | The M4 store, because the execution worker already reads it in every step (§5.1) |
| A separate revoke route | API_CONTRACTS lists `POST /v1/policies/{id}/revoke` | Revocation is the existing approval action, followed by a raise of the permission epoch |
| Intent states and abstention | DATA_CONTRACTS lists eight states and names an abstain result | Hold and abstain are decisions in the coordinator's artifact; no intent exists for them |
| `request_review` roles | [AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS](../AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS.md#tool-catalog) lists three roles | Granted to the coordinator for intents; assessment and candidate reviews stay fixed children |
| An agent's pause | [OPERATIONS](../OPERATIONS_M3_M7.md#m4-pilot-operations-execplan-0015) lists five latched causes | A discretionary entry pause of its own, beside those causes; only the owner closes it |
| Events | API_CONTRACTS asks for an outbox stream; M5 and M6 poll durable state | Pages poll, as today |
| Deployments during a pilot | OD-12 allows a graceful worker stop; OPERATIONS says never touch the pilot's services | The stricter rule holds until the boundary |
| "No account data in model input", "none of the new effects is financial" | AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS and [SECURITY](../../SECURITY.md) for generations 1 and 2 | Stay true for generations 1 and 2. Generation 3 adds the portfolio snapshot (M7-OD6) and two effects that queue a request toward the financial path; neither writes a financial table |
| The M4 Approvals section | [FRONTEND](../../FRONTEND.md): unchanged while the pilot runs | Unchanged until the boundary; the policy has a page of its own |
| Segments | [EVALUATION](../EVALUATION.md#agent-driven-workflow-evaluation): a model, prompt, source or policy revision starts one; the approved protocol names code identity only | `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` for agent-driven runs (§5.11) |

## 4. Requirements mapped to tests (PROPOSED)

IDs in the third column are [ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST](../../ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST.md) rows. M7 contributes partial evidence; no row is checked before M8. Evaluation gate G4 and row A38 need the live activation and are met, in part, only by M7-B.

| ID | Requirement | Rows | Tests (phase in §10) |
|---|---|---|---|
| R01 | The policy is one canonical, hashed document with every field of §5.1 set. It is approved by exact hash; an edit is another version; nothing trades under an unapproved or revoked one | A09, A27 | `PolicyDocument`; `ApiAuth7` |
| R02 | A model supplies choices from the policy's finite sets and references to server records. A price, a quantity, a stop level, a fee, a balance, a symbol outside the universe, an action outside the domain or a value outside a set is refused with no effect | A20, A28 | `DispatcherAuth7`; property tests in `IntentEngine` |
| R03 | An entry reaches an order only through the existing risk gate and sizing. The requested bound is an upper bound; code derives the reference, the stop and the envelope | A08, A20 | `IntentEngine` |
| R04 | One live position per symbol and one entry decision per symbol and finalized bar. Split requests, new operation keys and concurrent workflows cannot exceed a cap or spend unfilled proceeds | A29 | `IntentEngine` (concurrency) |
| R05 | An entry needs an `ACCEPTABLE` review bound to the draft's exact hash. It is valid until the draft expires and only while the positions, orders, pauses and latches are those of the snapshot it saw. A changed draft needs a new review. The database refuses a submission without it. A verdict never bypasses a check | A22, A31 | `DispatcherAuth7`; `IntentEngine` |
| R06 | An expired intent, a newer finalized bar, a bar that closed before the run's activation, a price beyond the deviation bound or an unusable quote has no effect, and the reason is recorded | A22, A31 | `IntentEngine` |
| R07 | Exit, cancellation and stop tightening happen only as the position's terms allow. Nothing loosens a stop, extends a hold or changes an owner. A protective exit in the same step wins | A21, A31 | `IntentEngine`; `IntentRaces` |
| R08 | A mandate and its terms survive a new policy version, a new experiment and every later choice; the database refuses an update, and a stop update that is not higher | A09 | `IntentEngine`; trigger tests |
| R09 | The discretionary limits (entries, committed and filled notional, re-entry and cancel cooldowns, submissions) are counted from immutable rows, hold across restarts and top-ups, and never block a protective exit | A30 | `IntentEngine` |
| R10 | Authority is checked by the dispatcher when a draft is recorded and when it is submitted, and by the engine in every step that takes, decides, retries or fills a discretionary entry: permission epoch, policy approval, run state, withdrawal, expiry, bar, deviation. A Disable and a revocation cancel at once what is not yet filled; an owner's cancel of a workflow and a workflow's end do so at the agent worker's next tick; all keep protection | A24, A25 | `AgentFaults7`; `IntentRaces` |
| R11 | A submission is acted on at most once. A crash between any two steps, a duplicate delivery and an unknown outcome are resolved by keys, never by a repeat | A24 | `AgentFaults7` |
| R12 | No agent identity gains a write on an M1–M4 table. No tool handler imports a module the import test forbids. The execution identity reads four agent-written tables and one function, nothing else of the agents | A14, A28 | Grant tests on PostgreSQL; the static import test; `DispatcherAuth7` |
| R13 | Bar, position and assessment triggers start bounded workflows with deduplication, a cooldown, per-family staleness, one open decision workflow, coalescing and budgets. A decision workflow ends when a newer bar finalizes. No model call per tick | A16 | `Triggers7` |
| R14 | With the model, the reviewer, the quota or the login unavailable there is no new exposure and protection continues. A recorded provider cannot make a forward decision | A32 | `AgentFaults7`; mode-guard tests |
| R15 | A model sees the positions, orders and decisions of the policy's own run and nothing of another run, of the owner or of the journal. A workflow's snapshot is stored once with its hash and bounded in size | A23, A28 | `DispatcherAuth7` |
| R16 | An outcome record is a function of financial rows, stored once with its hash. An outcome review is bound to it and changes no earlier record | A37 | `AgentOffline7` |
| R17 | Only the owner approves a promotion, by exact hash and scope. An entry on a candidate is accepted only while the candidate is approved, its exit values lie in the policy's sets and its entry condition holds; a claim of similarity gives nothing | A19 | `PromotionApproval` |
| R18 | One fixture loop runs from evidence through research, review, a reviewed intent, a modeled fill and the ledger to an outcome review, without an owner action per trade. It shows an entry without the reference's signal and an early exit | A20, A37 | `AgentOffline7` |
