### 4.1 Concurrency and failure cases

| Case | Required behavior | Test |
|---|---|---|
| Entries for BTC and SOL in one step | Decided in symbol order under the account lock; the second sizing sees the first reservation | `IntentEngine` |
| Two workflows submit an entry for one symbol | One evaluation per symbol and bar; the second is `REJECTED` as `DUPLICATE_DECISION` | `IntentEngine` |
| An entry meets a transient reason (unusable quote, missing reference) | No signal row is written; the submission waits and is tried in later steps until it expires | `IntentEngine` |
| Disable while an accepted entry waits for its fill | The order is canceled as `CAPABILITY_REVOKED` in the next step; nothing fills | `IntentRaces` |
| Disable in the step that would fill | The engine holds the epoch for that step: the fill stands with its protection, or the order is canceled; never a fill after the revocation committed | `IntentRaces` |
| The owner cancels a workflow whose entry is submitted and not yet filled | At its next tick the orchestrator ends the workflow and writes a withdrawal; the engine ends the intent as `CANCELED`, or cancels its accepted order and ends further attempts | `AgentFaults7` |
| The agent worker is down while a submission waits | No withdrawal can be written; the submission stays valid until its own expiry; Disable and the run's Pause still cancel it at once | `AgentFaults7` |
| An exit intent and a stop crossing in one step | Protection is decided first; the exit intent is `REJECTED` as `POSITION_CLOSING`; one sell | `IntentRaces` |
| A tightened stop at or above the bid | `REJECTED` as `STOP_ALREADY_CROSSED`; the stop in force stays | `IntentRaces` |
| A stop tightened while the entry is unfilled | `REJECTED`; valid after the first fill | `IntentRaces` |
| A quote older than a stop update arrives after it | It cannot trigger the new stop | `IntentRaces` |
| A cancellation racing a fill | The fill stands with its protection; the cancellation is `REJECTED` as `NOTHING_TO_CANCEL` | `IntentRaces` |
| The execution worker is down | Submissions wait and expire at their own expiry; nothing fills in retrospect | `AgentFaults7` |
| The agent worker dies after writing a submission | On restart it finds the row by its key and waits for the engine's answer; no second row | `AgentFaults7` |
| The engine dies between reservation and fill | The existing recovery; the intent still has one answer | `IntentEngine` |
| The epoch row is locked when a step wants it | The step does no intake, fills no discretionary entry and decides no policy signal; all three are tried in the next step | `IntentEngine` |
| A lease is taken while an agent-driven run is paused | The worker binds the policy; an engine bound to the reference does nothing in a step of that run and gives up its lease | `IntentEngine` |
| A top-up while an intent waits | Sizing uses posted equity only | `IntentEngine` |
| The owner stops the run with intents pending | Entries are refused as `RUN_STOPPING`; exits are still taken | `IntentEngine` |
| The review child fails or expires | The draft stays unreviewed; an entry cannot be submitted | `AgentFaults7` |
| A position changes between the snapshot and the submission | The entry is `REJECTED` as `REVIEW_STALE` | `IntentEngine` |
| A coordinator asks for an entry pause while its own entry is pending | The entry is canceled; the pause stays until the owner resumes | `IntentEngine` |
| A probe run raises the epoch | Decision workflows in flight end closed, with no effect | `AgentFaults7` |
| Migration `0009` cannot take a lock | Bounded retries, then the deployment stops before the agent worker is replaced | `Migration7` |

### 4.2 Each tool: one valid case and its denials

| Tool | Valid | Denied, with no row written beyond the denial |
|---|---|---|
| `get_snapshot`, view `PORTFOLIO` | The coordinator in a decision workflow receives the snapshot stored at its start | Another role, or any workflow that is not a decision or its intent review: `SCOPE_DENIED` |
| `draft_trade_intent` | An entry whose every choice is in its set: one immutable draft, its hash returned | Another role: `ROLE_DENIED`. A value outside a set, a number where a choice belongs, an unknown field: `SCHEMA_INVALID`. A symbol outside the universe, a position of another run, an action outside the domain: `SCOPE_DENIED`. A cited record the workflow was not given: `EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE`. No approved policy or no running run: `POLICY_PAUSED`. A third draft: `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` |
| `request_review` | A draft of this workflow that needs a review: one review child, the workflow waits and resumes once | A draft of another workflow: `SCOPE_DENIED`. A second request for one draft returns the first |
| `submit_trade_intent` | A reviewed entry, or an exit: one submission, the workflow waits for the engine's answer | An entry without a review: `REVIEW_REQUIRED`. A review of another hash, not `ACCEPTABLE` or past its validity: `REVIEW_STALE`. An entry whose cited assessment has no `ACCEPTABLE` review, is expired or is older than the policy allows: `EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE`. An expired draft: `INTENT_EXPIRED`. A changed epoch: `CAPABILITY_REVOKED`. A second submission returns the first. A probe's submission that passes all of these: refused as a probe's |
| `request_entry_pause` | An enumerated reason with a cited record: one request | Another role: `ROLE_DENIED`. Free text as the reason: `SCHEMA_INVALID`. A probe: refused |
| `record_decision` (FINISH) | A `PortfolioDecision` that names the workflow's intents | An intent of another workflow: `SCOPE_DENIED`. The states it shows are written by the server, not by the model |
| `record_review`, intent and outcome | A review of the bound hash | Another hash: `REVIEW_STALE`. Another role: `ROLE_DENIED` |

The engine's own refusals are the cases of §4.1 and R03 to R09.

## 5. Design (PROPOSED)

### 5.1 The operating policy: one record, where it lives, how it is approved

- **Record.** `AGENT_POLICY_V1` is an `execution_definitions` row of kind `STRATEGY` whose document says `policy_kind: AGENT_DRIVEN`, as the reference's document says `FIXED`. The engine needs such a row in any case: an evaluation, a mandate and an order each reference their strategy there (§2.2).
- **What the document holds** (M7-OD11, option a): the kind, the universe and the action domain; every value of §5.2; the cadence, cooldown and staleness of §5.7; the hashes of the generation-3 tool catalog, grants, step schema, templates, work budgets and prompts it is valid with; the rule for the model ("a runtime profile the owner has approved for exactly these definitions"); the rule for sources ("items of source versions approved when they were fetched"); what happens on missing or conflicting evidence and on failure; the objective, stated without a target. The risk, cost, valuation, filter, protocol and baseline definitions are named by the run's manifest, as today.
- **Approval.** The owner approves its exact hash in `owner_approvals`, as a `DEFINITION`. An experiment names it in its manifest's strategy slot; the manifest is approved as today.
- **Revocation.** A `REVOKE` is committed first. It reaches the engine in its next step as the cause `APPROVAL_REVOKED`: unfilled entries are canceled, new ones refused, the engine's exits continue (existing code, §2.1); the agents' own submissions are refused while the run is paused (§17, B8b decision 2). The API then raises the permission epoch in a second transaction and retries that if a lock is not free, so agent work in flight ends too. The engine's check does not depend on that second step.
- **Installation.** Migration `0009` writes nothing into an M1–M4 table. The policy's row and the row of `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` are installed at the boundary by one guarded command of the migration identity, run through the existing admin service. It compares each document with the code's frozen hash, refuses while a forward experiment of another strategy is `DRAFT`, `READY`, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` or `STOPPING`, and does nothing when the rows exist. Fixtures and replay databases call the same function. Before the boundary the API shows both documents and their hashes from the code as "not installed".
- **Why not a second migration.** Every default path migrates to the newest revision (§2.4), so a revision meant for the boundary would be one careless command away from the pilot. §2.6 found that the pilot's worker would tolerate the row; keeping every M1–M4 table as it is until the boundary is the rule M5 and M6 kept.
- **Versions.** A changed value is `AGENT_POLICY_V2` with its own hash and approval. A run keeps the version its manifest names; another version is another experiment. An installed row is never removed; without an approval it is inert.
- **The manifest** of an agent-driven run names the policy in its strategy slot and `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` in its protocol slot. A separate field records the hashes of the generation-3 definitions, the runtime profile, the research envelope and the source versions in force when the API composed it. The engine verifies the slots, as today; it cannot verify that field and does not try.

### 5.2 `AGENT_POLICY_V1` values (PROPOSED engineering defaults; no measurement stands behind them)

| Field | Proposed value |
|---|---|
| Universe, direction | BTCUSDT and SOLUSDT; long only; one position per symbol; no pyramiding |
| Action domain | `ENTER`, `EXIT`, `CANCEL_ENTRY`, `TIGHTEN_STOP` as intents; `HOLD` and `ABSTAIN` as decisions |
| Decision bar | The latest finalized 1h bar that closed after the run's activation. An intent is valid only while its bar is the latest one |
| Intent expiry | A choice of 10, 20 or 30 minutes after the draft |
| Entry reference | The best ask of a usable quote in the step that takes the intent; the cost scenario's envelope applies to it |
| Deviation bound | 50 basis points between the ask in a deciding step and the ask at the workflow's cutoff; beyond it the intent is rejected |
| Requested exposure | A choice of 5, 10, 15, 20 or 25% of equity as an upper bound; code takes the smallest of it and every risk limit |
| Protective stop | `ATR_STOP_V1`: reference minus a multiple of ATR(14) on finalized 1h bars; multiple 1.5, 2.0, 2.5 or 3.0 |
| Maximum hold | A choice of 24, 48 or 72 hours |
| Position terms | Early exit allowed; stop tightening by `BREAKEVEN` or by the stop template on the latest finalized close |
| Evidence for an entry | Cites a feature result of its own workflow for that symbol and bar, and an assessment for that symbol whose review is `ACCEPTABLE`, inside its own expiry and not older than 12 hours; or names an approved candidate (§5.10). The rule is enforced when the intent is submitted, by the dispatcher and by the database guard; a draft may exist without it and cannot be submitted |
| Review | An entry needs an `ACCEPTABLE` intent review of the exact draft, valid until the draft expires and while the snapshot's state holds. Exit, cancellation and tightening need none |
| Action limits | Accepted entries: one per symbol and two in total in any 24 hours. Committed entry notional plus filled exit notional in any 24 hours: at most 0.75 times equity, which is below two full-size round trips. No entry for a symbol within 2 bars of its position closing, 6 after a protective stop, also when the stop was the suppressed reason. After a cancellation no entry for that symbol on that bar or the next. Six submissions in any 24 hours |
| Failure | No model, reviewer, quota, login or required evidence: no new exposure. No fallback policy |
| Reporting | `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2`, `BASELINE_SET_V1` |

Every value is read from the policy document by code. A prompt may describe the choices; it does not own them.

### 5.3 From a model's choice to an order: the hand-off

New tables in `0009`, all named `decision_…` so that no check takes one for an M1–M4 table. None references an M1–M4 table by foreign key; a guard trigger checks what a key would.

| Table | Written by | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `decision_snapshots` | `asl_agent`, insert only, one per workflow | The portfolio snapshot (§5.8), its hash, and a state key: the hash of the run's live positions, open orders, pauses and latches |
| `decision_intents` | `asl_agent`, insert only | The draft, as below |
| `decision_submissions` | `asl_agent`, insert only, one per intent | Intent ID and hash, review artifact ID and hash where required, the cited assessment, permission epoch, profile hash, the snapshot's state key, the cutoff watermark, a context key (the hash of the profile, the approved source versions, the envelope and the approved candidates), and for a candidate basis the candidate's declaration and approval |
| `decision_withdrawals` | `asl_agent`, insert only, one per submission | Written in the transaction that ends a workflow (canceled, expired or failed) for each of its submissions whose entry is not yet filled or ended |
| `decision_verifications` | `asl_agent`, insert only | The result of a decision probe run, for a profile hash and a policy hash together (§5.6) |
| `decision_pause_requests` | `asl_agent` for a pause; `asl_api` for the owner's resume | One request, with its reason |
| `decision_events` | `asl_execution`, insert only | What the engine did: `ACCEPTED` with the order or obligation and the sizing, `REJECTED` with codes, `EXPIRED`, `CANCELED`; a pause opened or closed. One terminal event per submission |
| `decision_position_terms` | `asl_execution`, insert only, one per mandate | Policy ID and hash, intent, entry basis, stop template and multiple, whether exit and tightening are allowed |
| `decision_stop_updates` | `asl_execution`, insert only | Position, version, the new stop, the one it replaces, the intent, the quote it was checked against, its time |
| `decision_outcomes` | `asl_agent`, insert only, one per closed position | The outcome record (§5.9) and its hash |

- **An intent's document:** policy ID and hash; action; symbol or position; the choices the action takes (exposure, stop multiple, hold, expiry, or a tightening template); the entry basis; cited records; a bounded rationale, an invalidation condition and an uncertainty level; the decision bar; the snapshot's hash. The server adds the workflow, the role, the run's cost scenario and the template version.
- **States.** An intent's state is a view over these rows and the review artifact, in the eight names of [DATA_CONTRACTS](../contracts/DATA_CONTRACTS.md#agent-driven-records-and-ownership). `ACCEPTED` means the engine created an order or an obligation. What then fills is the order's state.
- **The database refuses a wrong row**, whatever the dispatcher does: another identity; a submission whose hash is not the draft's, without an `ACCEPTABLE` review where the action needs one, or a second one; an entry whose cited assessment has no `ACCEPTABLE` review or is past its expiry; a candidate basis without a current approval of that exact candidate; a stop update that is not one version on and strictly higher than the stop in force. These guards read the rows they check without locking them, so the engine's step never waits on an agent's commit.
- **Grants.** `asl_execution` may select the intents, submissions, withdrawals and pause requests, and execute one function that holds the permission epoch. `asl_agent` reads the engine's rows and the run's financial rows through `agent_read` views (§5.8). Neither gains anything else of the other's.
- **Keys.** A draft's ID follows from its tool call's server-made operation key, a submission's from its intent, an evaluation's from mode, policy, symbol and bar, as today. The same request twice is the same row.

### 5.4 The engine's intake and the actions

- **Binding.** When the worker takes the lease it reads the strategy that the mode's open experiment names, in any of the states `READY`, `RUNNING`, `PAUSED` and `STOPPING`. For the reference it builds the engine as today. For the policy it builds it with an `AgentPolicy` object behind the existing strategy interface, without the research-database requirement a candidate has. The engine compares its binding with the open experiment's strategy in every step. On a difference it does nothing in that step, and the worker gives up its lease and binds again at once. An engine bound to the reference therefore never manages a position of the policy, also after a lease taken during a pause.
- **The policy engine does not evaluate bars.** The reference's bar evaluation writes a row for every bar under the key the intake needs, so it is not run. The policy engine keeps its own scheduling state. Its loop reads four more markers: the newest submission, withdrawal and pause request, and the permission epoch. A step is forced by an unanswered submission, by an open discretionary order or signal, and by a changed epoch while either exists. Bars are read before the transaction, only for a symbol with a waiting entry or tightening. (Built otherwise in B3: three markers, and bars read inside the step; §17, B3's decisions 9 and 14.) After downtime a submission past its expiry is ended as `EXPIRED` in the first step; nothing is caught up.
- **Three places in a step,** all only in an engine bound to the policy. Every statement the reference engine issues stays as it is; what the policy engine reads in addition are statements of its own (R19).
  1. *At the start, before fills:* in a step with an unanswered submission, a waiting policy signal or an open discretionary entry order, the engine holds the permission epoch once (§5.5).
  2. *Intake, directly before `entries()`:* after valuation, so equity and references are known.
  3. *After `entries()`:* the engine's answer is written, because sizing can still reject.

| Action | What the engine does in the fenced step | Refused when |
|---|---|---|
| `ENTER` | Checks authority, validity, the choices against the policy document, the review and its state key, the limits and the market. Only when nothing, final or transient, stands against it: computes the reference, the ATR and the stop and writes a `strategy_evaluations` row with result `ENTRY_SIGNAL` for the decision bar. The existing `entries()` phase of the same step then decides, sizes, and writes mandate, position, order and reservation | A position or an order exists for the symbol; an entry decision exists for that bar; a limit is spent; a pause or a blocker is open; the bar is no longer the latest; the price moved past the bound; the state key differs |
| `EXIT` | Calls the existing obligation code with reason `TREND_EXIT`, the reason the mandate's own exit rule has; the existing exit phase sells in the next step | The terms do not allow it; the position is not open; an obligation already exists |
| `CANCEL_ENTRY` | Cancels the run's own accepted, unfilled entry order through the existing cancel code, or ends its waiting signal | Nothing is pending; the order has filled |
| `TIGHTEN_STOP` | Computes the new stop from the template; requires it above the stop in force and below the bid; appends a `decision_stop_updates` row | The terms do not allow it; not above the stop in force; at or above the bid; no first fill yet |
| Entry pause | Opens a pause and cancels the run's unfilled discretionary entries. Only an owner's resume closes it | — |

- **An entry is checked until it is filled.** The signal row carries the reference as `entry_reference` (the bar's own close stays in `close`), the stop, the ATR, the hold, the requested bound, the intent and its expiry. The policy's strategy object gives a validity of one bar and the reference's attempt limit; the six places that read the validity keep reading it, and the intent's shorter expiry is read from the row by the policy engine's own checks. Before each decision, each further attempt and each fill of a discretionary entry the engine checks again: the epoch, a withdrawal, the expiry, the bar, the deviation and the pause. If one fails, the signal is ended or the order canceled through the existing cancel code. A buy that waits for its fill is canceled today when a blocker appears; a moved epoch is treated the same way. In a step in which the epoch could not be held, the engine does no intake, fills no discretionary entry and decides no policy signal, so no attempt creates an order in it.
- **Why the reference is the ask at intake.** A workflow with a review takes minutes, and no model step starts before HH:06. H1's envelope is 20 basis points. Against the bar's close an entry would be refused whenever the price had risen and taken whenever it had fallen. The envelope keeps its purpose, the seconds between decision and fill; the deviation bound covers the minutes before.
- **The stop in force.** The policy engine reads it, the mandate's stop or the latest update, in its own load and summary statements; protection, planned risk and the stale-quote check use it. A quote triggers an updated stop only if its source time is later than the update. The API's risk and position views show the stop in force beside the original.
- **Reused values.** An agent's exit carries the order origin and obligation reason `TREND_EXIT`, as a candidate's signal exit does since M6; the mandate's exit rule names `AGENT_DISCRETIONARY_EXIT_V1`, and the engine's event names the intent. No M3 or M4 constraint changes. The report's trade list labels an exit from the position's terms, not from the order's origin alone.
- **Fingerprints.** The portable and M3 table sets do not change, so the reference's fingerprints do not. The new tables have a decision fingerprint of their own.
- **Replay.** An intent stream with its recorded times is a timed input of a replay, like owner controls (R26).
- **What partial reduction would need,** for the plan that builds it (M7-OD7): a fill deadline for a sell without an obligation; the obligation code canceling an open reduction, so that a protective exit is never delayed; no dust close after a reduction.

### 5.5 Authority at three boundaries

| Where | Who checks | What |
|---|---|---|
| A draft is recorded | Dispatcher, as `asl_agent` | Role and grant, epoch, runtime state, profile, workflow open, deadline, budget; the policy approved and its run `RUNNING`, read through a view; every choice in its set; every cited record in the workflow's scope |
| An intent is submitted | Dispatcher, and the database guard | All of the above again; the draft unexpired; the review bound and `ACCEPTABLE`; the entry's evidence rule; no equal intent outstanding |
| The engine takes an intent | Execution engine, in the fenced step | The epoch equal to the submission's; no withdrawal; the policy approved; the run live and unblocked; expiry; the bar; the deviation; the state key; then every financial check |
| The engine decides again, retries or fills | Execution engine, in the fenced step | The epoch, a withdrawal, the policy's approval, the run's blockers, the expiry, the bar, the deviation and the pause. Not the state key: the engine's own order has changed it |

- **What the engine cannot see** reaches it in two ways. A Disable, a revocation and a probe's revocation raise the epoch, at once. An owner's cancel of a workflow, a workflow's deadline and a workflow's failure become a withdrawal when the orchestrator ends the workflow, at its next tick of one second, and not while the agent worker is down. The owner's immediate controls over an entry are therefore Disable and the run's Pause; a workflow cancel follows within a tick, and an intent's own expiry bounds everything else. The runtime state, the profile and the workflow are the dispatcher's to check.
- **The epoch is held once a step, without waiting.** At the start of a step that needs it (§5.4), the function selects the epoch row `FOR KEY SHARE SKIP LOCKED`, the mode agent commits use, and the hold lasts until the step commits. A revocation then waits for that step or comes before it; no fill commits after a revocation has. When the row cannot be locked at once the function returns nothing; the step then does no intake, fills no discretionary entry and decides no policy signal, and goes on with everything else. Protection never waits on an agent-side row, and no error is raised inside the step.
- **Disable gets the retry the other owner requests have.** Its epoch update now also waits for an engine step that holds the epoch. Today a wait over one second is an unhandled error; with M7 it is retried and, if it still fails, answered as `503 TRY_AGAIN`, like the approvals.
- **B0 is a gate before B3.** It tries the hold on a disposable PostgreSQL 17: one engine stepping alone, back to back, must not starve a waiting epoch update, and `SKIP LOCKED` must return nothing while the update runs. (Amended on 2026-10-11 on the owner's delegation, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-11--m7-b1-the-owner-has-b0-merged-and-leaves-the-heavy-run-timing-and-the-epoch-hold-to-the-implementer). The rule first said that no new share lock may starve the update; B0 found that agent commit checks overlapping one another do so today, with or without the engine and under the fallback too, §18.) If either fails, the fallback is a plain read of the epoch at the start of the step and again immediately before COMMIT, with the step rolled back on a change. That leaves the commit itself as the window in which a fill can pass a revocation.
- **The engine trusts no value an agent row carries where it can read the fact itself:** the quote at the cutoff watermark, the bars, the position, the policy document, the state key.
- An intent the engine accepted and filled before a revocation committed stands, with its protection.

### 5.6 Agent definitions, generation 3

- **First the lookup is completed.** The `Generation` object exists; the remaining two-way branches become lookups on it. Generations 1 and 2 keep every hash; their suites pass unchanged.
- **Tools added** (catalog v3 holds catalog v2 and these):

| Tool | Role | Effect | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `get_snapshot`, view `PORTFOLIO` | COORDINATOR; EVALUATOR in an intent review | `READ` | The snapshot stored at the workflow's start (§5.8) |
| `draft_trade_intent` | COORDINATOR | `RECORD_INTENT_DRAFT` | At most two drafts a workflow; returns the ID and hash |
| `request_review` | COORDINATOR | `QUEUE_REVIEW` | Creates the review child for that draft; the workflow waits |
| `submit_trade_intent` | COORDINATOR | `QUEUE_MAIN_INTENT` | Writes the submission; the workflow waits for the engine's answer |
| `request_entry_pause` | COORDINATOR | `QUEUE_ENTRY_PAUSE` | An enumerated reason and cited records |
| `record_decision` | COORDINATOR | `RECORD_AGENT_ARTIFACT`, FINISH only | The `PortfolioDecision`: per symbol the action taken or none, the intent, the reason, and whether the owner's review is asked for |
| `record_review`, types `INTENT_REVIEW` and `OUTCOME_REVIEW` | EVALUATOR | `RECORD_AGENT_ARTIFACT`, FINISH only | Bound to the draft's or the outcome record's hash |

- **The line the tests draw moves, deliberately.** Generation 3 has four new effect classes. Two of them, `QUEUE_MAIN_INTENT` and `QUEUE_ENTRY_PAUSE`, queue a request toward the financial path. The import test stays as it is: a handler writes hand-off rows and imports no broker module. The policy document's parser lives in a module both sides may import, and the import test names it.
- **Templates.**

| Template | Role | Started by | Then |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MARKET_BRIEF_V1` | ANALYST | The cadence; the owner | A fixed review child. A brief whose review is `ACCEPTABLE` records an `ASSESSMENT_READY` trigger |
| `PORTFOLIO_DECISION_V1` | COORDINATOR | `ASSESSMENT_READY`, `BAR_CLOSE`, `POSITION_EVENT`; the owner | Drafts, reviews and submissions inside its budget; ends with a `PortfolioDecision` |
| `INTENT_REVIEW_V1` | EVALUATOR | The orchestrator, on `request_review` | A review bound to the draft's hash, the policy's hash and the snapshot's hash |
| `OUTCOME_REVIEW_V1` | EVALUATOR | SYSTEM, when a position closes | A review bound to the outcome record's hash |
| `PROMOTION_V2` | COORDINATOR | SYSTEM or the owner, for an `EVIDENCE_READY` candidate | A request whose scope the server writes: entry basis under `AGENT_POLICY_V1` |

  The generation-2 investigation cannot be the brief: its hashed document names its starters (§2.3).
- **What generation 3 changes in existing guards and code; B1 lists each with its test.**
  - A coordinator waits for an intent review as a tool wait with a completion, because `WAITING_REVIEW` can only end. The completions gain two kinds, a review and the engine's answer.
  - `INTENT_REVIEW` joins the child purposes of the CHECK; a finished intent review resumes its parent and does not end it; a review child's ID follows from the draft, so a workflow can have two.
  - `OUTCOME_REVIEW`, `DECISION` and `BRIEF` are purposes of parents; the lists of purposes, creators, artifact kinds, trigger families and owner-startable pairs grow.
  - A decision workflow queued by the scheduler expires from the queue with its trigger, not after six hours.
  - Decision work has a daily count of its own (M7-OD3); the statement that counts parents changes.
  - Once the policy is installed SYSTEM starts `PROMOTION_V2`, no longer `PROMOTION_V1`. A pending `PROMOTION_V1` request stays what its text says, consideration only; when a `PROMOTION_V2` request is recorded for that candidate, SYSTEM marks the older one `WITHDRAWN`.
  - The promotion gains the state `APPROVED` and the candidate the transition to `MAIN_APPROVED`, both only with the owner's approval row, which the guard reads.
  - The profile becomes version 3: a data scope per generation, and a consequence text that names the decision authority.
- **Step schema v3.** The argument object gains a flat `intent` object with the fields of §5.3, and `intent_id`, `review_id`, `pause_reason` and `pause_cites` (the cited records `request_entry_pause` takes; B0's preview adds it); `finish` gains `decision`. B0 measures it against the limits of §6 with the pinned CLI and the mock, as B0 of M6 did for v2. That shows the CLI transmits it. Whether a real model accepts it is shown first by a capability probe (below).
- **Prompts** `prompts/m7/`: `coordinator.v2.md`, `evaluator.v3.md`, `analyst.v3.md`; versioned, hashed, packaged like M6's. A prompt names only tools its role holds.
- **Two probe suites.**
  - *The capability suite* stays one run in `PROBE_ONLY`, CP-01 to CP-22. It gains two probes that need no policy. CP-21 is a generation-3 decision workflow that returns a valid abstention, the first real-model evidence for schema v3. A decision workflow of purpose `PROBE` starts without a policy and without a run: its snapshot is the empty one with a fixed state key, and the snapshot's guard admits that for a probe only. CP-22 is an analyst that asks for the portfolio view and for `draft_trade_intent`. The suite still holds the research probes, so it needs an approved envelope and a sealed dataset, as today. It can pass before the boundary, and it gives the measured step latency.
  - *The decision suite* is a second, owner-started run, CP-23 to CP-27. It needs the approved policy and its running run, raises no epoch, and runs in `PROBE_ONLY` or `ENABLED`; every workflow in it has the purpose `PROBE`. Its probes: a value outside a set; a price written into the rationale; a submission without a review; a review of another draft; and one positive chain, which is a probe's own brief with its review, a draft that cites it, and that draft's review, whatever the two verdicts are.
  - **A probe never writes a submission or a pause request.** The dispatcher applies its ordinary checks first and records their code, so a missing or wrong review is shown as such; a submission that would otherwise be accepted is refused because it is a probe's, as a probe records no promotion today.
  - *Its record.* The run is a row of `agent_probe_runs` with a new suite column; its result is a row of `decision_verifications` for the profile hash and the policy hash together. It is never written into the capability record, which Enable and the drift check read. Without a passing result the scheduler starts no decision workflow. A failure blocks decision workflows only; a runner violation latches the runtime as today. B5 lists the places that today assume one suite: the `PROBE_ONLY` requirement and the probe count in `app/agents/probes.py`, and the checks in the orchestrator, the controls and the runtime.
  - Offline both suites run with scripted models in M7-A.

### 5.7 Triggers, cadence, priority and budgets

- **Families added:** `BAR_CLOSE`, `POSITION_EVENT`, `ASSESSMENT_READY`. The agent worker's scheduler writes them from the `agent_read` views: a new finalized bar that the cadence names, an entry fill, a closed position, an assessment whose review is `ACCEPTABLE`. That includes the assessment of a source investigation, so a source revision reaches a decision through its reviewed assessment.
- **Cadence (PROPOSED).** A market brief at the bars closing 00, 06, 12 and 18 UTC. A decision after each reviewed assessment, at every bar close while a position or an entry is open, and after an entry fill or a position's end.
- **Deduplication and coalescing.** A key per bar and cadence slot, per position and event, per assessment. One open decision workflow at a time. While one is open, the newest waiting trigger is kept and an older waiting one is recorded as superseded; the kept one starts when the open workflow ends, if it is not stale.
- **Cooldown.** At least ten minutes between two decision workflow starts, except after a position event.
- **Superseded work.** A decision workflow ends as `EXPIRED` with `BAR_SUPERSEDED` when a newer bar finalizes before it has submitted; its intent would be invalid.
- **Staleness.** `BAR_CLOSE` 20 minutes, the others 30. A stale trigger is recorded as missed and starts nothing.
- **Priority.** Probes; owner-started work; decision workflows and their reviews; promotion; briefs and investigations; research.
- **Quiet window.** Unchanged, HH:59:30 to HH:06:00 UTC. A decision after a bar close therefore starts at HH:06 at the earliest.
- **Budgets v3 (PROPOSED).**
  - A decision workflow: 10 model steps, 8 tool calls, two drafts, two submissions; 900 s running; 300 s waiting for a review; the engine's answer is awaited until the intent's expiry plus 60 s.
  - An intent review: 4 steps, 3 tool calls, 300 s. An outcome review: 4 steps, 3 tool calls, 600 s. A brief: 8 steps and 6 tool calls, with a review child of 4 and 3.
  - A UTC day: 36 decision workflows (24 bar closes, 4 briefs and 8 position events at most), 4 briefs, 8 outcome reviews, counted apart from the 24 parents of generations 1 and 2.
  - The capability run: at most 70 live calls for 22 probes. The decision run: at most 25 for 5.
- **Not read for this plan:** how long a live step takes. Fourteen live probes ran on 2026-10-02; their durations are in the normal database. B0 asks the owner for one read-only query. How many calls a day the subscription allows is not known and is observed in M7-B.

### 5.8 What a model is shown

- **A snapshot, taken once.** When a decision workflow starts, the worker reads the `agent_read` portfolio views, stores the result in `decision_snapshots` with its hash and state key, and binds it to the workflow. Every read in that workflow returns it. The job context carries it, so no step is spent fetching it. It is at most 8 KiB of the 64 KiB prompt.
- **Content.** Equity and free cash from the run's latest valid valuation; for each symbol the position's quantity, average cost, stop in force, time exit and terms; an unfilled entry; remaining headroom under each risk limit; open pauses, and the account's open blockers by code; the action limits and what is used; the run's last eight intents with their states and reasons; the last four outcome records.
- **Scope.** Positions, orders, fills, intents and events are rows of the one run whose manifest names the approved policy; another run's rows are never in the views. Equity, cash, holdings and latches belong to the account, not to a run; they are shown only while the policy's run is live.
- **Other records a decision may cite.** The server puts into the workflow's scope the newest reviewed assessment for each symbol and the newest outcome reviews, as a review child is given its target today. The model does not choose them.
- **Less for the others.** The intent reviewer gets the draft, the cited records and the snapshot. The outcome reviewer gets the outcome record and the original draft and review, not the portfolio.
- **Never shown:** owner records, sessions, the journal, funding requests, another run, secrets, raw payloads.
- **Trust.** The snapshot is what the model reasoned on. The engine reads the current state itself.

### 5.9 Reviews, outcomes and feedback

- **Intent review.** The reviewer's context holds the draft, the cited records, the policy's summary and the snapshot. Its verdict is bound to the three hashes. It is valid until the draft expires and while the state key holds. `NEEDS_EVIDENCE` and `REJECT` end that draft; a new draft needs a new review.
- **Outcome record.** When a position closes, the worker builds its record from the views and stores it once with its hash: the intent, the review, the risk decision, each order and fill, fees and modeled costs, realized result, planned against realized loss, holding time, how it ended, gaps and downtime in its life. It is a function of financial rows; the API can compute it again and compare.
- **Outcome review.** SYSTEM starts one for each closed position. The evaluator separates what the evidence supported from what happened; it changes no earlier record and grants nothing.
- **Use.** A later brief or decision may cite outcome records and reviews (§5.8). They are typed artifacts with their times; none becomes a price, a balance or a measured fact.

### 5.10 Promotion approval and main eligibility (M7-OD4, option a)

- **Approval.** `agent_approvals` accepts `APPROVE` for a `PROMOTION_V2` request. Its scope is written by the server: entry basis under `AGENT_POLICY_V1`. The approval is refused when the candidate's exit values lie outside the policy's sets: ATR period 14, a stop multiple of 1.5, 2.0, 2.5 or 3.0, a hold of 24, 48 or 72 hours. The candidate guard admits `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING` to `MAIN_APPROVED` only with that approval row. A revocation suspends the candidate and raises the epoch.
- **Effect.** An `ENTER` may name an approved candidate as its basis in place of a reviewed assessment. The submission carries the candidate's declaration; the database guard has checked it against the approved candidate. In the fenced step the engine evaluates the candidate's entry condition on the decision bar with the M6 interpreter, as a pure function. If it holds (as built in B6a: the candidate's own signal is `ENTRY_SIGNAL`, so its exit signal does not hold at the same bar; §17, B6a decision 17), the position takes the candidate's stop multiple and hold, and its terms name the candidate and the approval. If it does not, the intent is rejected. The engine needs no grant on a research table.
- **What stays the coordinator's.** The entry still needs its intent review, and the exit is discretionary. The candidate's exit signal at the latest bar is shown in the snapshot as a computed fact.
- **What it is not.** The candidate does not trade by itself. Its exit rule is not run, so its shadow's results describe another rule set than the main account's, and the pages say so.

### 5.11 Evaluation, segments and comparisons

- **`EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2`** is V1 with two additions, and applies to agent-driven runs only; the pilot keeps V1.
  - *Segments.* A segment starts when the code identity changes, as today, and also when the runtime profile, an approved source version, the research envelope or the set of approved candidates changes. Each submission carries one context key over those four; the engine records a segment at the first submission whose key differs from the last. Another policy version is another experiment.
  - *Measures.* Decisions by action, state and reason; review verdicts and the claims reviews marked unsupported; time from trigger to submission, to the engine's answer and to the fill; model steps and tool calls per decision; expired, invalid and withdrawn intents; incomplete workflows; login and quota failures; pauses and owner interventions; the action limits and their use.
- **Reports** stay functions of stored rows. Entries appear in the signal sections through their evaluation rows.
- **Comparison.** A new document compares two isolated runs on one fixture: hourly paired differences with the protocol's bootstrap, the same sample minimums and no threshold. M7-A produces it for the full workflow, the single-coordinator variant `AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO` (fixture only: no brief, no intent review) and the reference. With scripted models it shows that the comparison works, not that a role helps.
- **The forward control after the pilot** (M7-OD5, option a). SYSTEM keeps one deferred replay of the reference over the agent run's window: frozen at the run's activation, advanced daily by the research runner, outside the trial budget, with the labels a shadow carries. It runs only while research is active. It is not a forward result and is never shown as one.
- **Forward comparisons** on the main account stay the M4 baselines on matched flows. The roadmap's paired comparison of the four variants exists after M7 on fixtures only.

### 5.12 Owner API and pages

- **Routes** (the session, CSRF, Host, strict-body, idempotency and no-store rules of M4 to M6):
  - reads: `GET /v1/trade-intents`, `/v1/trade-intents/{id}`, `/v1/policies`, `/v1/policies/{id}/versions`, `/v1/entry-pauses`, `/v1/outcomes`, `/v1/outcomes/{position_id}`;
  - owner requests: `POST /v1/entry-pauses/{id}/resume`; `POST /v1/agent-runtime/decision-probe`;
  - `/v1/approvals` raises the epoch after it has recorded the revocation of an agent-driven policy; `/v1/agent-approvals` accepts `APPROVE` for a promotion; `/v1/experiments` accepts a policy ID; `/v1/agent-workflows` accepts the owner-startable generation-3 templates.
- No route accepts an intent, a tool name, a price or a quantity from the browser.
- `SystemStatus` gains `decisions`. The web accepts both shapes during the rollout; the web is deployed before the API.
- **Pages** (the official shadcn skill and project discovery at implementation; existing primitives only; the Lightweight Charts skill if a chart changes):
  - `/decisions`: intents with their states; requested, permitted, submitted, accepted and filled as separate columns.
  - `/decisions/{id}`: the draft, its review, the submission, the engine's events with reason codes, the order and fills, the position's terms and stop history, the outcome record and review.
  - `/policies`: each policy's document, hash and state, "not installed" before the boundary, the difference from the previous version.
  - Approvals: once the policy is installed, a "Main operating policy" section with the consequences of approve and revoke; promotions gain Approve with their scope. Until then the page is as it is.
  - Experiments: the draft form offers the policies that are installed. Risk: the action limits and their use, the stop in force, an open entry pause with its resume. Agents: the new templates, the decision probe, and the role grants of each generation, which covers part of the "Autonomy and health" row of FRONTEND.
- **Labels:** `MODELED FILLS`, `HYPOTHETICAL COST SCENARIO`, "model-authored — untrusted", "discretionary — not a calibrated probability".
- **Frame policy.** Every page is sent with a header that forbids framing. M6 left this open; a page that approves a trading policy must not be framable.

### 5.13 Invariants

1. Only the execution identity creates an order, a reservation or a fill. An intent is a request.
2. No agent identity can write an M1–M4 table.
3. Every intent passes the risk gate, sizing, reservation and fill code a strategy signal passes.
4. A model supplies choices from finite approved sets and references to server records, never a price, a quantity, a stop level, a fee or a balance.
5. One live position per symbol; one entry decision per symbol and finalized bar.
6. A submission is acted on at most once.
7. Authority is checked at the draft, at the submission, and in every step until an entry is filled.
8. Protection depends on no model, review, intent, pause, budget or agent-side lock.
9. A stop only moves up. A mandate and its terms never change.
10. A discretionary sell exists only as the one exit obligation a position can have.
11. The reference engine's statements and its portable and M3 fingerprints are unchanged.
12. A forward decision comes only from live inference.
13. A model sees the positions, orders and decisions of the policy's own run and of no other.
14. No promotion, policy, protocol or profile is approved by anything but the owner's exact-hash action.
15. A probe hands nothing to the engine.

## 6. Official source and version evidence

Read on 2026-10-09 through the authoring client's page fetch, which returns an extract of a page. A manual page is not proof of this application's behavior. Versions as [INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE](../contracts/INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE.md#m6-b0-official-guidance--2026-10-03) records them from the lock files: FastAPI 0.141.1, Pydantic 2.13.5, SQLAlchemy 2.0.54, psycopg 3.3.6, HTTPX 0.28.1, PostgreSQL 17.11, Codex `rust-v0.159.2`. B0 reads them again. No dependency is added. `OBSERVED` on 2026-10-10 at `1da608f`: `services/engine/uv.lock` names FastAPI 0.141.1, Pydantic 2.13.5, SQLAlchemy 2.0.54, psycopg 3.3.6, HTTPX 0.28.1 and Alembic 1.20.0; `compose.yaml` names `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24`; `services/agent-runner/runner/invocation.py:16` pins `rust-v0.159.2`. None changed.

| Page | Observed | Use |
|---|---|---|
| [Structured Outputs](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/structured-outputs), "Supported schemas", reached by reading the page from character 100,000 on | "up to 5000 object properties total, with up to 10 levels of nesting"; names and enumeration values at most 120,000 characters; 1,000 enumeration values; every field required; "`additionalProperties: false` must always be set in objects"; the root is an object and not `anyOf` | Schema v3 adds one flat object to a schema that is at depth 9. That these API limits hold on the CLI path is `INFERRED`; B0 measures the real schema with the pinned CLI and the mock, and a capability probe shows a real model's answer |
| [PostgreSQL 17, ALTER TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-altertable.html) | "`ADD FOREIGN KEY` also acquires a `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` lock on the referenced table" | No M7 table references an M1–M4 table by foreign key; guard triggers check instead |
| [PostgreSQL 17, explicit locking](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/explicit-locking.html) | `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` conflicts with `ROW EXCLUSIVE`, the mode of `INSERT` and `UPDATE`; "Once acquired, a lock is normally held until the end of the transaction" | Such a key would hold the pilot's writes for the length of the migration |
| [PostgreSQL 17, SELECT](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-select.html) | A locking clause "requires `UPDATE` privilege as well"; "With `NOWAIT`, the statement reports an error"; "With `SKIP LOCKED`, any selected rows that cannot be immediately locked are skipped" | The engine holds the epoch through a definer function with `SKIP LOCKED`, so a locked row gives no error inside the step (§5.5) |
| [Content Security Policy Level 3](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/) | `frame-ancestors` is not supported in a `meta` element; with it "the `X-Frame-Options` header will be ignored". The directive's own definition was not reached by the fetch | The frame policy is a response header; B8 reads the directive's section before it is set |

To read at implementation, each in its batch: the PostgreSQL pages for partial unique indexes and for views with a security barrier; FastAPI and Pydantic for the new bodies; TanStack Query, Table and Router for the pages; the TanStack Start page for response headers; the official shadcn skill and, if a chart changes, the Lightweight Charts skill.

## 7. Data and trust-boundary changes (PROPOSED)

- **New model input.** The portfolio snapshot of the policy's own run, for the coordinator and the intent reviewer; the outcome record for the outcome reviewer. Everything else of the account stays excluded. The profile's data scope for generation 3 names it, and the owner approves that profile by hash.
- **New read paths.** `asl_agent` reads rows of M3 and M4 tables for the first time, through views the migration identity owns and that are limited to the policy's run. `asl_execution` reads four agent-written tables and holds the permission epoch through one function; it treats what it reads as a request to validate. *B6c:* `asl_research` reads the main account's activated agent-driven runs (run, state, policy, activation and end, nothing else) through one view, for the control replay (§17, B6c decision 2).
- **New write paths.** None into an M1–M4 table for any agent identity. The execution identity writes three new tables.
- **New authority.** The coordinator may request an entry, an exit, a cancellation, a tightening and an entry pause under an approved policy. The owner approves a policy, a protocol and a promotion. No role can approve, raise a limit, clear a latch or resume a pause.
- **New stored data.** Drafts with rationales (bounded, model-authored, untrusted), reviews, snapshots, outcome records, stop updates, pauses.
- **No new outbound path,** credential or service.

- `services/engine/app/execution/`: `engine.py` (the binding, the policy engine's scheduling, the intake phase, the checks before a decision and a fill), `store.py` (the policy engine's own reads and writes; the reference's statements unchanged), `risk.py` (an optional requested limit), `worker.py` (the binding at the lease), a new `intents.py`, `definitions.py` (the two documents), `identity.py` (the install command), `views.py` (the stop in force).

- `services/engine/app/agents/`: the generation lookup; `definitions_v3.py`, `schemas_v3.py`, `artifacts_v3.py`; `tools/` (snapshot view, intent tools, pause); `workflows.py`, `orchestrator.py`, `dispatcher.py`, `prompting.py`, `profile.py`, `probes.py`, `views.py`, `approvals.py`.

- `services/engine/alembic/versions/0009_m7_agent_decisions.py`.
- `services/agent-runner/prompts/m7/`; `services/engine/Dockerfile` (the prompt copy).

Not touched: `app/market_data/`, `app/accounting.py`, migrations 0001 to 0008, the pilot's Compose blocks, the agent runner and its egress gateway.

- Each batch is one pull request against `main`, with its documents. Before it is reported, its own phases have passed in an authoring run and an independent read-only review has read its diff; M6's reviews found defects in every batch they read.

## 10. Verification commands (PROPOSED; not implemented)

```powershell
./tools/m7-acceptance.ps1 -RunId <run> -Phase <phase>
```

- **No second harness.** The command is a profile of the M6 harness: its windows, lock, attempt records, Docker calls, cleanup and report skeleton, under the run root `.cache/m7` and projects `asl-m7-<run>-<kind>`. What is M6's by content (phase lists, budgets, pinned images, the revision) becomes data of a profile. B0 states the size of that change to the owner before it is made. New harness code beyond phase definitions and profile data goes back to the owner.
- **New phases:** `PolicyDocument`, `IntentEngine`, `IntentRaces`, `EngineParity7`, `AgentOffline7`, `DispatcherAuth7`, `AgentFaults7`, `Triggers7`, `DecisionInjection`, `PromotionApproval`, `Comparison`, `Migration7`, `MixedVersion7`, `BoundaryRehearsal`, `ApiAuth7`, `BrowserDecisions`, `BrowserDecisionsError`, `PerformanceEngine7`.
- **Phases kept from M6,** on the M7 build: `Build`, `Generate`, `Checks`, `Regression`, `ResearchRunner`, `HoldoutIsolation`, `Staging`, `ShadowSchedule`, `SourcePolicy`, `EvidenceInjection`, `AgentOffline6`, `DispatcherAuth6`, `AgentFaults6`, `ApiAuth`, `ResearchActivationRehearsal`, `BrowserResearch`, `BrowserResearchError`, `BrowserRegression`, `PerformanceApi`, `PerformanceBrowser`, `SecretScan`, `Report`.
- Isolated projects with `asl_m1_` databases, per-run secrets, their own subnets and no Internet route. Synthetic fixtures, scripted and recorded providers, the fake CLI and the mock provider only. No public endpoint and no model call. The owner's `.env` is never inherited.
- One heavy stack at a time, with the pilot watch and its stop rule, between HH:07 and HH:58 UTC (no watch for `m7-g1e`'s phases after the owner's stop of the pilot on 2026-10-18, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-pilot-stops-before-the-rest-of-the-m7-gate-which-runs-without-the-pilot-watch)). The M6-B gate runs come first on that slot; an M7 run waits for them. A skipped test fails its phase. `Clean` removes a run's resources by exact name once its evidence is recorded.
- **What M7-A is accepted on (M7-OD9):** every phase above, on one fresh run and one image.
- **Conditions of the activation, not of M7-A:**
  - `DecisionSaturation`, on the build to be activated: the engine with intake keeps its step budget, and market publication its own, while the agent worker and the research services work at their limits.
  - If M7 changed a file the research job path runs, or under M7-OD10 (b): the M6-B gate phases on the M7 build. Since 2026-10-17 they are the M7 profile's activation gate (`PerformanceResearch`, `ResearchSaturation`, `PilotCoexistence`, kept from M6 with its values), run on a run of their own (§16). By the [owner's decision of 2026-10-17](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-17--decisionsaturation-replaces-researchsaturation-in-the-m7-activation-gate) `DecisionSaturation` replaces `ResearchSaturation` in that gate, with its research, stream and fetcher load and judgments (§16, [TESTING](../TESTING.md#m7-activation-gate-decisionsaturation)).
  - The live measures of M7-B: step latency, calls per decision, calls per day.
- **Budgets declared before measuring:** the engine's existing step budget for a step with intake at fixture density; the M5 route budgets (p95 250 ms sequential, 500 ms with five readers) for the new reads on a year of rows; SSR p95 below 1,500 ms, LCP at most 2.5 s and CLS at most 0.1 for the new pages. None of the M2 to M6 budgets is relaxed.

## 12. Coexistence with the running pilot

- **Never touched before the boundary:** the pilot's containers, images, Compose blocks, M1–M4 tables, constraints, rows and grants. The deploy script compares container identities and rendered configuration before and after, as M6's does.
- **Migration `0009`** creates tables, views, functions and triggers of its own and changes agent, source and research tables only. A static test over its statements holds that none names an M1–M4 table except inside a view's or a function's body. It runs with a lock timeout and bounded retries.
- **Views over M3 and M4 tables** take no lock a writer waits for and need no grant on the tables. They are limited to the policy's run, which does not exist beside the pilot, so they return no pilot row.
- **Code identity.** M7 changes files under `app/`. No pilot worker is built from it, so the pilot gains no segment. The identity split of M5 and M6 continues until the boundary, where it ends.
- **Test runs** follow the pilot rules of M6: one heavy stack, the quiet window, the watch.

## 13. Deployment, the experiment boundary and activation

### 13.1 M7-A, inert (standing authorization for milestone deployments; pilot rules apply)
1. HH:15–HH:45 UTC, never 23:50–00:20; away from the minute of the market worker's hourly retention.
2. Read-only starting state; protected backup with its SHA-256; an isolated restore and migration, because the schema changes.
3. Rollback tags `:pre-m7` on the images that are replaced.
4. Web first, then the API (it accepts `0008` and `0009`), then migration `0009`, then `agent-worker` if it runs. The research and source images are built from the same commit; where those services run, they are recreated in the same window, because every service that takes part in a research job must have one code identity.
5. Verify read-only: every pilot container unchanged; lease epoch and experiment version unchanged; readiness; the pages without signing in; the policy "not installed"; the runtime waiting for a new profile.

**After it, before the boundary, each the owner's:**
- Compose and approve the runtime profile for generations 1 to 3 and run the capability suite. That suite holds the research probes, so it can pass only once research is activated with an approved envelope and a first sealed dataset: M7-B needs that part of M6-B in either order of M7-OD10. Under M7-OD10 (a) this is the second such run; under (b) it waits for M6-B's research activation, and the latency reading waits with it.
- Read the step latency from that run. If the policy's expiry, deviation or cadence does not fit it, those values change before the boundary, while the pilot still runs (since the owner's early stop of 2026-10-18: while its workers still run, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-pilot-stops-before-the-rest-of-the-m7-gate-which-runs-without-the-pilot-watch)). The policy is not installed yet, so this is a code change with its acceptance and a redeployment, and no further migration. After the boundary the same change is a new policy version and a rebuild of every service.
- A change to a generation-3 definition after `0009` is applied (a work budget, a prompt, the step schema) costs more: the seeded rows are append-only and the profile binds them, so it needs new definition identities, a further additive migration, a new profile approval and another capability run. The timing values most likely to move are therefore kept in the policy document; the work budgets are upper bounds.
- `DecisionSaturation` on the build to be activated (§10).

### 13.2 The boundary (each step on the owner's instruction)
0. **Before it.** The owner has stopped the pilot in the UI; the run is `STOPPED` and its final report made it `COMPLETED` (M7-OD2; the owner said at 20:16 UTC on 2026-10-18 that they were stopping it, before the rest of the gate, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-pilot-stops-before-the-rest-of-the-m7-gate-which-runs-without-the-pilot-watch)). A read-only look shows no signal without an outcome and no open latch. An open drawdown latch on the flat account would stop the new run at once; with one open the boundary waits for an owner decision that this plan does not contain.
1. `tools/pilot-deactivate.ps1`, the existing tool: backup, both workers stopped, both identities without login, both flags `0`.
2. `tools/m7-boundary.ps1`: build `execution-worker`, `evaluation-worker`, `pilot-admin` and `migrate` from the deployed M7 commit, without starting them. From here every service is one commit.
3. Install the two definitions with the guarded command (§5.1).
4. Verify read-only: images, schema, readiness, reconciliation, no position, no open order, both documents installed and unapproved.

### 13.3 M7-B activation (each step is the owner's)
1. Approve `AGENT_POLICY_V1` and `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` by exact hash on the Approvals page.
2. Create the experiment with that policy and a cost scenario; approve its manifest. It is `READY`.
3. `tools/pilot-activate.ps1 -ExperimentId <id>`, the existing tool: identities enabled, flags set, both workers started. They bind the policy at their first lease.
4. Press Start. The run is `RUNNING` and takes no decision yet.
5. Start the decision probes. When they pass, the scheduler may start decision workflows.
6. Enable the runtime, if it is not enabled.

A working login is a precondition of steps 5 and 6. The live token-refresh check of M5 is still open ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-03--m5-closed-on-the-capability-probes-live-token-refresh-becomes-a-follow-up-execplan-0020)).

### 13.4 The live walk-through
Observed by the owner in the UI, verified read-only by the implementer:
1. A trigger starts a brief; its review is recorded.
2. A decision workflow starts, by its trigger or by the owner, reads its snapshot and ends with a `PortfolioDecision`: an abstention, or an intent with the engine's answer. A brief whose review is not `ACCEPTABLE` records no trigger, so the owner's start keeps this step independent of a reviewer's verdict.
3. If a position is opened: its terms, its stop and, once it closes, its outcome record and review.

- A model that abstains is a valid outcome. Steps 1 and 2 need one success inside the daily budgets. The live draft and review are shown by the decision probes.
- That an unreviewed or out-of-domain entry has no effect is shown by the probes, not by the walk-through.

**Follow-ups after completion under M7-OD8 (a):** the first live submission with the engine's answer; the first live fill; the first outcome review; the first approved promotion used as a basis; a checkpoint date for the new run, which the owner names.

## 14. Failure and rollback

- **M7-A rollback:** the `:pre-m7` images after the guarded downgrade of `0009`, valid while no M7 row exists. The backup is never restored over the running pilot without an explicit owner decision.
- **The boundary's way back, before Start:** a new experiment of the reference on the M7 workers, which bind the reference when the open experiment names it. The pilot's old worker images cannot start a run any more: the M7 API writes its own code identity into the manifest, and a worker of another build rejects the Start. The installed definitions stay; they are inert without an approval.
- **After the first agent position exists:** fix forward only. An execution worker of an older build, or one bound to the reference, reads the mandate's stop and not the stop in force; it must never manage an agent position.
- **After M7-B:** the owner's Disable ends model work at once, cancels unfilled entries and leaves protection running. A policy `REVOKE` does the same through the engine. Stop ends the run under the M4 rules.
- **A parity failure** (R19) blocks the batch that caused it: the reference path must stay identical.
- **A schema the CLI cannot transmit** (B0), or one a real model refuses (the capability probe): the intent's choices move into separate nullable arguments. That changes generation-3 hashes. Found by the probe, after `0009` is applied, it costs new definition identities, a further additive migration, a new acceptance of the agent phases, a new profile approval and another capability run.
- **Model latency that does not fit the validity window:** the policy's values change before the boundary. No check is removed.
- **Runtime blocks** stop model work only. Nothing financial waits for a model.

## 15. Where the time goes, and what this plan does about it

- **Observed in M6:** ten batches in six days, then a final acceptance whose longest run took 11.3 hours, 9.2 of them in two measurement phases that had never passed; harness and measurement code of about 13,000 lines beside an engine change of 673 ([STATUS](../../STATUS.md)). The owner then scoped the acceptance to what the deployment switches on.
- **In this plan:**
  - the acceptance scope of M7-OD9 from the start, with load and live measures as conditions of the activation;
  - no second harness, and a harness change the owner sees before it is made;
  - B0 measures the step budget, the schema and the epoch lock before any design rests on them;
  - an authoring run and an independent review for each batch, so the final run meets no phase for the first time;
  - latency read before the boundary, so a policy value does not change after the pilot is stopped;
  - the engine and the agent batches in parallel after B2;
  - no partial reduction (M7-OD7).
- **What it cannot shorten.** Activation waits for the pilot to be stopped. Under M7-OD2 (a) that is after 2026-11-25, about seven weeks from this draft, during which M7-A stays deployed and inert. Only the owner's choice of (b) removes that wait. The owner chose (b) on 2026-10-16.
- **Not estimated.** There is no measured basis for a date or for the length of an acceptance run: eighteen of its phases have never run. It shares the one heavy slot with the M6-B gate runs.

## 17. Decision log

- 2026-10-09: "start the M7 plan" ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-09--m7-plan-requested-execplan-0026)). It approves writing this plan as a proposal and nothing else.
- 2026-10-09: "approve with your recommendations, merge it" ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-09--execplan-0026-approved-for-implementation-with-the-recommended-decisions)). The plan is approved for implementation with the eleven decisions as §3.3 records them.
- 2026-10-10: "1. decide for me 2. decide for me 3. I approve" ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-10--m7-b0-the-owner-leaves-the-latency-read-and-the-two-small-runs-to-the-implementer-and-approves-the-harness-profile)). The latency read and the two small B0 runs are decided by the implementer on the owner's delegation; the harness profile of §10 is approved at the size §18 states, with its three findings.
- Design choices of the plan, approved with it:
  1. The policy is an `execution_definitions` row approved in the M4 store, because evaluations, mandates and orders must reference their strategy there and the engine already reads that store in every step.
  2. No M1–M4 table is altered or written before the boundary, although the pilot's worker would tolerate the one row. The definitions are installed by a guarded command, not by a second migration, because every default path migrates to the newest revision.
  3. An entry is recorded as an evaluation row and decided by the existing entry phase, so the financial code has one path. The row is written only when nothing stands against the entry, and the entry is checked again until it is filled.
  4. The entry reference is the ask at intake with a deviation bound, because a decision takes minutes and the envelope is 20 basis points.
  5. An exit reuses `TREND_EXIT`, as candidates do since M6; no M3 or M4 constraint changes.
  6. A third definition generation, so generations 1 and 2 keep their hashes and their evidence.
  7. The engine holds the permission epoch with `SKIP LOCKED`, so protection never waits on an agent row and a locked row raises no error in a step.
  8. `request_review` is granted to the coordinator only, and the coordinator waits for it as a tool wait.
  9. The policy is code, like every other definition here, and not composed by the owner at run time. The cost is a rebuild for a changed value; latency is therefore read before the boundary.
  10. Two probe suites, because a decision probe needs an approved policy and a running run, and the capability suite must be able to pass before the boundary.

- **Decided while building B2 (2026-10-11), within the approved plan:**
  1. *The binding also follows live positions.* §5.4 binds to the open experiment's strategy. Without one, a position whose mandate names a policy now binds that policy, and a policy engine with nothing of its own gives way to the reference. Otherwise a policy position left by a run that failed would be managed by the reference, which would evaluate trend exits for it.
  2. *No draft beside another strategy's live position* when either strategy is a policy (`OTHER_STRATEGY_POSITIONS_OPEN`). One engine runs a mode, and the reference's positions take their trend exits from its bar evaluations, which the policy engine does not make. The reference's own drafts are unchanged.
  3. *The install takes both documents or neither, and only once they are complete.* While the six generation-3 hashes are empty (until B5) the command refuses the code's rows, so B5 may still change the policy's hash under version 1: no build before it can install that version.
  4. *Migration `0009` is not changed for B2.* Policies are told apart by `policy_kind` and by the build's own list, not by an ID prefix in the database.
  5. *Start refuses a policy run whose protocol or baseline slot is not the policy's,* beside the existing slot checks.
  6. *R19 is measured as statement text.* EngineParity's fingerprints cannot see an extra read, so `EngineParity7` also compares the sequence of statements per step with values measured on B1's build. The fast path's statements depend on how steps fall into time batches and are not compared.
  7. *A run's slot binds only the reference or an installed policy* (from the review). A slot that names anything else binds nothing, so the engine refuses that run's start with `CODE_DEFINITION_MISMATCH` as M4's did, instead of leaving the mode without an engine.
  8. *The start checks again what the draft checked* (from the review): `OTHER_STRATEGY_POSITIONS_OPEN`, and a fixture-only policy for the main account (`FIXTURE_STRATEGY_REFUSED_FOR_MAIN`; the draft answers `FIXTURE_STRATEGY_REFUSED`). B6's solo policy is the first such policy.
  9. *A rule field restates V1's rule* (from the review). The document's 29 fields that state a rule the code implements must equal V1's text, the ATR seed for its own period; a version with another rule comes with its code. `action_limits.protective_exits` now says what §5.2 and R09 say: never blocked by a limit, their filled notional counted.
  10. *Left to later batches:* the policy engine's intake markers (B3); the binding of a policy replay that has no run (B3 and B6: today a policy engine with no run and no position gives way to the reference, so a replay needs the exemption a candidate's replay has); the manifest's recorded agent context (B5); the owner's route for a policy draft and the "not installed" view of the documents (B7).

- 2026-10-11: "do whatever is needed" and, on the incidents read, "B" ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-11--m7-b2-merged-on-the-owners-word-b0s-pc-steps-run-on-the-pc-b3-keeps-the-epoch-hold)). B2's pull request was merged and B0's PC steps ran on the owner's PC; the epoch hold stays, on the condition the implementer set when the owner delegated the call.

- **Decided while building B3 (2026-10-11), within the approved plan:**
  1. *An intent's choices are one object with exactly the action's keys.* §5.3 lets a model choose from the policy's sets and nothing else. The intent's document carries them as `choices`: an entry chooses its expiry, exposure fraction, stop multiple and maximum hold, an exit or a cancellation its expiry. A missing or extra key, a value outside its set (a number where the policy writes text, or the reverse) or an intent that expires later than its chosen expiry after the draft is `CHOICE_INVALID`.
  2. *A waiting entry writes nothing.* When only waiting reasons (the watermark, a bar not yet visible, stale data, an ineligible instrument, an invalid valuation, a missing daily reference, a passive breach) stand against an entry, the step writes nothing for it and the next step takes it again, until its own expiry answers `INTENT_EXPIRED`. Every answer is then final and written once.
  3. *A missing quote at the cutoff is final* (`CUTOFF_QUOTE_MISSING`): the deviation bound needs it, and a later step would read the same quote.
  4. *The entry reference is the ask of the step that accepts the entry* (design choice 4), and its deviation from the ask at the workflow's cutoff is checked there and again before each fill.
  5. *Sizing takes two more upper bounds,* the requested exposure and the notional left in the window, beside the risk policy's caps, each checked again with the rounded quantity. The reference's sizing is unchanged.
  6. *How the limits count,* as [RISK_POLICY](../contracts/RISK_POLICY.md#agent-driven-action-enforcement) now states: an accepted entry counts even when its order is later canceled, and entries accepted earlier in the same step count; a submission counts whatever its action and answer, though only an entry is refused for their number (an exit or a cancellation lowers exposure); the committed notional is an entry's reservation while it is open and its fills once it is final, plus exit fills, protective ones too. Re-entry from the second bar after the bar a position closed in, the sixth after a protective stop (a suppressed one too); after an accepted cancellation, not on its bar or the next, where its bar is the newest that had closed when the engine answered it, or the intent's bar if that is later (from the review: the intent's bar alone let a cancellation that names an old bar end the cooldown at once).
  7. *The owner's pause and stop refuse entries, not exits.* An entry on a paused run is `POLICY_PAUSED`, on a stopping one `RUN_STOPPING`; an exit is taken in both, as protection is.
  8. *An agent's pause of a run that is not live opens with no effect* (`effect: NONE` in its answer), so it cancels nothing; the owner's resume closes it as any other.
  9. *The poll reads three intake markers, not four.* B2 named the epoch among them. An epoch change matters only to an unanswered request, which already makes every step run, or to an open entry, whose order or waiting signal already does, so the poll does not read the epoch.
  10. *Stop tightening answers `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED` until B4.*
  11. *A request left unanswered when the mode's binding returns to the reference stays unanswered.* The reference engine reads no request (R19); the intent's expiry passes.
  12. *The owner's Disable is retried.* Under the hold it waits for an engine step too; it is tried three times on a lock or statement timeout and then answered `503 TRY_AGAIN` with nothing recorded.
  13. *A replay writes the intent stream back on a disposable database only,* with the guards off for that transaction, as the engine's inputs are written for any replay there. The stream's rows and epoch changes must be on one clock: in a forward run both are the database's (the guards set a request's time); a fixture writes both on its simulated clock.
  14. *Bars are read inside the step, and only for an entry being taken.* §5.4 has them read before the transaction for a symbol with a waiting entry. An entry is taken a few times a day at most, so the read is one indexed query in that step, and inside the transaction it sees exactly the step's watermark.
  15. *An expired entry keeps what it waited on* (from the review, R06): its answer's `detail.waiting` lists the waiting reasons the answering step found.
  16. *Behind the cutoff the engine waits for it* (from the review): the quote at the cutoff is read only once the step's watermark has reached it, so a quote the engine has not handled can neither reject an entry for its deviation nor count as missing.
  17. *A bar past its signal's validity is superseded by time* (from the review): when the next bar is late, an entry for a bar whose signal could no longer be decided is answered `BAR_SUPERSEDED` at once, rather than written and missed.

- **Decided while building B4 (2026-10-11), within the approved plan:**
  1. *A tightening chooses its template, never a level.* Its `choices` are `expiry_minutes` and `tightening`, one of the policy's `stop_tightening` (`BREAKEVEN`, `STOP_TEMPLATE`); a stop level, or any other key, is `CHOICE_INVALID`. The "expected mandate version" of [AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS](../AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS.md) is not built: the engine computes the level from the template at its own step and refuses one that is not above the stop in force, so a draft that saw an older stop cannot loosen it.
  2. *The two templates.* `BREAKEVEN` is the position's cost basis over its quantity, rounded up to eight decimals so the stop is never below the cost; the answer records both inputs. `STOP_TEMPLATE` is the latest final bar's close less the position's own stop multiple times the ATR of the contiguous run ending at that bar, the entry stop's rule, in the engine's decimal context and unrounded, as the entry's stop is. On the entry's own bar it gives the entry's stop when the entry was taken at the close, so it is then `STOP_NOT_HIGHER`.
  3. *The newest final bar the step sees, not the intent's decision bar.* An exit or a cancellation is not checked against its bar either; the bar used is in the answer's `detail`.
  4. *A tightening waits for a usable quote and, for `STOP_TEMPLATE`, a resolved final bar,* and writes nothing meanwhile; its expiry records what it waited for in `detail.waiting` (`DATA_STALE`, `BAR_NOT_VISIBLE`). No visible final bar is a wait, not `STOP_INVALID` (from the review).
  5. *The refusals, in order:* no such live position `POSITION_NOT_OPEN`; another run or symbol `POSITION_MISMATCH`; terms without tightening or without the chosen template `TERMS_FORBID_TIGHTENING`; a `CLOSING` position or an exit obligation `POSITION_CLOSING`; no first fill `POSITION_NOT_OPEN` (an unfilled entry's position is `PENDING_ENTRY`, as for an exit); no ATR or a stop not above zero `STOP_INVALID`; then `STOP_NOT_HIGHER` and `STOP_ALREADY_CROSSED`, both when both hold. What the database guard checks the engine checks first, so a refused tightening is an answer, never an error in the step. The bid is compared at the scale the update row stores it (`quote_bid numeric(38,8)`, rounded half up), and the row carries the submission's run, which the guard compares with the terms' (both from the review).
  6. *"Later than the update" is later than its decision time,* `decision_stop_updates.at`, the step that wrote it. The update was checked against the bid of a quote of an earlier source time, so a quote between that quote and the step, received late, is held against the stop before the update.
  7. *Where the stop in force is read.* The policy engine reads the updates of its live positions in one more statement in its load and one in its summary; protection, the portfolio of the breach check and of sizing (planned risk), and the poll's check of a quote that is stale when handled use the stop in force at the quote's source time or the latest one. The reference engine reads its mandates' stops as before and issues no new statement (R19); a policy position's protective evidence adds `mandate_stop` when the stop that crossed is an update's.
  8. *The API's risk view* (`GET /v1/risk`) computes open risk against the stop in force. Showing `stop_in_force` beside `stop_price` in the position views changes the OpenAPI schema, so it is B7's, where §9 puts the owner API. The M4 harness's run summary (`app/experiments/acceptance.py`) keeps the mandate's stop: it summarizes the reference's runs and runs against databases at `0008` too.
  9. *§2.8's Changed 3 does not arise.* A buy's fill reads `orders.stop_price`, but no buy is open once a position has a first fill: an entry order is immediate-or-cancel, its remainder canceled in the step of a partial fill (`app/execution/engine.py:1449`), and `entries` makes no further attempt once a fill exists (`engine.py:2151`); the database guard requires the first fill. So a tightening never meets an open buy.
  10. *The trade list's exit label.* A closed position's exit is still the origin of its first sell, except that `TREND_EXIT` on a position whose mandate's exit rule is `AGENT_DISCRETIONARY_EXIT_V1` is `AGENT_EXIT`. A protective stop or a time exit keeps its label, and the reference's and candidates' labels do not change, so their reports are unchanged. The evaluation identity reads the rule from `position_mandates`, which it already reads; it has no grant on the decision tables.
