  11. *The decision fingerprint adds `decision_stop_updates` and `decision_events.stop_update_id`.* A decision fingerprint of B3's build is therefore not comparable with B4's; the reference's fingerprints do not change.
  12. *A tightening does not change the state key.* §5.3's key is the run's positions with their versions, its open orders, its pauses and the latches; an update changes no position's version, so a review that saw another stop is not stale for that alone.
  13. *`ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED` stays* for an action the database admits and a build does not take; since B4 there is none.

- **Decided while building B5 (2026-10-11), within the approved plan:**
  1. *An intent's fields are named as the engine reads them.* The model's intent holds the action, the symbol, a position for an exit, a cancellation or a tightening, and the policy's choices under the engine's names (`expiry_minutes`, `exposure_fraction`, `stop_multiple`, `max_hold_hours`, `tightening`; `app.policy.document.CHOICE_KEYS`, which the engine and the tool import). B0's preview named them `*_choice` and had the model echo the policy, its hash, the snapshot's hash and the bar; the server writes those instead, with the expiry instant (database time plus the chosen minutes) and the manifest's hash. The manifest's hash binds the cost scenario, so the draft has no cost-scenario field. The actual schema is therefore not B0's preview; it is measured and probed again (decision 22).
  2. *Budgets are per template* (`BUDGETS_V3`, one entry per template, each with its own wait deadline), with a daily cap per purpose: 36 decisions, 4 briefs and 8 outcome reviews a UTC day, counted apart from generations 1 and 2. Each of a decision's waits has its own bound (a review: its queue and running time plus 60 s; an engine answer: the draft's expiry, 30 minutes at most, plus 60 s) and is capped at 5,040 s, which is what two of each add up to at most; no check adds them up (from the review: the first text said one deadline bounded the sum).
  3. *The decision suite's call budget is 40, not 25.* Scripted, CP-23 to CP-27 with the chain's brief and both reviews take 26 calls (`OBSERVED` in the offline suite), so 25 fails CP-27 for its budget alone. A real model may take more; M7-B measures it.
  4. *Every decision that is not a probe needs a `VERIFIED` decision suite* for its profile and policy, `OFFLINE_TEST` ones included, so the offline tests run the suite's path too. The start refuses, in this order: the run not live (`POLICY_RUN_NOT_LIVE`), an unknown policy, a policy whose definitions are not the build's, the run not running, an unapproved policy, then `DECISION_SUITE_NOT_VERIFIED`.
  5. *What a decision is shown.* The snapshot is the policy's own run as stored at the start, labeled as such, with its action limits used and its recent intents and outcomes, and without risk headroom (`RISK_HEADROOM_NOT_SHOWN`: the engine computes it in its step). Its recent intents are the run's decisions', not the decision suite's, which runs on the same run. When it is above `snapshot_max_bytes` (8,192), older history is left out and labeled, never the state (account, positions, open orders, pauses, blockers); a snapshot that still does not fit ends the start (`SNAPSHOT_TOO_LARGE`). The scope is the newest reviewed brief of each symbol within the policy's `assessment_max_age_hours` and its own expiry (a brief is of the market, not of a run), and the four newest outcome reviews of the run's own positions; a probe sees its own probe run's briefs only (the last three points from the review).
  6. *An entry cites a reviewed brief, an `ACCEPTABLE` one, and its own `query_market_features` result at the decision bar* (the policy's `entry_evidence`). A `CANDIDATE` basis is `EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE` until B6 builds promotion approval.
  7. *A review that is not `ACCEPTABLE`, of another hash or past its validity is `REVIEW_STALE`* at submission; a missing one is `REVIEW_REQUIRED`. Exits, cancellations and tightenings need no review.
  8. *Withdrawals happen where a workflow ends:* in the orchestrator's terminal path, for every way it ends but `SUCCEEDED` (owner cancel, revocation, failure, expiry), and in the capability latch's path; a newer bar ends an unsubmitted decision as `EXPIRED` with `BAR_SUPERSEDED`. What is withdrawn is what the engine has not finished with, as §4.1 says: a submission it has not answered, and an entry it accepted that has no fill, whose order or waiting signal the engine then cancels (`WITHDRAWN`, B3's check until the fill). A refusal, an exit, a cancellation or a tightening it answered, and an entry with a fill, are final. The first version withdrew only unanswered submissions; the review found that it contradicted §4.1.
  9. *Triggers.* `BAR_CLOSE` is written once per bar from the earliest newest bar across the universe; `POSITION_EVENT` from the run's own fills and closes (`ENTRY_FILL`, `POSITION_END`), looking back 24 hours (older events were missed while no worker looked; nothing is written for them). A waiting trigger is stale after its family's staleness from its event time (the policy's cadence; 20 minutes for a bar); of several waiting triggers of the run, the one of the newest event is kept (by event time, not by when it was written) and stands for the others, which end `REFUSED` as `SUPERSEDED`, naming it, only when its decision starts; until then all wait, so a position event among them keeps the start exempt from the cooldown (both from the review). One open decision at a time, the policy's start cooldown except for position events, and the daily cap hold or refuse them; a decision is queued until its trigger's event time plus its staleness.
  10. *Briefs* are queued by the scheduler at the policy's brief hours (0, 6, 12 and 18 UTC), each only while its hour lasts, within the daily cap. *Outcome reviews* are queued by `SYSTEM` for each outcome record of the last day without one, at most four a pass.
  11. *Priority:* probes, then the owner's and fixture work, then decisions, promotions, briefs with investigations and assessments, then the rest. M6's order is kept within it.
  12. *The outcome record* is built from financial rows only (the intent and its review, the risk decision, each order and fill, fees and modeled costs, the realized result, how and when it ended), stored once with its hash. It does not yet name data gaps or downtime during the position; B9's report does.
  13. *Profile v3* binds every generation's budgets by key, states a data scope per generation, and its consequences text names the decision authority (paper only, within the policy's sets, no approval of anything). A profile of v2 no longer matches.
  14. *The policy's binding hash is the build's own:* `AGENT_POLICY_V1` takes its six generation-3 hashes from `definitions_v3.policy_binding()`; its hash is `2da61a78…`. B2's install refusal of a document with unset hashes stays, tested on that document as it was before B5.
  15. *Two probe suites.* The capability suite gains CP-21 (a decision without a policy or a run, on an empty snapshot) and CP-22 (a brief), judged from recorded rows. The owner's `DECISION_PROBE` request starts the decision suite only in `PROBE_ONLY` or `ENABLED`, with an approved profile, no latch, no other probe run and a ready policy; it runs CP-23 to CP-26 (a number where a choice belongs, a price and quantity asked for, a missing review, a stale review) and CP-27, the chain: a brief and its review, a draft, its review and a decision. Only CP-27's brief gets a review child. CP-23 to CP-26 pass on their technical conditions and record whether they were exercised; CP-27 must complete its chain. A probe's submission or pause is refused last (`PROBE_REFUSED`). A suite whose runtime leaves the state it needs is canceled and its verification `FAILED`. No new runtime event kind.
  16. *The owner's routes* to start the generation-3 templates and the decision probe are B7's; the request kind and `controls.decision_probe` exist now.
  17. *A review's target is named in its document,* not in an artifact column: intent and outcome review artifacts leave the M6 target column null.
  18. *R18 in B5 is the agent's half:* evidence, brief, review, decision, reviewed intent and submission, with the engine's answer written as the engine writes it. The full loop through the fill and the ledger is B6's `PromotionApproval` fixture and B9's.
  19. *The runtime view keeps M6's contract;* budgets v3 join it with the owner API in B7.
  20. *Context key.* A submission records the decision's context key, so the report can segment by it (§5.11).
  21. *The recorded provider does not enforce the output schema's enums,* so a symbol outside the universe is tested at the handler (`SCOPE_DENIED`), not as a refused output.
  22. *The actual schema v3 is probed again.* `AgentOffline7` builds it in the engine image, compares it with B0's preview and sends two SYNTHETIC outputs of its shape through the pinned CLI and the mock provider with B0's probe unchanged ([INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE](../contracts/INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE.md#m7-b5-agentstep-schema-v3-as-built--2026-10-11-execplan-0026)). This is the case B0's preview anticipated, by its phase definitions and two small scripts in `tools/m7/b5`; no harness code changed beyond them.
  23. *The M7 test fixture also clears generation 2's trigger and completion tables,* which M5's reset leaves, so a test's triggers never reach the next.
  24. *`CHOICE_KEYS` moved to `app/policy/document.py`* so the tool and the engine import one list; the engine's behavior is unchanged.
- **Decided while building B6a (2026-10-12), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B6 is two pull requests.* B6a holds promotion approval and the candidate basis with `PromotionApproval`; B6b the comparison document, `AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO` and the control replay with `Comparison`. They share no code, and one diff of both would be the largest of M7 for a review to read.
  2. *Who asks for an entry basis.* Once `AGENT_POLICY_V1` is installed, SYSTEM starts `PROMOTION_V2` where it started `PROMOTION_V1` before, for a candidate that is `EVIDENCE_READY` or is `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING` on a pending `PROMOTION_V1` alone, and for none whose exit values lie outside the policy's sets. Recording the new request withdraws the pending consideration request in the same transaction, because a request for consideration can only be rejected and would otherwise wait beside it; not one the owner has already rejected, whose rejection SYSTEM applies first (decision 19). The candidate is then already `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING`, so no event is written and that event names the older request; `MAIN_APPROVED` names the new one. The owner's start is B7's (decision 15).
  3. *The request's scope is the server's.* The `PROMOTION_V2` document (schema 2) carries `ENTRY_BASIS_UNDER_AGENT_POLICY_V1`, the policy's ID and hash, the candidate's exit values and the requests it supersedes; the model's `requested_scope` is overwritten. The job context shows the exit values beside the policy's sets.
  4. *"The policy's sets" are checked at each step by value.* The ATR period equals the policy's, the stop multiple is a member as a number (`2` is `2.0`) and the hold is a member. The request's finish refuses otherwise (`SCOPE_DENIED`), the owner's approval (`CANDIDATE_OUTSIDE_POLICY`, and `0009`'s guard), and the engine (`CANDIDATE_BASIS_UNAVAILABLE`). A draft writes the stop multiple as the policy's text.
  5. *Approval, rejection and revocation.* `APPROVE` once, while the request is pending and its candidate `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING`; `REVOKE` only after it, while the request is `PENDING` or `APPROVED`, raising the permission epoch when it is recorded; `REJECT` as in M6. The worker applies the latest record of each request: `APPROVED` and `MAIN_APPROVED`; `REJECTED`; `REVOKED` and `SUSPENDED`. An approval whose candidate left `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING` before it was applied (a research suspension) is `WITHDRAWN` and grants nothing. A suspended candidate leads only to `RETIRED` or `REJECTED`, so a revoked approval is not given again; a new approval needs a new candidate.
  6. *Three M7 functions take over three triggers.* `0009` points the triggers of `research_promotions`, `research_candidate_events` and `agent_approvals` at new functions that keep every rule of 0008's and add B6's, rather than replacing 0008's functions, so its downgrade restores them exactly and refuses while any B6 state exists.
  7. *What a decision is shown.* At most four approved candidates (`MAIN_APPROVED` on the owner's latest record of an `APPROVED` request for this policy and hash), oldest first, each with its binding, stop multiple, hold, symbols in the policy's universe, and its signal per symbol at the decision bar, computed with the M6 interpreter over the contiguous run of the `agent_read` candles that ends there and labeled as computed at the cutoff. The size bound never shortens them. A probe sees none.
  8. *An entry on a candidate takes its stop multiple and hold* and one of its symbols; it still needs its `ACCEPTABLE` intent review, the unchanged state key and its own `query_market_features` result at the decision bar, since the policy's `entry_evidence` rule names every entry. The draft's `basis` names the candidate, its normalized hash, the promotion and the approval.
  9. *The submission binds the approval as the guard checks it:* the candidate's latest event `MAIN_APPROVED` citing that approval, the promotion `APPROVED` for the draft's policy and hash, that approval still the owner's latest record, and the stored hash and declaration. A revocation recorded but not yet applied therefore already refuses a submission; the risen epoch ends the workflow at the worker's next pass.
  10. *The engine reads no research table.* It parses the submission's declaration with the M6 grammar, checks the ATR period, stop multiple, hold and symbol, and after the waits, the checks and the limits evaluates the entry condition on the contiguous run that ends at the decision bar, the run its ATR uses. The signal row records the result, the input digest and the hash of the execution definition the interpreter ran; the submission keeps the normalized hash. A run shorter than the candidate's warmup gives `WARMUP_INCOMPLETE`, so `CANDIDATE_SIGNAL_ABSENT`.
  11. *The prompts are edited in place.* `coordinator.v2.md` and `evaluator.v3.md` gain the candidate basis, and `coordinator-promotion.v1.md` is new; none has been sent to a model or installed, so their rows in `0009` changed with them, as B5's review changed one.
  12. *The policy's hash is now `cf251933…`* (`cf2519333007ad4bc37cee250a0eab0be178b9e0cfd5eac163a66899ec20fcb6`), because the catalog, grants, templates, budgets and prompts it binds changed: `7cb969a3…` with B6a's first draft, then this after its review changed two prompts (decision 17). B5's `2da61a78…` and the draft's were never installed anywhere.
  13. *Promotion work counts with the daily parents of generations 1 and 2* (24 a day), as `PROMOTION_V1` always has; generation 3's per-purpose caps do not name `PROMOTION`. Its budget is generation 2's for a parent: 12 steps, 10 tool calls, 1,200 s.
  14. *The replay's intent stream carries what the terms reference.* `decision_position_terms` has foreign keys to the candidate and the approval, so a replay on a fresh store needs both rows. The stream records each before the first submission naming it; the writer inserts it if absent and stops the replay if a stored row differs (`intent_stream_reference_differs`).
  15. *The owner's routes stay B7's.* `/v1/agent-approvals` already takes `APPROVE` and `REVOKE` in its body, and B6's rules apply to them. Starting `PROMOTION_V2` (and `MARKET_BRIEF_V1` and `PORTFOLIO_DECISION_V1`) needs B7's route and the database's owner pairs: §18's B1 list gave the pairs to B5, which did not add them, and `0009` still holds 0008's four. `/v1/promotions` admits only `PENDING`, `REJECTED` and `WITHDRAWN` and a `REJECT` decision, so it would fail on an approved or revoked request; none can exist before the boundary installs the policy, and B7 must widen it before B10.
  16. *Tests changed with the behavior they describe.* `test_m7_schema.py`'s B1 test asserted that a candidate basis is refused until the owner can approve a promotion; it now asserts B6's two refusals (no approval named, an approval that is not the candidate's current one), and the approved path is `test_m7_promotions.py`'s. Four kept suites count `PROMOTION_V2`, its prompt and the policy's hash; `test_m7_intents.py`'s desk can carry a candidate.
  17. *"Its entry condition holds" is read as the candidate's own entry signal* (B6a's review). The engine takes an entry on a candidate only when the M6 interpreter's result on the decision bar is `ENTRY_SIGNAL`: its entry condition holds and its exit signal does not. A bar on which both hold is `TREND_EXIT_SIGNAL` and refused (`CANDIDATE_SIGNAL_ABSENT`), as is a warmup the exit signal's features have not completed: research evaluated the candidate that way, and an entry on a bar where the candidate itself would exit is not what its evidence describes. Its exit rule (stop, hold, exit signal) is still not run for the position. The two coordinator prompts say so, so their rows in `0009` and the policy's hash changed again (decision 12).
  18. *A research suspension reaches a submitted entry through a withdrawal* (B6a's review, its major point). A shadow's integrity failure suspends the candidate as `asl_research` and raises no epoch, and the engine reads no research table. Each pass of the agent worker therefore withdraws every entry on a candidate whose latest event is no longer `MAIN_APPROVED` on the submission's approval, if the engine has not answered it or has accepted it without a fill, whatever its decision's state, with the new reason `UNAPPROVED`; the withdrawal guard admits that reason only in that case. The engine cancels it as it cancels any withdrawn entry. Raising the epoch from research instead would need a research identity to change an agent table and would end every agent's work for one candidate. What remains is the time until the worker's next pass, and none is written while the agent worker is down (§19).
  19. *An owner's rejection is never withdrawn over* (B6a's review). An entry-basis request is not recorded over a pending consideration request the owner has decided, by the agent's check and the guard, and `PENDING` -> `WITHDRAWN` is refused once the owner's latest record is a `REJECT`. The approval guard takes the candidate's lock before it reads the request's state, so a rejection that waited for SYSTEM's supersession finds the request withdrawn and is refused, rather than being recorded on a request no pass would apply.
  20. *Two smaller points of the same review.* A `REVOKE` of an approval withdrawn unapplied is refused (`NOT_APPROVED`, and the guard): it granted nothing, and the epoch it raised would only end every agent's work. SYSTEM's start of `PROMOTION_V2` reads every waiting candidate until four are eligible, not a window of fifty, so candidates outside the policy's sets, which wait without a request for ever, cannot hide a later one.

- **Decided while building B6b (2026-10-12), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B6b is split again: the control replay is B6c* (amends B6a decision 1). B6b holds segments, the decision measures, `AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO` and the comparison with its fixture, and adds the phase `Comparison`; the control replay (§5.11's forward control, M7-OD5 (a)) is B6c, its own pull request, which adds its tests to the same phase. The control replay is research-runner code with its own budget and labels, which nothing of B6b touches, and one diff of both would again have been the largest of M7.
  2. *A segment starts when the engine answers.* Every answer to a submission goes through one function, which writes `SEGMENT_STARTED` (SYSTEM, reason `CONTEXT_KEY_CHANGED`, the new and the previous key, the submission and the run's code identity) when the submission belongs to the live run and its context key (B5's, over the profile, the approved source versions, the research envelope and the approved candidates) differs from that of the run's submission before it in submission order, answered or not, which is the protocol's "the last" (B6b's review: an entry that waits is answered after later submissions, so comparing with the submission answered last made two spurious segments). The run's first submission starts none: activation began the first segment. A refused submission starts one too, since the context changed whatever the answer. The event is written in the step's transaction with the answer, and the report's existing segment list reads it, so no report code changed for it. A code identity change still starts a segment as in M4.
  3. *The evaluation identity reads decision records through views.* `0009` adds the schema `evaluation_read` with eleven `security_barrier` views of the decision and agent tables, owned by the migration identity like `agent_read`'s, whose `SELECT` only `asl_evaluation` holds; it holds no grant on any agent or decision table. They carry codes, states, counts and times, never a document's text: a review's type, verdict and count of unsupported claims; a decision's action and reason code per symbol, which the step schema holds to enums; a tool call's name, which is the model's request and is shown as none when the dispatcher refused it as `UNKNOWN_TOOL` (B6b's review). A run's workflows are its decisions (named by their trigger's run or their snapshot), their children and the reviews of its positions' outcomes. Probes and market briefs belong to no run: a brief serves every running policy, and counting it for one would be arbitrary.
  4. *The measures are a function of those rows at the report's as-of time.* The nine measures of `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` appear in a report generated under V2 only, as `decision_measures`, with one more limitation sentence; a V1 report keeps its bytes. A workflow's state counts once its finish is within the report, else `OPEN`. A run whose intents are a scripted stream has no workflows, so the values only agent workflows record (verdicts, steps, tool calls, trigger times, login and quota failures, incomplete workflows) are `NOT_RECORDED`, never zero. A value that is not a code, and a tool name outside the catalog, is counted as `UNRECOGNIZED`, so no text a model chose reaches a report; an engine code qualified by a reason (`INSTRUMENT_INELIGIBLE:<reason>`) counts by its code.
  5. *`AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO` restates three rule words of its own* (amends B2 decision 9 for this one document). It has V1's values, its own ID and hash (`cff95a27…`), status `FIXTURE_ONLY`, and its own `entry_evidence.assessment`, `entry_evidence.enforced` and `review.entry`, which say that an entry has no brief and no review; every other rule field is V1's. The policy check takes its rule words from it and accepts it only as `FIXTURE_ONLY`. It is in `POLICIES` and not in `M7_INSTALLED`, so the boundary never installs it, and the draft and Start refuse it for the main account (B2 decision 8). No agent workflow runs it: the dispatcher and the guards still ask every entry for its review, so in M7 it acts only through scripted intent streams on a disposable database. V1's hash is unchanged.
  6. *A policy's replay keeps its binding* (closes B2 decision 10). An engine that is not the main account's keeps the policy it is bound to while no run or live position of another strategy is in its mode, as a candidate's replay does. A live position of the reference counts too (B6b's review): it names no policy, so it is not among the policies the binding reads, and a policy engine would leave its trend exit unevaluated. Without it, a fixture run's engine gave way to the reference after its run stopped, and the replay driver's batched path rolled the whole batch back. The main account's engine still gives way.
  7. *The comparison is a document, not a row.* A pure function of two `FINAL` reports and the protocols they were generated under. It refuses, naming every reason (seventeen codes), unless both runs are distinct fixture runs (`OFFLINE_TEST`; a research replay names no fixture, and the labels say synthetic fixture) of the variants their replay blocks name, on one fixture, grid, start, cost scenario, baseline set, contribution, uncertainty rule and sample minimum, and each report matches its hash and protocol. The fixture's hash is what the report's generator was given, not one it computed from stored rows. The paired values are first-minus-second hourly log returns of run-relative NAV wherever both runs have consecutive valid points; the protocol's stationary bootstrap runs at its block of 24 hours and the sensitivity blocks of 6 and 72, each seeded from both manifests, both protocols and the block. Each run's costs, gaps, incidents, latches, segments, interventions and decision measures are carried from its report. There is no threshold, and the document says that scripted decisions show the comparison works, not that a role helps. Nothing stores or serves it in M7: the phase writes the three documents' hashes and statistics to its evidence file.
  8. *The fixture is synthetic and its decisions are scripted.* One labeled market (two symbols, 130 warmup and 176 run hours, a linear leg with a 12-hour triangle wave, quotes five times an hour, `FIXTURE_COST_F1`), three `OFFLINE_TEST` runs on a reset database, on the replay driver's fast path, each Start and Stop a timed owner control. The reference is `TREND_REFERENCE_V1` under V1. `AGENT_POLICY_V1` follows written rules for its brief (the last 00, 06, 12 or 18 o'clock bar above its EMA50), its coordinator (an EMA20 cross up enters, a close below it leaves) and its review (a veto when the close is below the one 24 hours earlier), and `AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO` the coordinator's rule alone; both under V2. Intents and submissions are written as the replay's intent stream writes them, with deterministic IDs, and the engine answers them as it answers any. The scripts stand in for the roles; they are not models.
  9. *A report's hashes depend on the code's identity.* The manifest names the code identity, one hash over the `app/` tree, and the experiment's ID and the bootstrap's seeds follow from it, so the reports' and comparisons' hashes change whenever `app/` changes, while their economic results do not. On one `app/` tree they repeat: a rerun of one variant gives the same report (the test checks it), and two phase runs here, on commits that changed only tests, wrote byte-identical evidence files. The identity normalizes line endings, so a run of the same tree on the owner's PC should give the same hashes; that is inferred, not observed.
  10. *`Migration7`'s helpers are repaired in B6b.* Running the phase's steps outside Docker (§18, B6b) found that they had not followed two earlier batches, whose own phases had not run on the owner's PC: since B5, `tests/m6_migration.py` expected generation 3's seed rows at `0008` (so its catalog step would fail there, in `Migration7` and, by inference, in the kept `Migration` phase on the M7 build); since B6a, `tests/m7_migration.py` did not know the three M6 triggers that now run M7 functions, their three new functions or the promotion states `APPROVED` and `REVOKED`. Each helper now expects exactly what `0009` declares (`REPLACED`, `M6_PROMOTION_STATES`, `M7_PROMOTION_STATES`, `EVALUATION_VIEWS`), and the snapshot covers `evaluation_read` (B6b's review). These are test helpers, not harness code.

- **Decided while building B6c (2026-10-12), within the approved plan:**
  1. *The run's window is replayed in periods of 21 days.* A shadow-shaped dataset holds at most the envelope's `shadows.max_days` (21), and a store is kept current only long enough for a window of that length to finish on it, so one replay cannot cover a run of unknown length. Period *n* of a run is frozen at its activation plus *n* × 21 days and ends 21 days later, or at the run's end if that comes first. Each period starts flat with 10000 of virtual funding, as a shadow does, and says so (`EACH_PERIOD_STARTS_FLAT`): a position of the reference open at a period's end does not carry into the next. A period starts only once the period before it has ended, whatever its end, and its own freeze has passed; a run that ended at or before a freeze gets no period there. A main account has one open forward run at a time, so at most one period is open per run and very few at all.
  2. *Which runs count, and how research learns of them.* Only the main account's runs count: mode `FORWARD_PAPER`, a strategy that is an installed `AGENT_DRIVEN` policy, and an `ACTIVATED` event. `asl_research` reads them through one view, `research_read.agent_runs` (security barrier, owned by the migration identity, `SELECT` for `asl_research` only): run, state, policy and its hash, the first `ACTIVATED` time and the first `STOPPED` or `FAILED` time as the run's end (`COMPLETED` follows `STOPPED`). It reads nothing else of an M1–M4 table. The pilot's runs, fixture runs and research replays are never controlled.
  3. *Two tables, guarded.* `research_controls` holds one row per period: its run, policy, activation, the reference's ID and hash, the envelope's hash, freeze, end, the run's end once recorded, funding, state (`QUEUED`, `RUNNING`, `SUCCEEDED`, `INCOMPLETE`, `SUSPENDED`), version and detail. `research_control_marks` holds immutable marks. `asl_research` inserts both and updates six columns of a period; `asl_m7_control_guard()` refuses a period whose run, policy or activation is not the view's, one that starts before its freeze or after its run ended, one that does not follow its ended predecessor, a change of anything but state, version, watermark, prefix fingerprint, run end and detail, a version that does not rise by one, a transition other than `QUEUED`→`RUNNING`→`SUCCEEDED` or an open period's end as `INCOMPLETE` or `SUSPENDED`, a falling watermark, and a run's end recorded twice or other than the view's. Only the research identity moves a period (the migration identity only a fixture row).
  4. *An advance is research work, not a trial.* It is a `research_jobs` row of the new kind `CONTROL`, whose hypothesis and candidate are both empty (`0009` drops their `NOT NULL` and adds a check: both empty for `CONTROL`, both set otherwise), and its result names no candidate. It needs its open period, takes no owner cancel request, writes no `research_trials` row and, as a shadow's advance (owner decision of 2026-10-04), spends neither the trial day nor the queue of four. Its input is `control_identity`: the reference alone over the window, every recorded mark a checkpoint, and a reference hash other than this code's refused. The runner replays it with a shadow's reference cell, and its result carries a shadow's labels, `CONTROL_LABELS` and `provenance.control`.
  5. *How a period ends.* `SUCCEEDED` when a final mark covers its window (`WINDOW_END`) or reaches the run's end (`RUN_ENDED`); `SUSPENDED` (`PREFIX_MISMATCH`) when a recorded mark no longer reproduces, or the catalog's check of a result's marks fails (`CATALOG_MARKS`); `INCOMPLETE` when its store is lost or at its cap before the window is staged, when main no longer kept rows after its freeze (`RETENTION_LOSS`), or when the run's stop committed after the period had started on a view that did not show it yet (`RUN_ENDED_BEFORE_ITS_FREEZE`). The bridge records the run's end on the open period in the same transaction that would start a period, under the research lock; an advance queued before it may replay a little past the end, and the mark says how far.
  6. *A window that holds nothing more ends with what was replayed* (found by the bridge tests). A window becomes final only when a publication after its end is staged inside it. When the run stops between two publications, staging passes the end with nothing new in the window, no advance can add to it, and the period would wait for ever. So when staging has passed the window's end and nothing was published in it since the last mark, the period ends: `SUCCEEDED` with the last mark, which is the whole window's replay, recording `covered_through` and `nothing_published_until`; or, with no mark at all, `INCOMPLETE` (`NOTHING_PUBLISHED_IN_ITS_WINDOW`). A `RETENTION_LOSS` gap since the last mark ends it as in decision 5 instead. M6's shadows have the same wait over a quiet tail, which only their owner's stop or the store's byte cap ends (§19); B6c does not change them.
  7. *No owner or agent read names control work.* The owner's experiment list, detail and cell reads, the status's active job and its counts leave out `CONTROL`; the owner's cancel route answers `JOB_NOT_FOUND` for one, and the agent's `read_experiment` tool `SCOPE_DENIED`. While a control advance runs, the research status still reads `running`, naming no job (§19). Nothing serves a period or a mark in M7.
  8. *The bridge does it with the shadows.* Its daily schedule first records each run's end and starts due periods, only when a new shadow could start (research approved, the current store usable and not about to rotate), then advances each open period on its store, one a day, only on an idle runner, as it advances shadows. Store loss, the store's cap and a retention gap end the periods bound to that store with the shadows, and a store is kept while an open period is bound to it.
  9. *The tests join two existing phases* (plan §9's line for B6). `Comparison` also runs `tests/test_m7_controls.py` on the engine target (one more file in its list, its time comment). `ShadowSchedule` runs `tests/test_m7_control_bridge.py` after M6's shadow tests on the same two clusters, as its own step (`b6c-control-replay-tests`, 300 s), removing an earlier attempt's evidence file before it and recording the file's path and SHA-256 after it, as `Comparison` does for its file (8 new lines besides comments). No new function, phase or profile row. They are recorded here as B2's, B6a's and B6b's harness lines were (plan §10).
  10. *What the independent review changed* (one major point, three minor points and three nits, §18):
      - *A period is replayed only from a store that holds its freeze.* A store holds every quote main published from main's quote retention (7 days) less an hour before the store's staging began (`COVERED`). Before that main may have deleted rows, and nothing records it: staging compares consecutive quotes only. A period frozen earlier ends `INCOMPLETE` (`STORE_STARTS_AFTER_ITS_FREEZE`, naming the store and the time it covers from) at its first advance, before anything is replayed or closed as a quiet window, and the run's next period can then start. That happens only to a period that starts long after its freeze, when research was unapproved for weeks or the store began late. Each mark records `store_covers_from`. The warm-up is then covered too: a new store backfills final candles for 21 days, and main keeps them for 730.
      - *A period that is not finished in time ends.* An advance that fails other than by a prefix mismatch leaves the period running. Nothing else ended a period whose last advance kept failing on a store at its day cap, and that store stayed referenced. A period not finished 7 days after its window's end, or after its own start when that is later (`FINISH_GRACE`), now ends `INCOMPLETE` (`NOT_FINISHED_IN_TIME`) and keeps its marks. It is not ended while its own work is with the runner.
      - *The grid is absolute time.* 21 days are 504 hours, in the code (in UTC) and in the check. In a session time zone with daylight saving, a period would otherwise be 503 or 505 hours.
      - *The owner's dataset list leaves out a window sealed for the control replay alone.* A window that a shadow also advanced on stays listed, as the shadow's.
      - *Late results.* The bridge asks a store for a late result only of control work that was sent. Work that its period's end canceled while queued was never sent. A job's trial row is skipped because the job's kind is `CONTROL`, not because its hypothesis is empty.
      - *Left as they are, with reasons.* A period whose replay of the reference did not reach the protocol's sample (a short window) still ends `SUCCEEDED`. As for a shadow, `SUCCEEDED` says the window was replayed whole, and the mark carries the reference's status. A later comparison with the agent run must evaluate the run under the period's cost scenario, `H1`, which its detail records. M7 compares nothing.
- **Decided while building B7 (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *The owner starts generation 3's three owner-startable templates.* `MARKET_BRIEF_V1`, `PORTFOLIO_DECISION_V1` and `PROMOTION_V2` name `OWNER` as a starter (§5.6), but 0008's workflow guard let the API identity insert only M5's and M6's four owner pairs. `0009` replaces that guard with 0008's text, unchanged but for three more owner pairs after its four, and the downgrade restores 0008's text. `Migration7`'s helper declares the change: the guard is 0008's text with exactly those three pairs inserted, under the same name and grants (found while writing B7's evidence: the helper's check that no existing function changes would have failed on the PC). A generation-3 start answers `503 DECISION_SCHEMA_PENDING` before `0009`.
  2. *The owner's decision names nothing.* `PORTFOLIO_DECISION_V1` takes no parameter; the server writes the universe of the policy its run trades as its symbols, as a trigger does, and the request's digest names only the template. The API refuses the start before a row exists for the reasons the worker would refuse it before a model call: `POLICY_NOT_INSTALLED`, then the worker's checks in the worker's order (the policy run, its policy, its definitions, its state, the owner's approval, the decision suite; `decisions.policy_ready`), read again from the tables the API identity reads (`decision_views.decision_refusal`, which the status also uses). The worker checks each again when it starts the workflow. It also refuses `DECISION_IN_PROGRESS` while any decision workflow is open. That last check is advisory: a trigger can start a decision between it and the insert. The worker's one decision per symbol and bar (B5) still holds then, so two decisions cannot both act on one bar. In `OFFLINE_TEST` (the isolated tests only) the run may be a fixture run, as the worker allows there.
  3. *A revocation is recorded first; the epoch follows it.* `/v1/approvals` records an owner's `REVOKE` of an installed `AGENT_DRIVEN` policy in its own transaction and answers it as recorded. The engine acts on the revocation in its next step without the epoch (B3). The API then raises the agents' permission epoch once for that revocation, reason `POLICY_REVOKED:<approval id>`, in a second transaction that first takes the epoch row's lock (`FOR UPDATE`) and raises only if no increment carries that reason, so two callers raise it once. A lock or statement timeout, or no free connection, is tried again, up to three attempts; any other database error ends the attempt. Either failure is logged (`permission_epoch_raise_failed`, with the error's class or `TIMED_OUT`). `permission_epoch_raised` says whether the raise has happened: `true`, `false` when it was not seen to finish, `null` for every other action. A commit whose answer was lost may have raised it, so `false` can follow a raise; the record's read then says `true`. Sending the same request again returns the same record and tries the raise again; `GET /v1/approvals/requests/{key}` only reports it. Raising the epoch inside the revocation's transaction was rejected: it would wait on every engine step holding the epoch and could fail a revocation that should stand. The owner's revocation never depends on the raise.
  4. *A resume is one per pause, whatever the engine answered.* The owner may resume a pause the engine has not answered yet, one it opened with effect, or one it opened without effect (`NONE`, a run that was not live). The engine answers requests in sequence order, and a resume always comes after its pause, so a pause and its resume answered in one step first end the run's unfilled entries, then close; the resume's consequences say so (`A_PAUSE_NOT_OPENED_YET_OPENS_FIRST_AND_ENDS_THE_RUNS_UNFILLED_ENTRIES`). A second resume is `409 PAUSE_ALREADY_RESUMED`, also when two commit at once (the table's unique constraint `decision_pause_resumed_once`). The key is unique across the owner's agent requests, as M6's are: a decision probe's key resent for a resume is `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. `GET /v1/entry-pauses/requests/{key}` reads a resume back (`ACCEPTED`, then `APPLIED` once the engine closed the pause), as M5 and M6 requests read back, so a browser that lost the answer can recover it.
  5. *Each stage of an intent has its own field.* The intent views keep the model's request (its choices only, labeled `MODEL_AUTHORED`, `UNTRUSTED`), the review, the submission or withdrawal, the engine's one answer, the risk gate's permitted sizing and the modeled fills apart. None is inferred from another: `permitted` comes only from the risk decision the engine's `ACCEPTED` event names, and `filled` only from fills of the orders the answer names. `state` is one label for a list and never replaces the fields.
  6. *Promotions show the approval V2 brings.* `approval` is `REJECT_ONLY` for a `PROMOTION_V1` request and `ENTRY_BASIS` for a `PROMOTION_V2` one; M6 sent `NOT_AVAILABLE_IN_THIS_RELEASE`, which the web still accepts while the API rolls out.
  7. *The stop in force is the latest recorded stop.* `stop_in_force` on a position is the newest stop of the decision's tightenings (`decision_stop_updates`), or the mandate's stop when there is none or before `0009`. The engine's own reads are unchanged.
  8. *The status says whether decisions run on the main account, from recorded facts only.* `decisions` is `unconfigured` before `0009`, `not_installed`, `unapproved`, `inactive` (no live forward run of the policy; the isolated tests' fixture runs count as none), `blocked` (the worker would refuse a decision, or the runtime is not `ENABLED` or is latched), `entry_paused` (an open pause of the run), `running`, or `unavailable` when it cannot be read.
  9. *The web accepts both API shapes.* The web is deployed before the API (§5.12). Every field the M7 API adds is optional in the web's Zod schemas, and every enum keeps its M6 values beside the new ones. The web calls none of the new routes until B8. Four existing tests change on purpose, each because M7 changed the contract they asserted: `test_api.py` (the status gains `decisions`), `test_m5_api.py` (the runtime's actions gain `decision_probe`), `test_m6_api.py` (a V1 promotion's `approval` is `REJECT_ONLY` and its actions name `approve` and `revoke`; the owner-startable templates and their parameters gain generation 3's three) and the web's `research.test.tsx` (a promotion decision of `APPROVE` now parses, so the invalid action it checks is `PROMOTE`).
  10. *`ApiAuth7`'s harness lines* (plan §10). The phase runs `tests/test_m7_api.py` (`-m api_auth7`) on the agent target as B5's agent phases run theirs: the phase in their list in `tools/m6/agents.psm1` and `tools/lib/acceptance-harness.ps1`, its marker and file, its scope, and its record's `not_run` (the same-origin functions and their Origin and CSRF checks, which come with B8's pages). `tools/profiles/m7.psd1` marks it `M7_B7_ISOLATED` (window 8 minutes, deadline 5, untimed on Docker); `check-profiles.ps1` counts it built. About 20 lines besides comments; no new function or phase.
  11. *The worker does not import the owner's module* (found by the database-free run of B7's code). The owner's start of a decision and the resume read `decision_views`, which imports the experiments' views and approvals. `app.agents.controls` imported it, and the worker's workflow, runtime and probe modules imported `controls` for `workflow_mode()` alone, so what a tool handler can reach included `app.execution.fill_model` and `app.experiments.approvals`, which M5's and B5's import checks forbid. `workflow_mode()` now lives in `app.agents.states`, which the worker already imports; `controls` imports it from there. Both checks fail on B7's first commit and pass now.
  12. *What the independent review changed.* A read-only review of the B7 diff at `9800352` found no blocker, two points to fix and seven nits; it had confirmed the epoch raise's fix for a free connection already in that commit. The changes to the two writes, the cursor and the epoch raise each have a test that fails without them (§18, B7):
      - *Two writes broke the rule of `503 TRY_AGAIN`.* The decision probe caught no database error, so a wait over a second on the owner's lock, or for a free connection, was a `500`; the resume caught the lock timeout but not the wait for a connection. Both now answer `503 TRY_AGAIN`, as every M7 start does. M5's probe and enable requests have the same gap; they are left as they are, since B7 changes no M5 route.
      - *Tests proved less than their names.* Added: the M7 promotions view (a `PROMOTION_V2` request approved by the owner, applied by SYSTEM, then revoked, with its actions and consequences), paging by time for intents and outcomes, the stages `DRAFTED`, `NOT_SUBMITTED`, `WITHDRAWN`, `EXPIRED` and an accepted entry not yet filled, and `/v1/experiments` with a `strategy_id`. The check before `0009` changes the revision the routes read on a `0009` database, so it does not reach the reads that look for `0009`'s tables; they ran once on a real `0008` database (§18, B7).
      - *Nits taken:* a sequence cursor past the largest `bigint` is `422 INVALID_CURSOR`, not a `500`; the resume's consequences name the pause that opens first (decision 4); the owner's decision takes its run's policy's universe, not V1's (decision 2; no test tells them apart, since both of today's policies trade one universe); a failed epoch raise is logged (decision 3); the contract says `false` can follow a raise; the harness's message and the profile's header name `ApiAuth7`; an intent's detail reads its orders once. *Left as they are:* the review and outcome-review lookups read `agent_workflows` by a `jsonb` parameter without an index, bounded by the page.

- **Decided while building B8a (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B8 is two pull requests.* B8a builds the pages that read (`/decisions`, a decision's page, `/policies` and the status row) and the frame policy, with their tests and harness phases. B8b builds the owner's controls on the pages that exist: the "Main operating policy" section of `/approvals` and a promotion's Approve, the operating policy in the experiment form, the resume of an entry pause, the Agents page's generation-3 starts, decision probe and grants, the positions' stop in force and agent exit, and the risk page's use of the action limits, which needs one more API field. Each control needs its own consequence text and its own browser test; the reads stand alone and let the owner see a decision first. Both come before B9.
  2. *Routes.* `/decisions` holds its filters (`symbol`, `action`) and its page (`cursor`) in the URL, validated by the route; `/decisions/$intentId` is a sibling route (`decisions_.$intentId`), so the list's search does not carry into a decision; `/policies`. The navigation gains "Decisions" and "Policies" after "Evidence". An outcome has no page of its own: a decision whose position has closed reads its outcome record (`/v1/outcomes/{position}`) on its own page. `/policies` reads the list, whose items carry the difference from their predecessor; the versions route has no page, since each policy of this build is its own first version.
  3. *Cadence,* as M6's pages (FRONTEND "Refresh"): a list holding an open draft (`DRAFTED`, `SUBMITTED`, `ACCEPTED`) and a decision that can still change at the engine's step (one of those states, or an open position whose stop can tighten) are read every 5 s; other lists, decisions and the policies every 30 s; an outcome record once. A read that answered "unavailable" is read again every 30 s, so the notice's "reads again by itself" holds.
  4. *The frame policy is set in the server entry, on every response it makes.* `src/server.ts` passes each response through `framed()`, which sets `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and appends a `Content-Security-Policy` of `frame-ancestors 'none'`, keeping any policy the response already has (a second header is a second policy, and each is enforced). CSP Level 3 ignores `frame-ancestors` in a `<meta>` element, so it is a header ([INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE](../contracts/INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE.md#m7-b8a-decision-pages-and-the-frame-policy--2026-10-16-execplan-0026)). The installed Start release's `createServerEntry` converts no error (the current documentation describes a later one that does), so an error that escapes Start or the body check would leave without the policy; the entry catches it and answers a framed plain `500` that names nothing, logging the error unless the client went away. Pages, server functions, the Host check's `421`, the body check's `400`, `408` and `413`, a `404` and the `500` all carry it. Nitro's static handler answers `public/` (`favicon.ico`, `manifest.json`, `robots.txt`) and the built `/assets/` before the entry, without it (found by the review, decision 10); none of those files is a document, and framing concerns documents.
  5. *What the decision pages keep apart.* A decision's page has one card for each stage the API keeps apart (B7, decision 5): the model's request with its choices and the draft's own rationale, invalidation and uncertainty level, labeled "model-authored, untrusted" and "discretionary, not a calibrated probability"; its review; the submission or withdrawal; the engine's one answer with its reason codes; the risk gate's permitted sizing; the orders and fills, labeled `MODELED FILLS` and with their cost scenario; the position's terms with each stop tightening; the outcome record and its review. A value is drawn only from its own field: nothing permitted or filled without the engine's acceptance, decimals as the API's strings. Model-authored text is drawn as text, never as markup or a link.
  6. *Unavailable is not an error.* A decision read before `0009` (`503 DECISION_SCHEMA_PENDING`) and a route an older API lacks (`404` without a code) show a notice naming the state and migration `0009`, with no control; an unknown decision or outcome (`INTENT_NOT_FOUND`, `OUTCOME_NOT_FOUND`) shows "not found"; anything else is the page's error. M6's shared `ReadState` now takes which routes a read belongs to, so its notice names the right migration; M6's pages pass `research` as before.
  7. *What B8a leaves to B8b,* because a page would show it wrong without B8b's control or field: the positions' and the risk view's stop in force and the agent-exit label (`TREND_EXIT` on an agent's exit, §19), and the risk page's use of the policy's action limits.
  8. *The harness phases* (plan §10). `BrowserDecisions` and `BrowserDecisionsError` run `apps/web/e2e/m7.spec.ts` on the images and source `BrowserResearch` built and verified, which they compare; they build nothing. `BrowserDecisions` resets the browser target's database at the profile's head, seeds `tests/m7_browser_fixture.py`, checks this run's API samples against the web's strict schemas and pages, starts the API and the web, and runs the six `@decisions` tests at 390 and 1440 px; `BrowserDecisionsError` resets it at the profile's previous revision for the `@unavailable` test, then stops the API for the `@error` test. M6's helpers in `tools/m6/browser.psm1` now take a suite (its spec, selecting variable, expected counts, results directories, fixture, sample place and tests); M6's commands and labels are unchanged. One new function, `Invoke-M7BrowserPhase` (81 lines besides comments); with the suite table, the helpers' new parameter, the dispatch and the profile, 122 lines added and 26 removed besides comments. `tools/profiles/m7.psd1` marks both `M7_B8_ISOLATED` (windows 12 and 10 minutes, deadlines 9 and 7, not measured on Docker); `check-profiles.ps1` counts them built.
  9. *M7-OD2 is decided* (the owner's card answer of 2026-10-16, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-16--m7-od2-the-pilot-ends-early-once-m7-a-is-deployed-and-probed)): (b), the pilot ends once M7-A is deployed and probed.
  10. *The independent review of B8a* (read-only, on `57a88ea` and `6e312ed`) found no blocker, two points and four nits. Each is fixed or, for the frame policy, the claim is narrowed; each fix in the web has a unit test that fails without it, and the harness's is shown on the stubs (§18):
      - *The list did not show a draft's review,* so a draft its review rejected read like an expired one. The list now has an "Intent review (model-authored)" column with the verdict and the review's workflow state.
      - *"Not found" was any coded `404`.* A read now names the one code that means its record does not exist (`INTENT_NOT_FOUND`, `OUTCOME_NOT_FOUND`; none for the list and the policies). An uncoded `404` (`REQUEST_FAILED`) is an API without the route; any other coded `404` is an error.
      - *Nitro's static files do not pass through the server entry.* `public/` (`favicon.ico`, `manifest.json`, `robots.txt`) and the built `/assets/` are answered before it, without the frame headers. None is HTML, and framing concerns documents, so the claim is narrowed to the entry's responses (decision 4) rather than adding a route rule that would also rewrite the entry's own policy header.
      - *The fixture's log was copied into Playwright's output directory,* which Playwright clears when it starts, so `fixture.json` would not have survived the run. M6's phase had the same line. Both now copy it beside the directory (`browser-results/research-fixture.json`, `browser-results/decisions-fixture.json`).
      - *The web drew whatever stages the API sent.* The summary and detail schemas now refuse a body that shows sizing without an accepted entry, orders, fills or a filled state without an acceptance, or an answer without a submission.
      - *A stored document was re-serialized before display.* M6's `StoredText` now re-indents a JSON text only when reading it back gives the same text; otherwise it shows the stored text, so a number, an escaped character or a key order JavaScript would change is shown as stored. Every sample in both sample sets reads back exactly.

- **Decided while building B8b (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B8 is three pull requests.* B8b builds the owner's controls of B8a decision 1 on the Approvals, Experiments, risk and position pages. The Agents page's generation-3 starts, decision probe and grants are B8c, together with the owner's two additions to that page and the System page ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-16--m7-the-owner-adds-a-clearer-runtime-label-and-a-pre-filled-profile-form)): one page, one review. Both come before B9.
  2. *The "Main operating policy" section of `/approvals`.* It holds the installed definitions only agent-driven runs bind: a `PROPOSED` strategy whose `policy_kind` is `AGENT_DRIVEN`, and a `PROPOSED` evaluation protocol that `applies_to` `AGENT_DRIVEN_RUNS_ONLY`; neither is listed again among the definitions. A fixture-only variant (`AGENT_POLICY_V1_SOLO`, which the install command does not write) stays among the definitions, and before the install the page is as it was. Each card states "If approved" and "If revoked". The revocation text is a new field of the approvals view, `revoke_consequences`, written for those two kinds only (`app/policy/document.py`): the run bound to the hash pauses at the engine's next step with cause `APPROVAL_REVOKED` and its unfilled entries are canceled; while it is paused the agents' drafts, submissions and decision starts are refused, so an open position closes only by its protective stop, its time exit, an exit order already accepted or the owner's flatten; for a policy the agents' epoch is then raised once, which ends all agent work in flight, not only this policy's (M6's revocation effects, `_REVOKED` in `app/agents/approvals.py`, each named); the run resumes only after a new approval and the owner's resume. A definition's confirmation shows the text of the action chosen; a rejection's says what a rejection records and gives the approval text only as the alternative (decision 13).
  3. *A revocation whose epoch raise was not seen.* The approval's answer carries `permission_epoch_raised` (B7 decision 3). When it is `false` the page says the revocation stands. The approvals view's history lists such a revocation with `permission_epoch_raised` `false` and the key and version it was sent with, so the page offers "Send the revocation of <ID> again" from the history, after a reload too, until the raise is recorded (decision 13); it sends the same key and payload, and the API returns the same record and tries the raise again. An outcome resolved by its key reads as the direct answer would; an answer that names another key, target, hash or action is an unknown outcome.
  4. *An entry-basis promotion* (`PROMOTION_V2`) offers Approve, Reject and Revoke as the API's `actions` allow; a request for consideration (`PROMOTION_V1`) offers Reject only, as in M6. Approve is offered only when the scope the server wrote into the request reads: the requested scope, the policy and its hash, and the candidate's ATR period, stop multiple and hold. A request whose scope does not read is shown in full and offers no Approve. The dialog shows the exact hash with the scope. The research approvals page now says which request can be approved, and M6's browser spec reads the changed texts.
  5. *A draft on the operating policy.* The draft form offers a Strategy choice once an operating policy is installed: the reference and each installed `PROPOSED` policy. A reference draft sends no `strategy_id`, so its request and its request hash stay M4's. The experiment's page now names the manifest's strategy and its hash; nothing on it said which strategy a draft trades (found by the browser test).
  6. *The action limits* are a new field of the risk view, `action_limits`, null before `0009` or without a live run of an operating policy in that mode. They hold the run, the policy and its hash, the window, each symbol's accepted entries and cooldown, the entries in all, the submissions and the notional the window counts (entries' commitments plus exits' fills, protective ones too, as the page's label says), counted with the engine's own functions (`intents.accepted_entries`, `committed_notional`, `last_close`, `last_cancel_bar`, and `cooldown_opens`, which the engine's `_limits` calls too since decision 13). A symbol's cooldown is the open time of the first decision bar a new entry passes the re-entry and cancellation rules ("a new entry can first decide on the bar opening …"), shown while it is later than the latest closed bar. The notional limit needs the latest valid equity; without it the limit is null with `VALUATION_UNAVAILABLE` and the page says so. The page reads; the engine checks each limit again at its step.
  7. *Entry pauses and the owner's resume.* The risk page lists the entry pauses of the limits' run, newest first, each with its state, the engine's answer, the owner's resume and the reason the agent gave, labeled untrusted, with the references it cited as stored text. "Resume entries" is offered when the API's `actions.resume` is true. It opens a dialog with the API's consequences and sends one key; an unknown outcome is resolved by that key (`/v1/entry-pauses/requests/{key}`). The page says that the engine closes the pause at its next step and shows nothing closed before that is recorded.
  8. *The stop in force and the agent's exit.* The positions list shows "Stop trigger in force" beside the planned stop (B4's `stop_in_force`; "Not reported by this API" when absent), and so does a position's ledger. The exit of a position whose mandate leaves the exit to the agent (`AGENT_DISCRETIONARY_EXIT_V1`) reads "AGENT EXIT (recorded as TREND EXIT)" on the position's page; the orders list keeps the origin the order carries.
  9. *The risk view on a database at `0008`.* B4's open risk read `decision_stop_updates` without the check the position views make, so `/v1/risk` failed on a database at `0008`, where the API can run during a rollout (R29). Found while building the action limits; the view now takes the mandate's stop there and has no limits.
  10. *An M7 request before `0009`.* The API refuses it with `503 DECISION_SCHEMA_PENDING` before writing anything, so the web reports a refusal, as M6's `RESEARCH_SCHEMA_PENDING`; any other `5xx` stays an unknown outcome to resolve by its key.
  11. *Phone width.* A definition's ID and a predecessor's changed values on `/approvals` have no break point; they now wrap at any character at 390 px (found by the browser test).
  12. *The browser fixture and the harness.* The fixture adds a closed position whose mandate leaves its exit to the agent, with a completed trend-exit obligation; SYSTEM's entry-basis request for an `EVIDENCE_READY` fixture candidate; a decision's entry pause, opened by the engine (a labeled engine answer); and the main paper account with the labeled synthetic initial contribution (`fixtures.initialize_account`), so a draft can be composed. It writes seven more samples (seventeen and the index). In `tools/m6/browser.psm1` the M7 suite expects 11 `@decisions` tests and 18 sample files, and runs `src/decisions/controls.test.tsx` on the fresh samples too: three changed lines, the table's values only.
  13. *The independent review of B8b* (read-only, on `de99f6a`, `6824233` and `52c512c`) found no blocker, one major point, six minor points and five nits. Each is fixed, or its claim narrowed, and each fix has a test that fails without it (§18):
      - *The policy's revocation text said "agent work in flight ends"* (major). Raising the one epoch ends more than the policy's work: queued and running research jobs and source refreshes (final for their input), started workflows of every template, model calls in flight and workflows the system queued, and a late result is history only. Both the policy's and the fixture variant's texts now name each effect; the unit test fails when M6's list gains an effect the text does not name.
      - *"Stops, time exits and exits continue"* was wrong for an agent's exit: while the run is paused its drafts, submissions and decision starts are refused (`POLICY_PAUSED`, `POLICY_RUN_NOT_RUNNING`). The text now says what closes a position then (decision 2).
      - *The notional was labeled "Entry notional committed"* though `committed_notional` counts exit fills too. Now "Notional: entries committed and exits filled".
      - *The resend lived in page state:* any later decision cleared it, a key resolved from an earlier request lost it, and a reload dropped it. It is now drawn from the history the API states (decision 3).
      - *A rejection's dialog showed the approval text.* It now says what a rejection records (decision 2).
      - *The view's cooldown restated the engine's rule.* `intents.cooldown_opens` now holds it for both (decision 6); the engine's two codes stay apart. The test of `/v1/risk` at `0008` seeds no position, so its claim is narrowed to what it shows (§18).
      - *`COMMANDS` had a placeholder* where the M7 database result belongs; it states the result now.
      - *Nits taken:* a pause opened without effect was polled every 5 s as if it could still change; the cooldown read as "from the bar's opening", while the first decision on that bar follows its close; the risk page named the resume as its control when no run offers one; the experiments' `recordApproval` did not check that the answer names the key, target, hash and action sent (M4's code; the resume checks its own). *Left as it is:* the notional limit's equity is the latest valid persisted valuation, which the page's valuation card shows with its time, while the engine uses its step's equity; the limit's text already says "the latest valid equity". Also found while fixing: the rules card did not say that open risk is measured to the stop in force, and the browser test of the revocation compared the dialog with an empty text (§18); both are fixed.

- **Decided while building B8c (2026-10-16), within the approved plan and the owner's two additions:**
  1. *The Agents page shows what code defines, per generation.* A "Templates and role grants" section has one card for each generation the runtime view lists (B7's `generations`): its templates with role, purpose, who starts them and whether the owner can, and each role's tools ("No tool" for an empty grant). The page only shows them; the dispatcher checks every call against the grant of its workflow's generation, and a role's name or a prompt confers nothing. An API released before M7 lists no generation, and the section is absent.
  2. *The start dialog offers what the generations mark `owner_startable`,* in the page's own order; a template the page does not know is not offered, and an API before M7 gets M5's and M6's four. Generation 3's starts send exactly what their templates register: a market brief its symbols; a portfolio decision nothing but its key (the server takes the policy's symbols); an entry-basis request (`PROMOTION_V2`) one `EVIDENCE_READY` candidate's ID. No template takes a price, a quantity, an intent or free text, and the dialog says so. A template the API would refuse for a reason the page can read is not offered: the research templates while research is unavailable (as in M6), and the decision and the entry-basis request while the policies read says no operating policy of this build is installed. A read that has not answered blocks nothing; the API checks again, and its refusals (`POLICY_RUN_NOT_LIVE`, `DECISION_SUITE_NOT_VERIFIED`, `DECISION_IN_PROGRESS`, a candidate outside the policy's sets) are shown as refusals. The candidates offered are the `EVIDENCE_READY` ones, as for `PROMOTION_V1`; a candidate held at `MAIN_APPROVAL_PENDING` only by a consideration request, which the API would also take, is not offered here; SYSTEM starts an entry-basis request for it once the policy is installed (§5.6).
  3. *The decision probe.* "Start decision probes" is offered when the API's `actions.decision_probe` holds, a current profile is approved, and the policies read shows the operating policy (`AGENT_POLICY_V1`, as `promotions.installed` names it) installed for this build, its latest decision an approval and a run of it `RUNNING` (decision 9). The API does not read the policy for this request and the agent worker refuses it later, which this page would not show; until the read answers the button is not shown, and when it answers otherwise the card says what the probes wait for, with a link to Policies. Its dialog states what the suite does: CP-23 to CP-27 on the installed policy and its running run, within the suite's own call budget, live calls on the owner's allowance, no submission, no entry pause and no epoch raise; the result is recorded for the profile and the policy together, and decisions start only while the latest is a pass; the agent worker refuses it unless the policy is installed, approved and running. It sends the approved profile's hash and the runtime's version with one key. The probe runs table names each run's suite (the decision suite with its policy and hash); a "Decision suite results" table lists the latest five results with profile and policy. The capability probe's text no longer says "CP-01 to CP-14" (M6 had extended the suite); it says the suite holds every capability probe of the release, that without an approved research envelope and a sealed dataset the research probes fail, and that a failure latches the runtime, which blocks Enable and the decision probes until a later run passes.
  4. *The owner's runtime label* ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-16--m7-the-owner-adds-a-clearer-runtime-label-and-a-pre-filled-profile-form)). The System page reads the agent runtime view only while the status says `login_required`. With a signed-in runner (`auth_state` `PRESENT`) in `LOGIN_REQUIRED`, the model row reads "needs profile approval" while no current profile is approved, and "profile approved, probes not enabled yet" once one is and the owner has not yet allowed probes: the API's state is the same, and the first label would then be false. Both link "Open Agents". Without that view, or with any other sign-in state, the row keeps the API's word; a sign-in fault is `blocked` and stays so.
  5. *The pre-filled compose form* (the same decision). It starts from the newest approved profile: the newest by composition, among the ten newest profiles the runtime view lists, whose latest decision is an approval, current or not. After a deployment changes the definitions the approved profile is no longer current, and that is when the form is needed. "Newest" is not the latest approval in time (the view carries no approval time): an owner who approves an older profile again after a newer one starts from the newer, and with more than ten profiles composed since the last approval nothing is chosen (decision 9). The model is kept when this discovery offers it as usable, and the effort when that model offers it (the default effort needs only the model); otherwise nothing is chosen and the card says why. When it is the profile in force the card says so, and composing a document the API already holds (`controls.compose_profile` returns the existing row) is reported as that profile with its decision, not as a new draft. It fills the form only: the owner composes, reviews and approves. The card starts again when the discovery or the newest approved profile changes, and keeps the owner's choice on any other refresh.
  6. *Texts.* The page's opening no longer says agents have no trading authority: under an approved policy a decision may submit a trade intent, which the engine checks; no agent names a price or a quantity, or holds approval or account authority. A profile's changes group generation 3's budgets (`budgets_v3`) apart from the runtime identity. The candidate list's empty text names no template.
  7. *The browser fixture and the harness.* The fixture enables the runtime on the agent worker's own path: activation, discovery, two profiles the owner approves over HTTP (an older one composed before generation 3 at effort `low`, which authorizes nothing, then the current one at `medium`), `PROBE_ONLY`, a `VERIFIED` capability record and `ENABLED`. Its decisions run under that profile, whose decision suite it records as passed with the policy; and it adds a second `EVIDENCE_READY` candidate inside the policy's sets with no request, on the first one's fixture hypothesis (a dataset's hash is unique). It writes `agent-runtime.json` too (nineteen files with the index). `revoke-profile` revokes the current profile over HTTP and runs the agent worker's own reconcile, which returns the runtime to `LOGIN_REQUIRED`; the `@profile` selection runs on that state. In `tools/m6/browser.psm1` the M7 suite expects 12 `@decisions` tests, 1 `@profile` test and 19 sample files and runs `src/agents/controls.test.tsx` on the fresh samples, and `BrowserDecisions` runs `revoke-profile` and `@profile` after `@decisions`: 4 lines added and 2 changed besides comments and the suite table's values. `revoke-profile` first checks that the database is disposable, at `0009`, with one owner and the runtime `ENABLED`, before it changes any role: the roles are the cluster's (decision 9). Its window and deadline in `tools/profiles/m7.psd1` grow from 12 and 9 to 14 and 11 minutes, not measured on Docker.
  8. *From run `m7-b8a` on the owner's PC* (§18). The frame test's body over the limit now goes through a request context of its own, which it closes: the server answers `413` without reading the body and may close that connection, and the PC run's next request on it got `ECONNRESET`. The risk page's `500` at `0008` was B8b decision 9, already on `main`. The fixture database that recovered for longer than the harness waits, and the run's scheduling, are B9's.
  9. *The independent review* (read-only, of `a441b5b`): no blocker, one major point, six minor points and seven nits; all are fixed and each fix has a test that fails without it (§18, B8c). The major point: the market brief's text said only that it drafts no trade, but an acceptable review of an owner-started brief or event investigation records `ASSESSMENT_READY` (`decisions.py`, `_assessment_ready`), and while the operating policy's run is live, with `REVIEWED_ASSESSMENT` in its cadence (as in `AGENT_POLICY_V1`), a portfolio decision follows that may submit a trade intent; both texts now say so. The minor points: "last approved" did not mean the latest approval in time, so the form now says "newest approved profile" and decision 5 states the window; the form started from the profile in force called composing it again a new draft (decision 5); the capability probe's text left out that the research probes fail without an envelope and a dataset and that the latch blocks Enable and the decision probes (decision 3); the decision probe was offered with no policy ready and the worker's refusal would not show (decision 3); three tests could not tell the profile in force from a newer approved one, the form's restart from a refresh, or a refusal from an acceptance, and now do; `revoke-profile` changed the cluster's roles before it checked the database (decision 7). The nits: the second label now reads "probes not enabled yet", as it is also shown after the owner asked for probes and before the worker applied it; `policyInstalled` counts only `AGENT_POLICY_V1`, not a fixture-only variant a disposable database installed; `tools/profiles/m7.psd1`'s comment gives the cloud times; decision 7's line count; the older profile's docstring; the text for an approved profile without a model; and `modelState`'s comment names `UNKNOWN` and says why every `login_required` row links to the Agents page (it shows the runtime's state and its sign-in in each case).

- **Decided while building B9a (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B9 is three pull requests and one run.* B9a is `PerformanceEngine7`, with the fixture database's clean stop that run `m7-b8a` needed (decision 7). B9b is `MixedVersion7`. B9c is `tools/m7-deploy.ps1`, `tools/m7-boundary.ps1` and `BoundaryRehearsal`. Then B9's final run on the owner's PC takes every §10 phase on one fresh run and one image (M7-OD9). Each pull request has its own independent review.
  2. *The target.* The phase runs on the saturation target, which an M7 run already prepares (`tools/profiles/m7.psd1`, `Kinds`) and which no other M7 phase used then, since `ResearchSaturation` was not one of M7's (since 2026-10-17 the activation gate's saturation phase runs on it too, on a run of its own). It uses the product Compose file's PostgreSQL (2 CPU, 2 GiB) and two engine-tests containers at the product limits (1 CPU, 512 MiB each): the feed with the agent desk, and the production execution worker (`app.execution.worker.Worker`) bound to `AGENT_POLICY_V1`, as M3 and M6 measured it. No research service, agent worker, API or model runs: the engine beside the agent worker and the research services at their limits is `DecisionSaturation`, the activation's condition (§10), not this phase.
  3. *The budget is the existing one, unchanged* (ExecPlan 0011, line 882, and the owner's RSS-growth decision of 2026-10-06). `tests.m6_performance.saturation_execution` judges every step and every publication of the loaded interval exactly as `ResearchSaturation` does: step p95 at most 750 ms and p99 at most 1,500 ms, RSS below 256 MiB and growth below 10 MiB, at least 99 % of the publications looked at, none stale, no step raised and the lease kept. ExecPlan 0011 declared a 60-minute feed; the interval is thirty minutes inside one HH:07–HH:58 window, as the owner decided for `ResearchSaturation`, whose execution part this is (2026-10-06, point 2). R24 asks about a step *with intake*, so the same two percentiles are judged again over the steps begun while the engine knew that a submission or a pause request waited for its answer (`Summary.intake_open`, which the worker refreshes before every batch): at least 20 of them, or the phase fails as unmeasured, and at most twice the answers of the interval, so that a request left waiting cannot fill the count with cheap steps.
  4. *The load is an agent desk at about ten times the policy's rate.* The desk writes thirty requests in the thirty minutes. The first, 30 s in, is an entry of SOL that the engine accepts and fills: it takes `_take_entry`'s whole path (reference, ATR, stop, sizing, reservation and order) inside the measured minutes, and two positions are open from then on, as in M3. Then one a minute: tightenings to breakeven and to the stop template, entries the engine refuses, cancellations with nothing to cancel, an entry withdrawn before its answer, the agent's entry pause and the owner's resume, and the agent's exit of BTC five minutes before the end. SOL's bid dips below its stop in force for 20 seconds ten minutes before the end, and its protective stop closes it. `AGENT_POLICY_V1` admits six submissions a day. Each refused entry decides on the newest final bar: in the cloud, SOL's four were refused as `DUPLICATE_DECISION` (SOL's accepted entry had decided on that bar) and BTC's three as `POSITION_OPEN` with `WATERMARK_BEHIND`; no request reached a limit's refusal, so the limits' refusals are B3's tests', not this load's (§18, B9a). Every request must get its planned answer, both positions must end as planned (closed, with or without dust), SOL's entry must be filled inside the interval and at least one tightening accepted, or the phase fails: a run whose load did not happen measures nothing. At least 99 % of the publications the feed's rate gives (two quotes and one provisional candle a second) must be published in the interval, so a feed that fell behind cannot lighten the load. The desk writes its rows as the dispatcher and the owner's API write them, by the migration identity with the guards off for that transaction, as `IntentEngine`'s tests do; testing the guards is B1's and B5's job.
  5. *The setup runs on a simulated clock.* The feed installs the policy as the install command writes it and records the labeled synthetic owner's approvals and Start through the API's own functions. The run is activated two minutes before the start of the previous UTC hour, and BTC's entry, ten seconds into that hour, is filled through the intake, so it is about an hour old when the feed is ready. The market rows are published as the simulated clock reaches them: when everything was published before the engine started, the quotes after the entries never reached it and the entries expired unfilled (found in the cloud, §18).
  6. *One window, with two minutes' lead.* The phase starts between HH:07 and HH:09 (`Window` 48, `Deadline` 46). The thirty minutes begin 120 s after the feed is ready. Nothing is claimed unless they lie inside one HH:07–HH:58 window, the worker started at least a minute before them, and the thirty minutes plus the readings after them fit the phase's deadline. A UTC hour boundary inside the measured minutes is therefore not measured; the setup includes an hour's final candle and valuation. The phase also reads the limits the worker's own cgroup reports (`cpu.max` `100000 100000`, `memory.max` `536870912`) and fails on any other.
