  7. *From run `m7-b8a`* (B8c decision 8): a PostgreSQL container of the target (the main, fixture or research one) now gets 60 s, not 15, to stop cleanly before the next phase starts it (`tools/m6/isolation.psm1`, `Stop-M6Target`). Killed after 15 s, a fixture database had spent over a minute in crash recovery, past the next phase's wait.
  8. *No baseline on the M6 build.* B0 recommended an unloaded run on the unchanged M6 build before B3 (§18, B0), and it never ran (§18, B3 and B4). The budget is absolute, and B0 lists the M3, M4 and M5 figures as its history. A separate run on the M6 build would cost a heavy window beside the pilot for a comparison that no gate uses, so it stays `NOT RUN`.
  9. *The independent review* (read-only, of `f237230`): no blocker, two major points, five minor points and seven nits; all are fixed (§18, B9a). The major points: the setup's two entries were both accepted before the measured minutes, so no accepted entry was measured with intake (decision 4); and the feed's density was not judged, so a feed that fell behind would have lightened the load unnoticed (decision 4). The minor points: steps with intake could be diluted by a request left waiting (decision 3); `ready.json` could be read half-written, and a feed that exited before it was ready was noticed only after seven minutes; the worker's limits were not checked (decision 6); the thirty-minute interval's basis was not written down (decision 3); and the latency inherited from M6 is one window's, not M3's 60 minutes (decision 3). The nits: the report's row names, the secret scan's check of the saturation cluster's volume, the window's start set before the phase's first step, a date read as a date in PowerShell 7, the report's rows for the lease, the publications, the requests and the tightenings, and decision 7's grace for every PostgreSQL container of the target.
  10. *Size.* The new harness code is 226 lines in `tools/m6/performance.psm1` (the phase's function is 221 of them), 6 in the harness, 28 in `tools/m6/report.psm1` (the report's rows and the secret scan's check of the saturation cluster), 5 in `tools/m6/isolation.psm1` and 1 in the profile check, about 270 lines within §10's estimate of 1,020 to 2,035 lines for new phase code. The workload, `tests/m7_performance.py`, is 620 lines of test code.
- **Decided while building B9b (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *The start is reached as the normal application reached it.* `MixedVersion7` builds M6's pre-M6 state (`Start-M6PreM6State`: the release before M6 on `0007`, its seeded owner and agents, the synthetic pilot prepared and started by the pilot's own images, the M5 runtime fixture `PROBE_ONLY`), then deploys the M6-A release in the order its deployment of 2026-10-08 took: web, API, migration `0008` by the M6-A migrate image, agent worker. The M6-A agent worker must leave the runtime `LOGIN_REQUIRED` with `PROFILE_DEFINITIONS_CHANGED`, as on the normal application since that day, and hold the orchestrator lease, so that `0009` runs beside a worker of the release before it that is working. Seeding the pilot on `0008` directly would have needed the pilot's M4 code to write a database it never wrote; this way every row is written by the release that wrote it on the normal application.
  2. *The M6-A images are the profile's `PreM7`,* by full ID: `migrate`, `api`, `web`, `agent-worker`, `agent-admin` and the five research and source images, as the session on the owner's PC read them on 2026-10-16 (16:07 UTC, read-only, matching the deployment record of 2026-10-08 from source `583a98a`). The phase stops before it changes anything unless all ten are on the host. The pilot's workers and the market stand-in keep `PreRelease`'s images, which the normal application's pilot runs (`execution-worker` and `evaluation-worker`) or which only sleeps (the stand-in). `PreRelease` stays M6's for the phases kept from M6.
  3. *The states, in §13.1's order, with the pilot's workers untouched throughout:* (0) the M6-A release on `0008`; (1) the M7 web against the M6-A API; (2) the M7 API on a database still at `0008`, the research and source services taking the M7 image without a container; then `0009` live, every lock of the migration sampled every 5 ms with `Migration7`'s protected set (no relation of M1 to M4 stronger than `ACCESS SHARE`) while the pilot's workers and the M6-A agent worker run; (3) the M6-A agent worker through `0009` in the same container, then restarted on it: it states the definitions mismatch (generation 3's rows), takes no lease in nine reads over 45 s, and keeps running blocked in its one container with no restart added, so a worker that crashed in a loop fails the phase; (4) the M6-A API and web on `0009`, what a rollback that swapped images before the downgrade would give: the API is live and not ready; (5) the M7 release on `0009`, the agent worker last: it must record a start or a recent heartbeat, hold the lease and leave the runtime `LOGIN_REQUIRED` with `PROFILE_DEFINITIONS_CHANGED` and no approved profile; nothing of M7 is installed or recorded, and research and sources have no login and no container. Then §14's way back: the M7 agent worker stops, the guarded downgrade of `0009` by the M7 migrate image, the M6-A API, web and agent worker, which takes its lease again. Last, after `0009` again, one non-fixture M7 record makes the downgrade refuse and change nothing.
  4. *What each state is judged on.* The API's answers, read over HTTP with the synthetic owner's session by `tests/m7_rollout.py http`: the M6-A API on `0008` answers every route of M4 to M6 and has no M7 route (404); the M7 API on `0008` answers them, answers 503 `DECISION_SCHEMA_PENDING` for the three M7 reads that need `0009` (trade intents, entry pauses, outcomes) and 200 for the policies read, which shows this build's two documents `NOT_INSTALLED` from M4's store (B7); a generation-3 start is refused with that 503 and leaves no workflow row; the M6-A API on `0009` is not ready; the M7 API on `0009` answers every read of M4 to M7 with nothing installed. The web's container image in each state (the first page is the same in both releases). The pilot as M6 judged it (`Test-M6PilotUnchanged`: the same containers, images, start times and restarts, the lease epoch held, no new segment, downtime, failure or incident, a forward checkpoint that moved on, and no failure line in the workers' logs), twice: over the whole phase, and over the minutes on `0009` alone, from the live migration to the end of state 5 (R22), so that a pause on `0009` cannot hide behind the minutes on `0008` before and after.
  5. *A record that blocks the way back.* The M7 agent worker writes nothing that `0009`'s downgrade refuses while no operating policy runs: its triggers, briefs and decisions need a live policy run (`app/agents/decisions.py`). `inert` reads every decision table empty and counts what the downgrade's guard counts, with the migration's own constants (the agent-written and execution-written tables, the M7 workflows, artifacts and trigger families, generation 3's templates, probe runs other than the capability suite's, decision-probe requests, promotions, approvals of a promotion and the control rows); any of them fails the phase there, not later as a downgrade left on `0009`. The record that makes the downgrade refuse is a trigger of an M7 family, as `Migration7`'s static copy writes it.
  6. *The helper.* `tests/m7_rollout.py` (286 lines of test code) reuses `tests/m6_rollout.py`'s API client, owner sign-in, runtime read and database guard (only a disposable pilot, rehearsal or saturation database), and `tests/m6_migration.py`'s lock sampler. `tests/m7_migration.py`'s protected set becomes `lock_sets()`, which `Migration7`'s live run and this phase share, unchanged.
  7. *Window.* 18 minutes, deadline 15, not measured on Docker: M6's `MixedVersion` took under 6 minutes on three runs, and this adds the M6-A deployment, a state, a 45 s lease watch and two waits for a lease.
  8. *The independent review* (read-only, of the uncommitted B9b on `9bd2eb5`): no blocker, one major point, four minor points and five nits. The major point: the pilot was judged only from before state 0 to the end, a span with several minutes on `0008`, so a pilot that paused on `0009` and resumed after the downgrade would have passed (decision 4: now judged on `0009` alone as well). The minor points: `inert` did not count the history the downgrade refuses, and an empty list of identities passed (decision 5); the M7 worker's runtime check passed without the M7 worker, since the M6-A worker had already left that state (decision 3: a start or a heartbeat, and the reason); a crash-looping M6-A worker on `0009` passed (decision 3); and a phase past its deadline would have thrown in the feed's stop before the target's stop (both now run, the target's in a `finally`). The nits: five of the ten `PreM7` images were checked (decision 2), state 0 did not show the M6-A worker's lease (decision 1), "refused, never queued" was shown by the 503 alone (decision 4), and this decision log's draft said four M7 reads answer 503 (decision 4). The fifth is inherited from M6: §13.1 step 5's "experiment version unchanged" is not compared by the pilot watch, and R22 does not name it; on the normal application the deploy script compares it, as M6's does (`tools/m6-deploy.ps1`), and B9c keeps that. Not taken: tying state 4's "not ready" to the schema rather than any database fault. The M6-A API at `583a98a` answered 503 on `0009` and 200 on `0008` against the same database minutes apart (§18, B9b), and its readiness accepts `0006` to `0008` only.
  9. *Size.* The new harness code is 325 lines in `tools/m6/rollout.psm1` (the phase's function is 274 of them and the M6-A deployment 37), 22 in the profile (15 of them the image list), 3 in the harness, 5 in `tools/m6/report.psm1` and 1 in the profile check, about 356 lines against §10's estimate of 300 to 400 for `MixedVersion7`. The helper is 286 lines of test code, and `lock_sets()` moves 11 lines of `tests/m7_migration.py`.

- **Decided while building B9c (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *B9 is four pull requests and one run.* B9c is `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` with its model; B9d is `tools/m7-boundary.ps1` and `BoundaryRehearsal`, which also runs this deploy script and its model on the owner's PC, as M6's rehearsal ran `tools/m6-deploy.ps1` and its model. The deploy script is what B10 runs and what B9d's rehearsal starts from, and the boundary script with its rehearsal on Docker (§10 estimates 400 to 700 lines) is a review of its own.
  2. *M7-OD10 is (b)* (the owner's card answer of 19:39 UTC, [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-16--m7-od10-m6-b-finishes-after-m7-as-inert-deployment)). M7-A deploys with research and sources inert, as on the M6-A release: a research or source service that runs, a flag that is `1` or an identity with login stops the deployment in its preflight, and their images are built from the M7 commit and not started. §13.1 step 4's recreation "where those services run" therefore recreates nothing. M6-B's remainder follows on the M7 build, its gate phases on that build (R29).
  3. *A copy, not a parameter.* `tools/m6-deploy.ps1` stays as it ran on 2026-10-08, with its model's 17 scenarios and 76 expectations. `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` is a copy for one step, `0008` to `0009`. A shared script with a revision parameter would change a script whose evidence belongs to M6-A, and the two steps differ in their names, inert facts and agent worker. M6's script took `-Revision 0009` for a rehearsal only; that parameter is not in the copy.
  4. *Ten rollback names.* `:pre-m7` tags for `api`, `web` and `agent-worker` from what runs, and for `migrate`, `agent-admin` and the five research and source names from what each name points to: those names exist since M6-A, and the builds move them. `-ExpectedPreM7Images` defaults to the ten prefixes of the profile's `PreM7` (the PC's image check of 2026-10-16), and a tag is created only from an image it names. A rollback, and a run that stops before it replaced a service, returns all ten names. `-ExpectedPilotImages` defaults to the market worker's image of ExecPlan 0024, as M6-A's deployment recorded it; M6's default is older.
  5. *M7's inert facts.* Before any tag, a deployment refuses an agent runtime in any state other than `DISABLED` or `LOGIN_REQUIRED`, an unread one included: M7-A is deployed beside a runtime that runs no model work, and the normal application's has been `LOGIN_REQUIRED` since 2026-10-08. After the agent worker: the ten decision tables exist and are empty, no agent-driven policy and no protocol other than `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V1` is installed (the query of `tests/m7_rollout.py`), no M7 agent history or control replay row exists (the downgrade's own conditions, as `MixedVersion7`'s inert check counts them), and the runtime is in the state it was in at the preflight; its state and reason are printed and recorded, which is §13.1 step 5's "runtime waiting for a new profile". The policy's "not installed" is read in the database, because the script does not sign in. The pages without signing in are the deploying session's browser check (Milestone completion deployment, step 4), as in M6.
  6. *The M7 agent worker must start.* With `ASL_AGENTS_ENABLED=1` in the env file, the deployment waits up to 60 s for a `WORKER_STARTED` event recorded after the new container started (Docker's `StartedAt`) whose lease epoch is the agent lease's epoch held now: the worker records it only after its definitions check has passed on `0009` and it holds the agent lease (the old worker releases it on stopping; it expires after 10 s). Compose stops the replaced container before it starts the new one, so a start recorded later is the new worker's. The container's start time and the row's time are both read from the clock of the machine that runs Docker and PostgreSQL; the model cannot show that, and B9d's rehearsal on Docker does. The flag is read once, in the preflight; a deployment refuses when the running worker was created with another value, and the recreated worker must carry the value read. With another value the worker idles without a database connection, and the record says so. A rollback that recreates the agent worker waits for the M6-A worker's start on `0008` the same way. A replaced service whose restart count is above 0 stops the last check, so a crash loop read in a moment of `running` does not pass. This carries B9b's review point on the phase's worker check into the script.
  7. *The restore rehearsal fingerprints M6's tables too.* Migration `0009` changes checks of research tables and creates `research_controls` and `research_control_marks`. The research, source and evidence tables of the restored copy must read the same after the upgrade and the downgrade as before, a third digest beside those of M1 to M4's tables (unchanged by the upgrade too) and of the agent tables.
  8. *Kept from M6 as it is:* the windows, the protected backup, the restore rehearsal in an internal helper with one migrate image, explicit builds, web then API, the bounded migration, the pilot judged as M6 judged it (the open experiment's state and version included, which B9b decision 8 left to this script), the way back with its repair and refused downgrade, and what a stopped run says and records.
  9. *The model.* `tools/m7/deploy-model.ps1` is M6's model adapted, 25 scenarios and 114 expectations: M6's 17 with M7's revisions, names and messages (M6's "a rollback at `0009` is refused" becomes "a deployment and a rollback at `0007` are refused"), and eight new ones: M7 stays inert, the M7 agent worker on `0009`, the research tables on the way back, and the five of decision 10 (the start of the worker this run created, `ASL_AGENTS_ENABLED` read once, reads and runtime states that are not known, a rollback that M7 records refuse, the M6-A agent worker on the way back). The model keeps the agent lease and each `WORKER_STARTED` row with its time and epoch, and answers the start query by the conditions it names. Each new check was taken out once and its scenario failed (§18).
  10. *The independent review* (read-only, of the uncommitted B9c before the market worker's default image was corrected; that change is decision 4's last sentence): no blocker, one major point, five minor points and five nits, all taken:
      - *Major:* the start check compared the count of every `WORKER_STARTED` row with the count read in the preflight, so a new lease epoch of the replaced worker (one that lost and took the lease again while the migration held its locks) passed for the new worker's start. Now decision 6's proof: after the new container's start, under the epoch held now.
      - *Minor:* a stop at the agent worker or at the last check printed "The M7 release is deployed ... Nothing has to be returned"; it now says that the release's images run but its check failed, and names the way back. `ASL_AGENTS_ENABLED` was read after the replacements; it is now read in the preflight (decision 6). The last check did not count the history and control rows the downgrade refuses (decision 5). A rollback at `0009` took its backup and stopped the agent worker before a certain refusal; its read-only preflight now counts what the downgrade refuses, with the downgrade's own conditions, and refuses with "fix forward" before any change, and the downgrade's refusal stays for a record written after that read. The rollback did not show that the M6-A worker starts on `0008`; it now waits for it (decision 6).
      - *Nits:* the runtime check is the allow-list of decision 5, the same state before and after; a rollback at `0009` refuses before any change when the `migrate` name points to its `:pre-m7` image, which cannot downgrade `0009`; both migrations name `0009_m7_agent_decisions` as their target; the rollback's recorded read of the runtime cannot fail a finished rollback; and the model now exercises the count of ten decision tables, a preflight read that fails and a start seen on a later reading.
      - *Found by the mutation pass of these fixes:* the model's clock began again with each run of the script, so a rollback's new worker could start at the same model time as the deployment's; with the epoch condition taken out, the deployment's start then counted for the rollback's. The clock is now one for the whole model, and that mutation fails only its own scenario.
  11. *Size.* `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` is 1,357 lines, of which 371 added and 166 removed against `tools/m6-deploy.ps1`; the model is 969 lines, 445 added and 105 removed against M6's. Neither is a phase, so neither counts against §10's estimate for new phase code.

- **Decided while building B9d (2026-10-16), within the approved plan:**
  1. *The engine says whether the boundary may go on.* `python -m app.execution.identity boundary-check --experiment <id>` reads, in one transaction of the migration identity: the schema; the pilot `COMPLETED` (and a forward experiment at all) and the newest forward run; no other forward experiment open or `STOPPED`; no live forward exposure; no entry signal without an outcome; no open latch, no account pause, no earlier failed reconciliation, and the reconciliation that it runs `OK`; both worker identities without login and without a session (`NOLOGIN` refuses new sessions only); no execution lease held; and the code's two documents absent, or installed with the code's hash, and unapproved. Every reason is named at once, and like every reconciliation it latches a discrepancy it finds. Its seven tests run on a scratch database (§18). The script asks it and does not repeat its reads in SQL of its own, so the rule has one place, tested in Python.
  2. *The script is §13.2's steps 2 to 4, nothing else.* Steps 0 and 1 stay the owner's existing actions (the stop in the UI, `tools/pilot-deactivate.ps1`), and the activation of §13.3 stays the owner's too. `tools/m7-boundary.ps1 -ExperimentId <pilot>` refuses, in a preflight that names every refusal at once and changes nothing but the latch the check's reconciliation sets on a discrepancy: `DOCKER_HOST` or `DOCKER_CONTEXT` in the shell; an uncommitted product tree, or one of another commit than the newest completed record of `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` for the project, or a newest record that is a rollback; `postgres`, `api` or `web` not running, or the API not ready; a schema other than `0009`; a pilot worker that runs; a pilot flag that is not `0`; and every reason of the boundary check, asked in the deployed `migrate` image. Then a protected backup, the builds of `execution-worker`, `evaluation-worker`, `pilot-admin` and `migrate`, the install in the `pilot-admin` just built, and the check again in that image. It starts, stops and recreates nothing, enables no identity, sets no flag and approves nothing; it ends with the owner's next steps. The market worker is never built here, and its image name must not move.
  3. *Every service one commit.* The code identity covers `app/` only, so two checks together: the commit must be the one the M7 release was deployed from (decision 2), and each image the script builds must compute the running API's code identity (`app.experiments.code_identity.compute()` in the image, without a network). The M7 API writes its identity into the manifest, and a worker of another build refuses the Start (§14), so a mismatch would show only at the owner's Start. The script stops at stage `build`, installs nothing, and says to build from the commit the API runs and to run it again before any activation.
  4. *The deployment's windows and records.* The builds load the machine the market worker runs on, so the script keeps M7-A's rules: a start between HH:15 and HH:35, the dump between HH:07 and HH:50, the work between HH:15 and HH:45 and never between 23:50 and 00:20 UTC, asked again before the builds and before the install. It writes `m7-boundary.json` beside its backup, or `m7-boundary-failed.json`, and a stopped run says what it left. It can be run again: a second run may name new images of the same tree (run `m7-b9a`), and the newest `m7-boundary.json` names those the activation starts; the install reports an existing row as `PRESENT`. It clears the shell's `ASL_`, `COMPOSE_` and `WEB_PORT` variables as `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` does.
  5. *The model.* `tools/m7/boundary-model.ps1`, 7 scenarios and 52 expectations, in the manner of `tools/m7/deploy-model.ps1`: the boundary and a second run; each refusal of the preflight and all of them at once; a build of another commit; a failed build, a market worker that restarts and an image name that moves during the builds; a refused install, one that names one document and one that reports another outcome; the six ways the verification can fail, among them a hash reported that the check does not read and a market worker that restarts or whose image name moves during the install; the record of the deployment (none, another commit, a rollback, and records that do not count); a discrepancy the check latched; the holdings printed; and the refused parameters and shell variables. Fifteen of the script's checks were each taken out once: eleven failed the model at once, and four did not, because no scenario reached them; the four last cases were added for them, and each of the four then failed. The nine checks the review's fixes added were each taken out once and failed it too (§18).
  6. *`BoundaryRehearsal` on the `pilot` target.* The phase uses the target kind of `MixedVersion7`, whose harness code already deploys the M6-A release beside a running synthetic pilot (`Start-M7PreM7State`) and resets that target first, rather than the `rehearsal` target, whose two research clusters `SecretScan`'s report expects after `ResearchActivationRehearsal`. Its steps: (0) both M7 scripts against their models; (1) `tools/m7-deploy.ps1`, its `-Rollback` and the deployment again, with the pilot's workers unchanged; (2) while the pilot runs, the boundary refused with nothing changed, and the guarded install refused by the database ("a forward experiment of another strategy is open"); (3) the synthetic owner's stop with flatten, until the run is `COMPLETED` with its `FINAL` report (up to 15 minutes); (4) the boundary refused while the ended pilot's workers run, with exactly the workers' logins, sessions and lease as reasons, then `tools/pilot-deactivate.ps1`; (5) the boundary, a second run that finds both documents `PRESENT`, and `tools/m7-deploy.ps1 -Rollback` refused in its preflight because M7 records exist; (6) the synthetic owner's approvals and the policy's run, `tools/pilot-activate.ps1`, and Start: the run `RUNNING` and `ACTIVATED`, its manifest naming `AGENT_POLICY_V1`, `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` and this run's code identity, both workers on the images the boundary built, and the execution worker's log bound to the policy. `postgres` and the market worker's stand-in must be unchanged from the first step to the last.
  7. *A synthetic owner through the owner's routes only.* `tests/m7_boundary.py` signs in as the fixture owner and stops, approves, creates and starts through `/v1/experiments` and `/v1/approvals` only, each approval with the version the approvals view lists at that moment and the hash it lists, which must be the code's. It refuses when a document was approved before the activation began. The cost scenario is `COST_SCENARIO_HYPOTHETICAL_H1`, the one the owner's pilot runs under ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-09-30--m4-pilot-approvals-activation-and-checkpoint-execplan-0015)).
  8. *The shell.* The M7 scripts run under a shell that holds the wrong value of every variable Compose would read (a database that does not exist, port 1, the flags the other way round, another project and other profiles); they clear them. The M4 pilot tools clear none, so the phase runs them with a clean environment, and OPERATIONS says to run them from a fresh shell.
  9. *The phase's slot.* Profile version 5, window 45 minutes, deadline 42. Its length is estimated at 30 to 35 minutes, not measured: three deployments' builds when their layers are cached, the pilot's final report, which waits for the evaluation protocol's 300 s lateness after the stop (under 8.5 minutes in the simulation here), and the activation.
  10. *What the phase does not show:* the normal application and B10; the decision probes, the runtime profile and a live decision cycle; the owner's pages (the browser phases show them); a real market worker (the stand-in feeds the pilot); live positions at the stop (the synthetic pilot is flat); §14's way back before Start with a new reference experiment; faults that only the models produce; the real clock's windows (`-Rehearsal`); and a product tree with uncommitted changes, which only `-Rehearsal` accepts.
  11. *Size.* The phase's harness code is 431 lines of `Invoke-M7BoundaryRehearsal` and 34 more in `tools/m6/rollout.psm1` (`Get-M7OwnerScripts` and the parameters the deploy model, the owner-script list and the helper leftovers gained), 10 in the profile, 4 in the harness, 1 in the report and 1 in the profile check: about 480 lines against §10's estimate of 400 to 700 for `BoundaryRehearsal`. The owner's script is 394 lines and its model 519; the helper is 213 lines of test code; the engine's check is 118 lines with 233 of tests.
  12. *The independent review* (read-only, of `3f7b9ac`): no blocker, one major point, three minor points and four nits, all taken:
      - *Major:* the check judged only the run it was given, so an older `COMPLETED` forward run could stand for the pilot while the newest one was `STOPPED` and waiting for its final report, or `FAILED`; the install's own guard counts only the open states. Now `NOT_THE_LATEST_FORWARD_RUN` and `FORWARD_EXPERIMENT_STOPPED` (decision 1), with a test.
      - *Minor:* the preflight was called read-only and a refusal said "nothing changed" although the check's reconciliation latches a discrepancy; the wording is now exact, and a stop after a discrepancy says that the account is latched. "Every service one commit, proved" was stronger than the code identity, which covers `app/` only; the commit check of decision 2 joins it (decision 3). A worker whose session outlived `NOLOGIN` went unseen; the check now counts the worker roles' sessions (decision 1).
      - *Nits:* the market worker's name after the builds is compared as text, so an absent name no longer reads as moved; `DOCKER_HOST` and `DOCKER_CONTEXT` are refused; the summary line prints the holdings the check read (dust after the flatten is not refused, because it is not a position); and one failed reading during the phase's fifteen-minute wait is kept and retried rather than ending the phase. The same `DOCKER_HOST` gap in `tools/m7-deploy.ps1` is left as it ran its review; B10 runs it from the owner's shell, which sets neither.

### 17.1 What the two reviews of 2026-10-09 changed

Both reviewers read the first draft and the code without writing anything. Their findings were checked against the cited lines before the text was changed.

| Finding | Change |
|---|---|
| **Blocker.** Authority was checked when the signal row was written, but a deferred decision, a retry or a waiting order could act up to twenty minutes after a Disable | §5.4, §5.5, R10: intake directly before `entries()`; no row while a transient reason holds; the checks repeated before every decision, attempt and fill; an order canceled when the epoch has moved; the loop reads the epoch |
| **Blocker.** Live decision probes could not run in the stated order: they need a policy and a run that exist only after the boundary, and one failing probe latches the runtime | §5.6, §13: two suites; the capability suite passes before the boundary and gives the latency; a probe never writes a submission |
| The signal's validity is read in six places and the summary's query is pinned by a test | §5.4: a validity of one bar in the strategy object; the intent's expiry read by the policy engine's own checks |
| The bar evaluation's row collides with the intake's, and without it the catch-up reads up to 168 bars a step | §5.4: the policy engine does not evaluate bars and has its own scheduling state |
| A reduction has no deadline, can delay a protective exit and can close a position as dust | M7-OD7 recommends leaving it out; §5.4 lists what it would need |
| Reports label an exit by the first sell's origin | §5.4, §8: the trade list labels from the position's terms |
| A tightened stop: a worker of another build would read the old one; fingerprints; no database guard; the risk view; an older quote | §5.3, §5.4, §14 |
| A manifest's definitions must be approved execution definitions | §5.1: a separate recorded field for agent hashes |
| `NOWAIT` raises an error that aborts the step | §5.5, §6: `SKIP LOCKED` |
| A boundary migration would sit behind every default "migrate to newest" | §5.1: one migration and a guarded install command |
| M6-B could not be activated after `0009`; research images share the code identity | R29, §8, §13.1; `ResearchActivationRehearsal` kept |
| The engine cannot read a candidate or its approval; a candidate's stop and hold can lie outside the policy | §5.10: the declaration travels in the submission; a basis outside the policy's sets is refused |
| The boundary ignored that identities lose their login when a run is deactivated | §13.2, §13.3: the existing deactivate and activate tools, in that order |
| The policy document lacked what the contracts' operating policy binds | §5.1 and M7-OD11 |
| Generation 3 against existing guards: a review wait that can only end, one child per template, the queue's six hours, the daily count, `PROMOTION_V1` | §5.6 lists each; the daily count is part of M7-OD3 |
| Segments for source, envelope and candidate changes; the approved protocol fixes its rule | §5.11: `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` |
| An owner's cancel and a workflow's deadline raise no epoch | §5.3, §5.5: withdrawals |
| A review is not stale by time alone | R05, §5.3: the state key |
| Triggers were dropped, not coalesced; no cooldown; a workflow outlived its bar; a source revision reached no decision; cap below cadence | §5.7 |
| No requirement for replay, injection, the entry pause | R26, R27, R28 |
| An assessment with a `NEEDS_EVIDENCE` review qualified; citations across workflows, snapshot and outcome storage were unspecified | §5.2, §5.3, §5.8, §5.9 |
| Decisions were stated one-sidedly | §3.3 rewritten: the pilot's end ends the forward control; where the deferred work goes; what completion leaves open; the order of M6-B and M7-A |
| Completion does wait for the calendar under M7-OD2 (a); a policy value is code; real-model evidence came last; no per-batch review; no load measure after the boundary | §13.1, §15, §9, §10 |
| Smaller corrections of fact | §2 |

Not taken: a policy the owner composes at run time (decision 9 above); a labeled test of reviewer errors and a new escalation artifact (§3.2).

**A narrow second check of the revised text,** by a third reader, judged the two blockers closed in substance and found gaps in how the fixes were written. Changed for it:
- the epoch is held once at the start of a step, before fills, and a step without it also decides no policy signal (§5.4, §5.5);
- a withdrawal is written for every submission whose entry is not yet filled, and it acts at the agent worker's next tick; Disable and the run's Pause are the immediate controls (§5.3, §5.5, R10);
- Disable gets a retry, because it now waits for an engine step too, and B0's test of the hold is a gate before B3 with a stated fallback (§5.5);
- the guards read without locking (§5.3);
- the capability suite's decision probe starts without a policy; the decision suite has its own record; the entry's evidence rule is enforced at the submission, so the positive chain does not depend on a reviewer's verdict (§5.2, §5.6);
- the worker binds on every open state of an experiment and the engine compares its binding in every step (§5.4);
- the boundary's way back is the reference on M7 workers (§14);
- what a changed generation-3 definition costs after `0009` (§13.1, §14); that M7-B needs M6-B's research activation in either order (M7-OD10).

Not re-read by anyone after these last changes: §5.4 to §5.6, §13 and §14 as they now stand.

## 18. Completion evidence

Each batch records its commands, results, measurements against budgets and what was not run here.

### B0 — contracts, measurements and the harness sketch (2026-10-10)

A cloud session on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `1da608f`. It had no Docker daemon. No command addressed the normal application or its database, and `.env`, `secrets/` and `codex_home` were not read. The commands are in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-b0-m7-contracts-and-measurements-2026-10-10).

| Item of §9 | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| Contracts reconciled (§3.4) | Every row of §3.4 written into the contract that disagreed: ROADMAP, DATA_CONTRACTS, AUTONOMY_MATRIX, API_CONTRACTS, AGENT_TOOL_CONTRACTS, OPERATIONS, FRONTEND, EVALUATION, SECURITY, CONFIGURATION and RESEARCH_WORKFLOWS. Eight hold an M7 section marked PROPOSED; ROADMAP, CONFIGURATION and RESEARCH_WORKFLOWS carry the M7 text in place, marked the same way. No override document. The link check found 0 broken links in 93 files | `OBSERVED` |
| §2 read again on `main` | §2.8: every fact holds; four notes | `OBSERVED` |
| Versions (§6) | Unchanged | `OBSERVED` |
| Schema v3 against the limits | Preview within every documented limit (below); v2 unchanged. Through the pinned CLI and the mock | `OBSERVED` in process; the CLI probe `NOT RUN` |
| Epoch hold, gate before B3 | G2 holds. G1 fails: share holders that overlap without a gap starve a waiting increment. One engine alone never did; agent commit checks that overlap did, with or without the engine. Script check on PostgreSQL 16.15; the gate on 17 | `OBSERVED` on 16; 17 `NOT RUN` |
| Step budget as it is | Last measured on an M5 build; none on an M6 build | `OBSERVED` from records; a new measure `NOT RUN` |
| Live step latency (§5.7) | One read-only query drafted for the owner | `NOT RUN`; approved on the owner's delegation of 2026-10-10, for the owner's PC |
| The harness's M7 profile | Sketch and size (below) | `INFERRED` estimate |

**Schema v3 preview** (`tools/m7/b0/schema_preview_v3.py`, built from the code's own schema v2 in the engine's environment). `OBSERVED`: 144 properties, nesting depth 9, 1,878 characters of names and enumeration values, 49 enumeration values, every object strict, 10,488 bytes, SHA-256 `a9eeb963da3ff1e10630d35900a49da919c113d91406fabe1bd45a9e154dedbb`. Against the limits of §6 (5,000; 10; 120,000; 1,000) it is within each. Schema v2 measured in the same run: 111 properties, depth 9, 8,309 bytes, SHA-256 `bb328865…aa05b`, as [INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE](../contracts/INTEGRATION_EVIDENCE.md#m6-b6-agentstep-schema-v2-and-queries--2026-10-05) records. Only `schema_version` and `finish.review.review_type` change from v2; 37 paths are new. The choice fields are strings, as v2's are: the policy's sets reach the model in the job context and the step's validation refuses a value outside them. `INFERRED`: projecting them as enumerations would add a few dozen values, still far inside the limit. Two SYNTHETIC outputs, a `draft_trade_intent` request and a FINISH with a `PortfolioDecision`, are valid against the preview under the engine's validator (`schema_probe_v3.py --self-check`) and under the mock's own validator, run outside its container; the mock's validator refuses an output with an unknown `kind`. That the pinned CLI transmits v3 unchanged and accepts the answers is `NOT RUN`: `tools/m7/b0/Dockerfile` and `schema_probe_v3.py` reuse M6 B0's loopback mock in a container without a network. That a real model accepts v3 is shown first by CP-21.

**Epoch hold** (`tools/m7/b0/epoch_hold_trial.py`). It runs only on a disposable cluster: it refuses a database whose name does not begin `asl_m1_m7_b0` or that holds another table, and a cluster that holds the application's database or identities. `OBSERVED` on a scratch PostgreSQL 16.15 in the cloud session, a script check and not the gate. "One engine" is one connection that holds the epoch through the function for a step of 0.2 s, commits and waits its gap, as one fenced engine does; agent commit checks are separate holders of a plain `FOR KEY SHARE`. Each case gives every increment 5 s (10 s in the stress cases); a case in which a holder failed, or a case meant to overlap that never had two holders at once, would be `INCONCLUSIVE`. None was.

| Case | Observed |
|---|---|
| A locking select by a role without `UPDATE` | Refused `INSUFFICIENT_PRIVILEGE`; the definer function returns the epoch |
| The increment while a step holds the epoch | Waits; returns 0.5 s later, when the step commits |
| G2: a step while an uncommitted increment holds the row | The function returns nothing in 0.001 s; after the commit it returns the new epoch |
| A new step while the increment waits for step A | Gets the epoch in 0.001 s, ahead of the waiting increment; the increment then waits for that step too (granted at 1.21 s) |
| One engine, gap 0.05 s; eight increments | Not starved: each granted within 0.001–0.105 s |
| One engine back to back (no gap) | Not starved: within 0.002–0.106 s; four steps found the row taken and went on without the epoch, as G2 intends |
| One engine and agent checks of 0.05 s every 0.1 s (at most two holders) | Not starved: within 0.002–0.109 s; with the API's 1 s lock and 2 s statement timeouts within 0.001–0.109 s, no timeout |
| One engine whose gaps are bridged by agent checks of 0.12 s every 0.06 s (up to four holders) | **Starved:** three increments, none granted in 5 s; each granted in 0.002 s once the holders stopped |
| The same agent checks with no engine at all, as today and under the fallback | **Starved** the same way |
| Stress: engine holds that overlap one another (as one engine never does) and agent checks of 0.3 s | **Starved** in 10 s, with a statement timeout only. With the API's timeouts: five attempts, five statement timeouts and no lock timeout, because each holder that commits ends one lock wait |

- **What it shows.** A waiting increment does not stop new share holders: each new `FOR KEY SHARE` is granted at once, so the increment gets the row only at an instant when no holder remains. G1 asks that it never be starved, and PostgreSQL 16 gives no such guarantee: the gate as written fails. One engine alone never starved it, back to back included. What starved it were agent commit checks that overlap one another, with or without the engine, and they exist today.
- **Which case decides.** By §5.5's rule a failed G1 means the fallback: a plain read of the epoch at the start of the step and again immediately before COMMIT. `INFERRED` from the cases above: the fallback does not remove the starvation, because agent commits cause it alone; it gives up the guarantee that no fill commits after a revocation has. Keeping the hold or following the rule is put to the owner, since it changes an approved rule. Either way the increment is bounded by a statement timeout, not a lock timeout, and Disable gets the retry of §5.5. How long the product's agent commits hold the row, and how often they overlap, is not measured.
- `INFERRED`: PostgreSQL 17 treats a share lock behind a waiting update the same way; not observed. The gate run on `postgres:17.11-alpine3.24` is `NOT RUN`; its commands are in COMMANDS.

**Step budget as it is.** Declared in [ExecPlan 0011](0011-paper-broker-reference.md) (line 882): publication to step commit p95 at most 750 ms and p99 at most 1.5 s, with 1 CPU and 512 MiB, on a 60-minute synthetic feed of two symbols at one quote a second and two positions. The latest figures recorded: M5 `PilotCoexistence` run `m5-b7j` (2026-10-01) p95 286.8 ms ([ExecPlan 0020](0020-m5-agent-runtime.md#m5-a-isolated-acceptance--final-run-m5-b7j-2026-10-01)); M4 p95 310.85 ms ([ExecPlan 0015](0015-evaluation-fixed-pilot.md), the 60-minute engine row); `PerformanceEngine` of gate `rt-0929f` p95 291 ms, p99 309 ms ([ExecPlan 0017](0017-replay-throughput.md)). No figure exists on an M6 build: the M6 harness has no engine phase of its own, and `ResearchSaturation` of run `m6-b9l` failed for research-side reasons. Per-step timings are not stored, only summaries. Three ways to measure the M6 build, each the owner's call because each is a heavy run beside the pilot:
1. M6's `ResearchSaturation`: loaded, two windows, and it failed for reasons outside the engine.
2. The M3 or M4 `PerformanceEngine`: 3,600 s and no window gate, and the older harness passes the owner's `.env` to its stack because it has no env-file option. Not recommended.
3. **Recommended:** the unloaded `PerformanceEngine7` of the M7 profile, run once on the unchanged M6 build as the baseline before B3. It is harness code and comes with the profile.

**Live step latency.** `tools/m7/b0/probe-latency.sql` reads, read-only and as the API identity, the timings of the live probe calls of 2026-10-02 from the agent tables only: per call the step, the call and the CLI process, with a summary. It reads no prompt, output or argument. It was run once against stub tables with one synthetic row on the scratch PostgreSQL 16 to check that it parses; never against the application. Running it against the normal database needed the owner's approval in chat; the owner left it to the implementer on 2026-10-10, who decided yes for one run ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-10--m7-b0-the-owner-leaves-the-latency-read-and-the-two-small-runs-to-the-implementer-and-approves-the-harness-profile)).

**The harness's M7 profile** (`INFERRED` estimate from a read of the harness at `1da608f`; nothing edited or run). Today's harness is 9,338 lines: the entry script of 613 lines, fourteen modules and three shared libraries.
- **Shape.** The body of `tools/m6-acceptance.ps1` moves verbatim into a dot-sourced file, and what is M6's by content becomes two data files, `profiles/m6.psd1` and `m7.psd1`: run root, project prefix, phases with windows and deadlines, kinds, database templates, revisions, pinned images, test selections, the report's required phases. `tools/m6-acceptance.ps1` keeps its parameters and its 32 phases byte for byte; `tools/m7-acceptance.ps1` has its own 44. A generic handler turns phases that only run pytest selections into definitions.
- **Size.** The refactor and the M7 entry: about 300 to 370 lines of harness code, 430 to 540 lines of profile data, and a check of 80 to 120 lines that M6's profile reproduces today's values. The new M7 phases beyond definitions: about 1,020 to 2,035 more lines, mostly `MixedVersion7` (300–400) and `BoundaryRehearsal` (400–700). Under §10 that new code goes back to the owner before it is written.
- **Three findings that need the owner before any code:**
  1. The heavy lock must stay `.cache/m6/heavy.lock` for both profiles. Derived from the run root it would let an M6-B gate run and an M7 run hold heavy stacks at the same time.
  2. `ResearchActivationRehearsal` cannot pass unchanged on a build whose head is `0009`: `tools/m6-deploy.ps1` asserts `0008` after migrating, `tools/research-activate.ps1:227` refuses any other revision, and the rehearsal's `With0008` revision is used six times in `tools/m6/rollout.psm1`. B1 must reshape it, which §3.1 foresees.
  3. The pre-M7 images that `MixedVersion7` starts from are the M6 release on the normal application. They are not known here and need a read-only observation the owner approves.
- **Also found:** the kept phases' test guards require M6 database names, so the M7 profile keeps M6's templates; the pilot watch hard-codes `.cache/m6` and stops a run with the M6 entry, so it needs a harness parameter; Prepare reserves subnets by scanning its own run root only; merging the refactor while an M6 run is open invalidates that run's tree check (M7-OD10 puts the M6-B gate runs first); nothing tests the harness itself today, so the only full proof that M6 is unchanged is an M6 run on the refactored harness.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** the schema probe through the pinned CLI; the epoch-hold gate on PostgreSQL 17; the latency read on the normal database; a step-budget measure on the M6 build. Each needs Docker on the owner's PC. As [DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-09--m6-b-the-performance-pull-request-is-merged-without-its-job-time-verdict-what-remains-is-done-later-and-does-not-hold-m7) records the reading of 2026-10-09, whether and when a heavy test runs beside the pilot again is the owner's decision; and the latency read is a command against the normal database.

**Left for B5, outside B0:** the name "AgentPolicyVersion" and a "reduce" action survive in `services/agent-runner/prompts/portfolio-coordinator.md:17`, and the name in `docs/templates/EXPERIMENT_REPORT.md:31`.

A cloud session on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq`, on top of B0's three commits (`f77cad9`), while B0's pull request waited for its merge; moved onto `main` after that merge (`7b3e52c`, 2026-10-11) with its tree unchanged. The commands are in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-b1-migration-0009-the-generation-lookup-and-the-harness-profile-2026-10-10).

| Item of §9 | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| Migration `0009` | Ten insert-only `decision_*` tables, their guards, 20 `agent_read` views, the epoch hold and the install function, grants, generation 3's values added to M6's four agent checks, and R25's two guards ([DATA_CONTRACTS](../contracts/DATA_CONTRACTS.md#migration-0009_m7_agent_decisions-b1-2026-10-10)). Upgrade, refused and successful downgrades, and upgrade again on PostgreSQL 16; after the downgrade the schema dump equals that of a database migrated straight to `0008`, grants included | `OBSERVED` on 16; 17 `NOT RUN` |

Live step `VERIFIED`: the 21 M1–M4 relations held `AccessShareLock` only, the migration took 0.26 s, and the downgrade restored the snapshot exactly. Three of the four failures recur after B1 for the same reasons as before; `test_m6_agents.py`'s investigation test, which depends on the clock, passed after B1 and is not counted as fixed. Of the three, `test_m6_schema.py`'s downgrade test fails in a run of many files on one database because an earlier file leaves mock source definitions that M5's check refuses, and passes alone on a fresh database. Whether these three also fail in the harness, where each phase has its own database, is not known here.

**What generation 3 changes in existing guards and code (§5.6), and where each is:**
- `INTENT_REVIEW` as a child purpose, and the lists of purposes, creators, artifact kinds and trigger families: B1, in `0009`'s checks (`test_the_widened_checks_take_generation_3_rows_and_a_review_of_an_intent_is_a_child`, `test_generation_3_values_are_only_added_to_the_m6_checks`). Until B5 consumes them, the M6 scheduler refuses a trigger of a new family (`test_a_generation_3_trigger_is_refused_by_the_m6_scheduler_not_started_as_research`).
- Owner-startable pairs, the review as a tool wait with completions, the finished intent review that resumes its parent, a decision workflow's queue expiry, the daily count of decision work, and profile version 3: B5.
- `PROMOTION_V2`, the promotion's `APPROVED` state and the candidate's `MAIN_APPROVED` with the owner's approval row: B6. `0009`'s submission guard already refuses a candidate basis until a candidate is `MAIN_APPROVED`.
- Found while building and left for B5: `profile.current()` and `consequences()` still name `budgets_v2`, and the response model in `main.py` knows `budgets` and `budgets_v2` only; `check_registry_v2` checks the union of every graph generation's catalog, so its handler and effect lists grow with generation 3; the probe run's call budget becomes the newest generation's that the profile binds.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the engine diff) found no blocker and seven points to fix; each is fixed and tested:
1. A guard read tables by bare name, so a temporary table of the writer's session could stand in for one; every M7 function now has a fixed search path. The M5 and M6 guards have the same pattern and are left as they are (§19).
2. An assessment without `expiry_hours` passed its own-expiry check; it is now refused.
3. An assessment's creation time is its writer's; one dated in the future is refused, the assessment must be an analyst's, and its review must be its own workflow's review child.
4. A draft, submission or pause could come from a workflow that had ended; only a running or waiting workflow may write one.
5. A snapshot's and an outcome's columns were not bound to their hashed documents; they are.
6. The downgrade test did not prove what it restores; it now compares the catalog objects `0009` touches before and after, and reaches each refusal on its own.
7. TESTING claimed more for R25 than the three guarded tables; corrected.

Also from its notes: the upgrade refuses by name two open promotions of one candidate, which the unique index cannot be built over (tested from `0008` in the downgrade test); an M6 trigger family is mapped to its template explicitly; and the views' text says the account-level rows include the pilot's era.

**The independent review of the harness diff** (read-only; PowerShell 7.4.6 in a scratch copy, no Docker, no database) found no blocker, five points to fix and six nits. It checked that the M6 entry's parameters, every phase's `-Describe`, the rendered Compose files and the M6 values are as before. What became of each point:
1. The heavy lock is a file of one checkout, so a run from another clone or worktree took another lock and two heavy stacks could run beside the pilot. A phase other than `Clean` now also refuses while a container of another `asl-mN-` project runs, whichever checkout or harness version started it; the lock's directory gets the unlinked-path check its run root gets. The lock file stays M6's, as approved.
2. `research-activate.ps1` accepts `0009` without a rehearsal of its own. That is R29's requirement, rehearsed by the M7 profile's `ResearchActivationRehearsal`, and the normal application reaches `0009` only by M7's deployment; the script's comment overclaimed and now names the owner-shape guard `0009` adds to the research tables.
3. M6's own runs change with B1 in ways an M6 rerun shows: `m6-deploy.ps1` (one of the files `ResearchActivationRehearsal`, `PilotCoexistence` and `ResearchSaturation` hash) takes `-Revision`, its static comparison skips `decision_` objects and it refuses a rollback from `0009`; the deploy model has 76 expectations; and a phase refuses while another stack runs (point 1). So an M6 rerun on a build with B1 is not a rerun of the code M6-A was accepted on. The deploy model runs first, then the M6 run is the proof (COMMANDS, B1, steps 3 and 4).
4. `check-profiles.ps1` missed changes that matter for isolation. It now also holds each profile's name, stem, run root, milestone and activation, M6's product kinds against the baseline, M7's product kinds, static checks and revisions against M6's, and every phase but the run's own operations as required; the harness itself refuses a profile whose stem or run root is not its own. Fifteen mutations of the two profiles in a scratch copy each failed the check.
5. `Migration7` runs `test_m7_schema.py` without Regression's `api-login` step. Not changed: its `m6` and `m7` fixtures set the logins they use, `asl_api`'s among them, from the target's own secrets, so the step is not needed; the phase says so in a comment.

Nits fixed: the checker evaluates only an expression it has found pure; the pilot watch creates `.cache/m6` before it writes the flag there, and it and [OPERATIONS](../../OPERATIONS.md) name `-Harness`. Left: fixture subnets are drawn at random as in M6 (Docker refuses an overlap, so it fails loudly); `Migration7`'s grant proof compares table and function grants, not column or schema grants or role membership, which `0009` does not change; the report's texts still name M6 in places and say nothing of M7's activation, for B9, whose final acceptance writes M7's report. Two points the review found were there before B1 and stay: cleanup and the secret scan match a run's resources by name prefix (the project label is exact), and no phase refuses to start while `.cache/m6/PILOT-DIFFERENCE.txt` exists.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** every harness phase, `Migration7` and `ResearchActivationRehearsal` among them, because this session has no Docker; PostgreSQL 17; Windows PowerShell 5.1, the profile check on it included; the deploy model with its 76 expectations; and an M6 run on the refactored harness, the only full proof that M6's harness is unchanged. B1's phases are heavy test stacks, so when they run beside the pilot is the owner's decision.

Refused: a strategy that is not an installed policy (`UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`), a policy this build does not hold (`POLICY_NOT_IN_THIS_BUILD`), a fixture-only policy (`FIXTURE_STRATEGY_REFUSED`), and a draft beside a live position of another strategy when either is a policy (`OTHER_STRATEGY_POSITIONS_OPEN`). A draft binds nothing, and a slot naming anything else binds nothing, so its start is refused as in M4. Every step compares its binding in memory after it has locked the run and the positions, and the lease's own transaction does with the run; on a difference it raises `Rebind` and writes nothing, and the worker gives up its lease and binds again at once (a back-off only when a fresh binding is refused again). A policy this build does not hold is refused and recorded as a failed step, never bound as something else. Start refuses a policy run whose protocol or baseline slot is not the policy's, a run beside a live position of another strategy when either is a policy, and a fixture-only policy on the main account | `OBSERVED` in tests |

With one statement added to every step it fails on all twelve digests and counts and on nothing else. The fast path batches steps by time, so only the per-step sequence is compared. The two failures are B0's and B1's (`test_api.py` and `test_database.py` without a migrated database and the API's secret). These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- Two things the tests found and that are fixed: a test that sets `ASL_TEST_ISOLATED=0` kept it through its fixture's teardown, and an earlier file's Alembic run disables the worker's logger, which the worker test now re-enables for itself.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B2 diff; it ran the policy tests without a database and ruff, and no Docker) found no blocker, three points to fix and six nits. It found no way for an engine bound to the wrong strategy to write anything, and no change to what the reference does or sends in a step. What became of each point:
1. *`PolicyDocument` would fail in the harness.* Its test service sets `ASL_REPLAY_ISOLATED=1` too, which also marks a database as isolated, so the install refusal the test expects would not happen. The test now sets both flags off. With `ASL_REPLAY_ISOLATED=1` the old test failed (`DID NOT RAISE`) and the new one passes, and both files pass with the flags the harness sets.
2. *A rule field took any token,* so a document could say that a failed model falls back to the reference or that an entry needs no review, while the code and the approval text say otherwise. The 29 rule fields now restate V1's rules (§17, B2 decision 9); the hash changed.
3. *Only the draft checked a live position of another strategy,* in the API's own transaction. The start now refuses it again under the step's locks (decision 8), in memory, so the reference sends the same statements (`EngineParity7` again after the fix, below). The reviewer found no product path to that state; the risk is inferred.

Nits, all taken: ill-shaped lists raised `TypeError` instead of naming the field, which would have made the approvals view answer 500 for such a row (three cases added); the draft's `except ValueError` turned any error into `POLICY_NOT_IN_THIS_BUILD` (now an explicit check before composing); the approval text left out the approved candidate as an entry basis and said PROPOSED whatever the status (it now states the document's own label and status); a fixture-only policy was not refused for the main account (decision 8); `POLICIES` must keep every installed version or the mode is left with no engine (said where it is defined; a policy replay's binding is decision 10); `protective_exits: EXEMPT` did not say whether their notional counts (renamed). From its notes, also taken: a run whose slot names neither the reference nor an installed policy now binds nothing, so the engine refuses its start as M4's did instead of leaving the mode without an engine (decision 7). Each change has a test that fails without it: switched off in place, the two start refusals fail their tests, and the old slot rule fails three.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `PolicyDocument`, `EngineParity7` and `Regression` as harness phases, because this session has no Docker; PostgreSQL 17; Windows PowerShell 5.1. B2 is light beside B1's phases: two test files and one replay script.
