| Item of §9 | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| Intake | `app/execution/intents.py` and three places in the engine bound to a policy, none in the reference's: *before fills* it answers pause requests, holds the epoch once and checks every accepted entry again; *directly before `entries`* it takes the mode's unanswered submissions in submission order; *after `entries`* it answers the entries those became. Each submission and pause request gets one `decision_events` row, whose ID follows from the request. The engine steps while a request is unanswered and at each expiry of an intent, a waiting signal or an open entry | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| `ENTER` | Authority (expiry, withdrawal, epoch, policy), the choices, the run, then every final reason found with any waiting reason, the limits, the bars, ATR and stop; only then the `ENTRY_SIGNAL` row, which the same step's `entries` decides, sizes and orders as the reference's signal, with the entry's terms written beside the mandate. Sizing takes the requested exposure and the window's remaining notional as two further caps. A waiting reason writes nothing; the next step takes the entry again until its expiry | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| `EXIT` | The existing obligation code with reason `TREND_EXIT`, when the position is the run's, open and filled, and its terms allow an exit; the next step's exit phase sells. A protective stop found in the same step came first: the exit is `POSITION_CLOSING` and there is one sell | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| `CANCEL_ENTRY` | The run's unfilled entry on the symbol: its order through the existing cancel code (`AGENT_CANCELED`), else its waiting signal and submission, else an earlier waiting submission; never a fill (`NOTHING_TO_CANCEL`) | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The entry pause | An agent's pause of a live run opens, cancels the run's unfilled entries and refuses later ones (`ENTRY_PAUSED`) until the owner's resume; protection and exits are untouched. A pause of a run that is not live opens with no effect | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The checks until the fill | Before each decision, attempt and fill of a policy entry: the epoch, a withdrawal, the expiry, a newer bar, the deviation from the cutoff ask, an open pause; the first that fails cancels the order or ends the signal. In a step that could not hold the epoch: no intake, no policy fill, no policy signal decided; all three run in the next step | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| Limits, validity, deviation, state key | As [RISK_POLICY](../contracts/RISK_POLICY.md#agent-driven-action-enforcement) now states: entries, submissions, committed notional and the two cooldowns counted from immutable rows; the intent's own expiry; a bar before activation or superseded; 50 bps from the cutoff ask; `REVIEW_STALE` when the run's state key differs from the submission's | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| Disable | Retried up to three times on a lock or statement timeout, then `503 TRY_AGAIN` with nothing recorded | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The intent stream in replay (R26) | `app/replay/intents.py` records a run's four request tables as JSON rows with their times and the epoch's changes; a replay writes them back as timed inputs (on a disposable database only, with the guards off for their transaction). `decision_fingerprint` hashes the engine's answers and terms. A fixture run with an entry, a stale review, an early exit, a cancellation with nothing to cancel and a pause, replayed from a reset on the fast path with every reused read verified, gives the same portable fingerprint, the same decision fingerprint and the same stream | `VERIFIED` on 16 |
| R19 (`EngineParity7`) | Run again on B3's code, before and after the review's fixes (187 s on the final code): `{"status": "VERIFIED", "failures": []}`: the reference's and the candidate's statements per step, and their fingerprints, are B1's | `VERIFIED` on 16; the phase `NOT RUN` |
| The harness | `IntentEngine` (`test_m7_intents.py` with `test_m7_intent_units.py`) and `IntentRaces` (`test_m7_intent_races.py`) on the engine target, no skip allowed, in `Invoke-M7EnginePhase`; 39 lines added across the profile, its check, the harness body and the module. B4 adds stop tightening to both | The profile check `OBSERVED` on PowerShell 7.4.6; the phases `NOT RUN` |

**Tests on the scratch cluster** (`OBSERVED`; PostgreSQL 16.15, Python 3.13 from the locked environment), on the final code, after the review's fixes:
- `test_m7_intents.py` (24), `test_m7_intent_races.py` (7) and `test_m7_intent_units.py` (11 property and unit tests) with the policy and binding files: `130 passed`, with and without `ASL_REPLAY_ISOLATED=1`. With the binding file's lease taken during a pause (B2), they cover every row of §4.1 marked `IntentEngine` or `IntentRaces` except the three stop rows (B4); of the transient reasons, an unusable quote is tested and a missing daily reference is not on its own. Of the requirements they test the engine's part of R02, R05 (the state key) and R10, and R03, R04, R06, R07 (exit and cancellation), R09, R26 and R28; the dispatcher's parts and the database's review guard are B1's and B5's.
- The M5, M6 and M7 files with `test_generations.py`, one run on a database at `0009`: `2 failed, 863 passed, 51 errors`, which is B2's result plus these 42. The failures and errors are B1's (§18, B2).
- The M1 to M4 files on `0009`: two runs on the final code, each `1 failed, 317 passed` (20 and 21 min), with a different test of the market worker failing each time; B2 had `318 passed`. The market worker loads none of the modules B3 changes (checked through `sys.modules` after importing it and the M2 test modules). In the first run `test_m2_integration.py`'s retention test found its old row still stored: the prune takes the 1,000 oldest rows a call, and the test does not clear market data first. Inferred cause: an earlier M1 to M4 run of this session, stopped part-way because it overlapped another run on the cluster, had left older fixture rows, which later tests cleared; the file passed alone afterwards (`6 passed`) and in the second run. In the second run `test_m2_reference_checks.py::test_rotation_waits_for_a_verified_clock[failed-request]`, which is timed by the wall clock, closed the feed later than it expects; it passed in the first run and three times alone (`2 passed` each). Neither is counted as passed in the run it failed.
- `EngineParity7` on the final code: `VERIFIED` again (187 s).
- Without a database, the whole `tests` directory: `2 failed, 708 passed, 308 skipped, 1 warning, 235 errors`: B2's result plus the 11 B3 unit tests passed and the 31 B3 database tests skipped for want of a prepared isolated database. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- Two things the tests found in their own fixtures and that are fixed: publications must be written in the order they become available, as the replay driver reads them in sequence order; and a timed input gets its own step, so two submissions meant for one step are written by one input.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B3 diff; it ran ruff and the unit tests, not the database tests, and no Docker) found no blocker, three points to fix and ten nits. It found every new statement behind the policy binding, protection running before the intake and never waiting on an agent row or a limit, exactly one answer per request, cancellations that never touch a fill, and the Disable retry free of deadlock. What became of each point:
1. *An agent could end the cancel cooldown at once* by naming an old bar in its cancellation, because the cooldown was keyed on that bar. It now runs from the newest bar that had closed when the engine answered the cancellation, or the intent's bar if later (§17, B3 decision 6). A test whose cancellation names a bar five hours old fails without the fix.
2. *The tests covered less than this section claimed.* Added: `ENTRY_LIMIT_TOTAL` (two accepted entries, both canceled, then a third); the window's notional binding an entry's size and then refusing the next (`NOTIONAL_LIMIT`), with the limit lowered in the engine's memory because V1's 75% cannot bind before the entry limits do; a unit test of both caps; a cancellation that reaches the step that fills (the earlier test, a cancellation after the fill, keeps that name); no policy signal decided while an increment is in flight (a retry waits for the next step that holds the epoch); the six-bar cooldown after a stop suppressed into an exit; and an epoch change in the replayed stream. The Disable test now counts the attempts (two or more, then exactly three), reports a failure in its holding thread and checks that the hold began; it holds the epoch with the step's own statement on a connection of its own, as its docstring now says.
3. *The stream mixed two clocks* in a fixture: the tests' rows carried simulated times and an epoch change the database's. The desk now writes an epoch change on its own clock, the R26 test starts with empty agent records as a replay database does, and the module says that rows and epoch changes must be on one clock (decision 13). In a forward run the guards set both from the database's clock.

Nits taken: rows of one time are ordered by ID too; an expired entry keeps its waiting reasons in `detail.waiting` (R06, decision 15); an earlier step's rejected order is answered `REJECTED` (inferred unreachable, now explicit); a bar past its validity is answered `BAR_SUPERSEDED` (decision 17); the deviation is computed in the engine's decimal context and the re-entry bar in UTC; the cutoff quote is read only once the watermark has reached it (decision 16); §5.4's bar read is recorded as built (decision 14). Each changed answer has a test that fails without it: switched off in place, each time restored from a copy and compared, the four tests fail. Left as notes: the poll compares `max(seq)`, so a lower sequence committed later is seen one trigger late, as the owner marker is; pause requests are answered without the epoch check, which only lowers exposure; an unanswered request forces a step on every trigger, a cost for `PerformanceEngine7` (B9) to measure; exits and cancellations count toward the submissions but are never refused for them (decision 6).

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `IntentEngine`, `IntentRaces`, `EngineParity7` and `Regression` as harness phases, because this session has no Docker; PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; Windows PowerShell 5.1; the step budget on an M6 or M7 build. B3's phases are light: three test files.

### After B3 — the profile check on a Windows checkout (2026-10-11)

The session on the owner's PC ran B1's step 1 before run `m7-b3a` (`OBSERVED` there, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100): `check-profiles.ps1 -Baseline 7b3e52c` exited 1 with one line, "the M6 entry's parameters: expected ..., found ...", the two blocks reading the same. The check compared the entry's parameter block at the baseline, which `git show` gives as lines it joins with LF, with the same block read from the checkout, which holds CRLF on Windows. On Linux the checkout holds LF, so B1 to B3 saw `VERIFIED`. No harness phase calls the check. The checkout's text is now read with CRLF turned into LF before it is parsed; nothing else compares text with the baseline (the other reads take phase names and revisions).

`OBSERVED` in a cloud session, PowerShell 7.4.6, in a scratch worktree whose PowerShell files were converted to CRLF: before the fix the same single `FAILED` line; after it `VERIFIED: ... (44 phases, 13 not built)`, as on the LF checkout; with one parameter of `m6-acceptance.ps1` renamed in the CRLF worktree it still prints `FAILED: the M6 entry's parameters`. `NOT RUN`: the check on Windows PowerShell 5.1 after the fix.

### Run `m7-b3a` on the owner's PC: B1 to B3's phases, and the rehearsal's revision (2026-10-11)

The session on the owner's PC, on the owner's `do it` of 15:46 UTC and with the pilot watch the owner started at 16:25 UTC ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-11--the-owner-starts-the-pilot-watch-for-the-m7-run-on-the-pc)), ran the phases from B3's code (`08b94b3`, worktree `.claude\worktrees\m7-b0`) with Docker, PostgreSQL 17.11 and Windows PowerShell 5.1, each asked for once, inside HH:07 to HH:58 UTC. What follows is its report, `OBSERVED` there; the result files stay on the PC under the worktree's `.cache\m7\m7-b3a\` and `.cache\m7\m7-b3a-seq.log`. Before it (15:54 UTC, no Docker) it ran B1's step 3, the deploy model: `tools\m6\deploy-model.ps1` exit 0, 17 scenarios, 76 of 76 expectations, which §18 B1 listed as `NOT RUN`.

| Phase | Result |
|---|---|
| `Build` (16:34:06 to 16:35:03) | Images built; 12 static tests passed (189 deselected); lint `All checks passed!` |
| `EngineParity7` (16:35:14 to 16:39:21) | `{"status": "VERIFIED", "failures": []}` against B1's pinned values |
| `Migration7` (16:39:34 to 16:41:54) | The schema and role tests `32 passed` in 66 s; `0009` on a populated copy at `0008`, static and live, `VERIFIED`, with its bounded locks and the refused and inert downgrades |
| `PolicyDocument` (16:42:06 to 16:42:45) | `88 passed` in 27 s |
| `IntentRaces` (16:42:59 to 16:43:31) | `7 passed` in 19 s |
| `IntentEngine` (16:43:44 to 16:44:49) | `35 passed` in 53 s |
| `Regression` (17:07:06 to 17:24:38) | M3 and M4 on the fast path `186 passed` in 1,015 s; M0 to M2 `77 passed` in 22 s. No market-worker test failed, unlike the two cloud runs of B3 |
| `ResearchActivationRehearsal` (17:24:56 to 17:45:17) | `FAILED`, exit 1: `ResearchActivationRehearsal: the continued deployment did not reach 0007; the refused rollback left revision 0009_m7_agent_decisions` (41 commands recorded) |

The seven passed on their first attempt, with no test skipped (a skip fails these phases), no failed or superseded record and the tree's digest the same at the start and end of every attempt. The watch ended by itself at 17:45:28 UTC when the run was over, with no difference file; every reading from 16:30 to 17:40 UTC held lease epoch 6, no `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` row and no collection gap. Its note, not judged there: the market worker recorded `RETENTION_FAILED` twice and `RETENTION_RECOVERED` once from 16:25 UTC. The run's 15 stopped containers, volumes and images were kept for the diagnosis; its `Clean` phase has not run.

**Why the rehearsal failed: a harness check, not the deployment** (found in the cloud session from the code; `INFERRED` until the record's steps are read on the PC). B1 gave `Invoke-M6ActivationRehearsal` (`tools/m6/rollout.psm1`) a parameter `$Revision`, the schema the build migrates to (`0009_m7_agent_decisions` on the M7 build), and compared three results against it. The function already assigned a local `$revision` (the revision after the rollback, `0007_m5_agent_runtime`). PowerShell names ignore case, so from step 2 on `$Revision` held `0007`, and the two later checks compared correct results with it: the continued deployment, which reached `0009`, "did not reach 0007", and the refused rollback, which left `0009` as it should, "left revision 0009". The deployment itself was told `-Revision 0009` before the assignment, so it ran as intended. The same holds on the M6 profile with `0008`: its rehearsal has not run since B1. `OBSERVED` on PowerShell 7.4.6: a function with `param([string]$Revision = "0009_m7_agent_decisions")` that assigns `$revision = "0007_m5_agent_runtime"` prints `0007_m5_agent_runtime` for `$Revision`.

The fix renames the local to `$rolledBack`, with a comment; nothing else changes. A scan of every function and script parameter block under `tools/` (PowerShell's parser, `OBSERVED` on 7.4.6) lists 31 assignments to a parameter's name, most of them a parameter normalizing its own value; read one by one, no other overwrites a parameter under another case that is read afterwards as the original: `Initialize-M6ProductTarget`'s `$root`, the two model scripts' `$work` and `m6-b0-probe.ps1`'s `$value` reassign their parameter on purpose, and `m6-deploy.ps1` copies `$Revision` into `$with0008` before it assigns `$revision` and reads only the copy. The module parses and stays ASCII; the profile check prints `VERIFIED` (44 phases, 13 not built). `NOT RUN`: the rehearsal again, which needs a fresh run ID (a failed record stays in its run) and the watch; the reading of the failed record's `steps` on the PC, which should show `0009` at both places; `m7-b3a`'s `Clean`; the profile check on Windows PowerShell 5.1.

### B4 — stop tightening, the stop in force and the trade list's exit label (2026-10-11)

The cloud session of B0 to B3, on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `dbe17b1` (after B3, the profile check's fix and the token-refresh record). It had no Docker daemon. It ran database tests on four scratch PostgreSQL 16.15 clusters it created and removed, each holding only `asl_m1_` databases and one test run at a time. No command of this session addressed the normal application or its database, and `.env`, `secrets/` and `codex_home` were not read. The commands are in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-b4-stop-tightening-the-stop-in-force-and-the-trade-lists-exit-label-2026-10-11).

**On the owner's PC meanwhile,** run `m7-b3a` ran B1 to B3's phases on B3's build (the section before this one). B4's phases have not run there.

| Item of §9 | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| `TIGHTEN_STOP` | Taken in the intake as the other actions are: authority and choices first, then the position, its terms, the quote and the template (§17, B4 decisions 1 to 5). An accepted tightening is one `decision_stop_updates` row, version one above the last, with the quote it was checked against and the step's decision time, and an `ACCEPTED` answer with effect `STOP_TIGHTENED` and the same `stop_update_id`; two in one step are versions 1 and 2. The mandate and its terms are unchanged | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The stop in force | The last update's stop, else the mandate's (`intents.stop_at`). Protection holds a quote against the stop in force at the quote's source time, so a quote earlier than an update, even received late, is held against the stop before it; the poll's check of a quote that is stale when handled does the same, so such a quote forces a step only if it crosses the stop of its own time. A protective exit of an updated stop records `stop` and `mandate_stop` | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| Planned risk | `GET /v1/risk`'s open risk is measured to the stop in force (zero once the stop reaches the cost); so are the breach check's and sizing's, which read the loaded positions (a test added after the review) | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| A stop crossing and a tightening in one step | Protection runs first; the tightening is `POSITION_CLOSING` and writes nothing | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The trade list's exit label | `AGENT_EXIT` for a `TREND_EXIT` sell when the mandate's exit rule is the agent's; the evaluation loader reads the rule from `position_mandates` | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| R26 | The replayed stream now holds a tightening; the replay gives the same answers, the same update and the same decision fingerprint, which hashes the stop updates (decision 11) | `VERIFIED` on 16 |
| R19 (`EngineParity7`) | On B4's code, before and after the review's fixes (213 s on the final code): `{"status": "VERIFIED", "failures": []}`: the reference's statements per step and its fingerprints are B1's. The reference reads its mandates' stops as before; `stop_at` is pure Python | `VERIFIED` on 16; the phase `NOT RUN` |
| The harness | B4 changes no M7 phase: `IntentEngine` and `IntentRaces` run the same three files, which now hold B4's tests. The rehearsal's fix is in the section before this one | `NOT RUN` |

**Tests on the scratch clusters** (`OBSERVED`; PostgreSQL 16.15, Python 3.13 from the locked environment), on the final code:
- `test_m7_intents.py` (31), `test_m7_intent_races.py` (13) and `test_m7_intent_units.py` (16) with the policy and binding files: `148 passed`, with and without `ASL_REPLAY_ISOLATED=1` (115 s and 117 s), B3's 130 and B4's 18. They cover the three stop rows of §4.1 (a stop tightened during a fill, a stop at or above the bid, a quote older than the update) and test the engine's part of R08 (tightening) besides B3's; the dispatcher's part is B5's.
- The M5, M6 and M7 files with `test_generations.py`, one run on a database at `0009`: `2 failed, 881 passed, 51 errors` in 10 min, B3's result plus B4's 18 tests; before the review's fixes `2 failed, 876 passed, 51 errors`, without the last races test. The failures and errors are B1's (§18, B2).
- The M1 to M4 files on `0009`: `318 passed` (21 min) on the final code, and `318 passed` (20 min) before the review's fixes, B2's result; neither run had the market-worker failures of B3's runs.
- Without a database, the whole `tests` directory: `2 failed, 713 passed, 321 skipped, 1 warning, 235 errors`: B3's result plus the 5 B4 unit tests passed and the 13 B4 database tests skipped for want of a prepared isolated database. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- **Each change matters.** Switched off in place, each time restored from a copy and compared (`OBSERVED`): protection reading the latest stop whatever the quote's time, protection reading the mandate's stop, the poll's stale check reading the latest stop, the summary or the load without the updates, the risk view reading the mandate's stop, the trade list labeling by origin only (two tests), no check of the template against the terms, and no check against the bid: the test written for each fails. Without the first-fill check the tests still pass: a position without a first fill is `PENDING_ENTRY`, which the state check refuses first, and the database guard refuses it too; the check stays as a guard of the engine's own. The review's fixes added six more (below).

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B4 diff under `services/`; it ran ruff and the unit tests, not the database tests, and no Docker) found no blocker, one gap in the tests and six nits. It found the reference's statements and protective evidence unchanged, the engine's checks in agreement with `asl_m7_stop_guard` (two tightenings in one step see each other), protection before the intake, the replay cache cloned before the stops are attached and cleared by the insert, the evaluation identity reading only a column it was granted, and the labels and fingerprint as decided. Each point was taken, and each change that alters behavior has a test that fails without it:
1. *Planned risk with the stop in force had no test.* Added: BTC tightened to breakeven, then a SOL entry; every planned-risk computation of that step (sizing and the breach check) holds BTC at the stop in force, and the SOL entry's `open_risk_before` is below the distance to the mandate's stop alone.
2. *The bid's scale.* `decision_stop_updates.quote_bid` is `numeric(38,8)` and the table requires the stop below it, while a quote's bid is unbounded `numeric`: a stop between the stored and the received bid would have passed the engine and failed the table's check, an error in every step until the intent expired. The engine now compares with the bid as stored (`intents.stored_bid`, half up, which is PostgreSQL's rounding for a positive value: `OBSERVED` `100.123456785::numeric(38,8)` is `100.12345679`). A test with a bid of nine decimals just above the breakeven stop gets `STOP_ALREADY_CROSSED`.
3. *The stop in force was computed for every position on any trigger,* before the symbol, quality and first-fill checks; it is now the last term of the same condition, so the reference's order of evaluation is as before.
4. *The update row carried the position's run,* so the guard's comparison with the terms' run could not refuse another run's tightening; it now carries the submission's. A test of a stopped run's submission naming this run's position gets `POSITION_MISMATCH`.
5. *`BREAKEVEN` recorded no inputs;* its answer now records the cost basis and quantity.
6. *A `STOP_TEMPLATE` with no visible final bar was refused* (`STOP_INVALID`); it now waits, and its expiry records `BAR_NOT_VISIBLE` (with `DATA_STALE` when the quote was unusable too). The test stands in for bars lagging the quotes by making the engine's read of the latest final bar return none.
7. *One more statement in every policy step* (R24): the stop updates are now read only when the step has a live position or the summary a stop.

Switched off again in place, sixteen changes in all, each restored from a copy and compared (`OBSERVED`): fifteen make their tests fail; the first-fill check is the one that does not, as above.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `IntentEngine`, `IntentRaces`, `EngineParity7` and `Regression` as harness phases on B4's build, because this session has no Docker; PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; the step budget on an M6 or M7 build.

- Without a database, the whole `tests` directory: `2 failed, 717 passed, 321 skipped, 1 warning, 276 errors` in 49 s: B4's result plus B5's four tests that need no database, passed, and its 41 database cases in error for want of a database (`failed to resolve host 'postgres'`). The two failures are B0's and B1's. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- **Each change matters.** In a copy of the tree, 25 checks were switched off one at a time, each file restored and compared afterwards, and the tests written for each were run: 24 fail. The one that does not is the review's hash in `submit_trade_intent`: a review names its draft by ID, a draft is immutable, so a review of the right draft with another hash cannot be written; the check stays, as does the database guard's. In the first round, of sixteen, four passed their tests: the review's hash; a maximum hold outside the set and one open decision once the cooldown has passed, which now have tests that fail without them; and a foreign position, whose run had selected the wrong test and which fails the right one.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B5 diff; it ran ruff, the unit tests and the profile check, and could not run the B5 database tests because its database was at the first version of `0009`) found no blocker, one major point, three minor points and five nits. Each was taken; each change that alters behavior has a test that fails without it (the round above):
1. *An accepted entry without a fill was not withdrawn when its decision ended* (major), contrary to §4.1 and R10. Now it is, and the engine's check until the fill cancels its order or signal; a filled entry or a refusal is not (§17, B5 decision 8). The test runs the three cases.
2. *A decision was shown outcome reviews of any run in its mode.* Now only its own run's.
3. *The kept trigger was the last written, not the newest event.* Now by event time.
4. *A superseded position event was settled before the start was decided,* so a start held by one open decision lost its exemption from the cooldown. The others are now superseded only when the decision starts.
5. *A probe's drafts appeared among a decision's recent intents* (the suite runs on the policy's run). Now left out.
6. *The bound could shorten open orders.* Now only history; a snapshot that does not fit otherwise starts no decision.
7. *The coordinator's prompt promised a "filled" answer;* the decision gets the engine's first answer. The prompt's sentence is changed, so its row in `0009`, the prompts' hash the policy binds and the policy's hash changed (`2da61a78…`, before `be7f4b01…`); nothing was installed anywhere.
8. *The documents said one deadline bounds the sum of a decision's waits;* each wait has its own bound and the sum follows from them (§17, B5 decision 2).
9. *A decision could name a draft under another action.* Now refused (`SCOPE_DENIED`).

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** the five phases on the owner's PC, with the actual schema v3 through the pinned CLI; `EngineParity7` and PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; `test_m1_account.py`; the live probes CP-21 to CP-27, which are the owner's in M7-B.

### B6a — promotion approval and the candidate basis (2026-10-12)

The cloud session of B0 to B5, on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `bf4e7bc` (after B5). It had no Docker daemon. It ran database tests on two scratch PostgreSQL 16 clusters it created and removed, each holding only `asl_m1_` databases. No command of this session addressed the normal application or its database, and `.env`, `secrets/` and `codex_home` were not read. No model was called: every workflow below is an `OFFLINE_TEST` one served by the recorded provider with a scripted model. B6 is two pull requests (§17, B6a decision 1); this is the first. The commands are in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-b6a-promotion-approval-and-the-candidate-basis-2026-10-12).

| Item of §9 | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| Definitions | `PROMOTION_V2` in generation 3 (template, `request_promotion` granted to `COORDINATOR`, its budget, `coordinator-promotion.v1.md`); the two prompts B5 wrote gain the candidate basis. `0009`'s frozen rows and the policy's hash follow (`cf251933…`, §17, B6a decision 12); nothing was installed anywhere | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| Promotion approval | SYSTEM asks with `PROMOTION_V2` once the policy is installed, with the scope, policy and exit values the server writes; only the owner approves, rejects or revokes, by hash, through the existing `/v1/agent-approvals` record; the worker applies the latest record. `0009`'s three new guard functions on `research_promotions`, `research_candidate_events` and `agent_approvals` repeat every rule (§17, B6a decisions 2 to 6, 19 and 20) | `OBSERVED` in tests; the owner's routes for starting and reading are B7's |
| The candidate basis | A decision is shown up to four approved candidates with their signals; an `ENTER` may name one, taking its stop multiple and hold; the submission binds the approval, which the guard checks against the owner's latest record (§17, B6a decisions 7 to 9) | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The engine | Reads no research table: it parses the submission's declaration, checks the ATR period, stop multiple, hold and symbol, and takes the entry only on the candidate's own `ENTRY_SIGNAL` on the decision bar (§17, B6a decisions 10 and 17). The reference path is untouched | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| A research suspension | An entry on a candidate research suspends after it was submitted is withdrawn at the worker's next pass (`UNAPPROVED`, §17, B6a decision 18) | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| The replay | The intent stream carries the candidate and approval rows the position's terms reference (§17, B6a decision 14) | `OBSERVED` in tests |
| R19 | The engine changed only on the policy path (a candidate entry's declaration, its checks and its signal): `python -m tests.m7_engine`: `{"status": "VERIFIED", "failures": []}`, exit 0, in 179 s on the final code | `VERIFIED` on 16; `EngineParity7` `NOT RUN` |
| Harness | `PromotionApproval` runs `tests/test_m7_promotions.py` on the engine target after its `api-login` step, through B2's `Invoke-M7EnginePhase`: one name in the harness body's dispatch list, the file, step and scope in the function's three maps (4 changed lines besides comments), one row of profile data in `m7.psd1` and one name in the profile check's list of built phases. No new function. The profile check: `VERIFIED` on PowerShell 7.4.6, 44 phases, 7 not built | Phase `NOT RUN` |

**Tests on the scratch clusters** (`OBSERVED`; PostgreSQL 16, Python 3.13 from the locked environment), on the final code, on databases migrated again after the review's fixes changed `0009`:
- `test_m7_promotions.py`, 11 tests, as the phase runs it (`-m promotion_approval`): `11 passed` in 38 s, none skipped. They cover R17, R26 for an entry on a candidate, and R18 through the engine's fill. Before the review: 7 tests, `7 passed`.
- The M5, M6 and M7 files with `test_generations.py`, one run at `0009`: `2 failed, 937 passed, 51 errors` in 11 min, B5's result plus B6a's eleven tests. The failures and errors are B1's (§18, B2), the same two tests and the same four files. A run on the code before the review also failed `test_m6_agents.py`'s `test_an_investigation_refreshes_a_source_waits_without_the_slot_and_cites_what_it_was_given` at its first fetch (`assert len(fetcher.tick()["new"]) == 1`), before any agent code runs; B6a changes no source or fetcher code, and the test passed in the final run. It is recorded, not investigated.
- Every other test file, M0 to M4 (`test_api.py`, `test_database.py`, `test_m1_*` to `test_m4_*`), on the final code: `324 passed, 13 errors` in 18 min, as B5's run. The 13 errors are `test_m1_account.py`, whose fixture connects to the host `postgres` by name, the Compose service, which this session has not. They are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- Without a database, the whole `tests` directory: `2 failed, 717 passed, 323 skipped, 1 warning, 285 errors` in 49 s: B5's result plus B6a's eleven database tests, two skipped (`M3 integration requires an isolated asl_m1_ disposable database`) and nine in error for want of a database (`failed to resolve host 'postgres'`). The two failures are B0's and B1's. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- **Each change matters.** In a copy of the tree, 28 checks were switched off one at a time (19 in Python, and 9 in `0009`'s guards, each on a database migrated with the switched-off revision), each file restored and compared afterwards, and the tests written for each were run: 27 fail, each on the assertion written for it or on the next layer's refusal (the guard, where the agent's own check was switched off). The one that does not is the probe's empty list of candidates: a probe's submission is refused in any case (`DispatcherAuth7`), so the list is hygiene, not authority. Before the review, 17 of 18 failed, the same one passing.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B6a diff at `2ee3384`; it ran ruff, the unit tests and the profile check's constructs, and no database test) found no blocker, one major point, two minor points and three nits. Each was taken; each change that alters behavior has a test that fails without it (the round above):
1. *A research suspension did not reach an entry already submitted* (major), contrary to R17's "only while the candidate is approved": a shadow's integrity failure suspends the candidate as `asl_research` and raises no epoch, and the engine reads no research table, so an unanswered or unfilled entry stayed open until its expiry. Now the worker withdraws it at its next pass (`UNAPPROVED`), which the withdrawal guard admits only once the candidate has left `MAIN_APPROVED` (§17, B6a decision 18). The time until that pass remains (§19).
2. *The owner's rejection of a pending consideration request could be lost* when SYSTEM recorded an entry-basis request over it before applying it, or while the owner's record waited for SYSTEM's transaction: the request ended `WITHDRAWN` and no pass applies a decision on a withdrawn request. Now neither the agent's check nor the guard records a request over a decided one, `PENDING` -> `WITHDRAWN` is refused after a `REJECT`, and the approval guard reads the request's state after the candidate's lock (§17, B6a decision 19). Two tests, one of them a race on two connections.
3. *`CANDIDATE_SIGNAL_ABSENT` did not match the documents:* the engine refused a bar on which the candidate's exit signal held with its entry condition, which the documents did not say. The behavior stays and is now stated everywhere (§17, B6a decision 17), with a test of that bar; the two coordinator prompts changed with it, and so did the policy's hash.
4. *A revocation of an approval withdrawn unapplied was accepted* and would only have raised the epoch. Now refused by the API and the guard.
5. *SYSTEM's start of `PROMOTION_V2` read a window of fifty waiting candidates,* which out-of-set candidates, waiting without a request for ever, could fill. Now it reads past them.
6. *The harness edits are code, not only profile data* (plan §10). They are the four lines above, in the form B2 and B3 gave theirs; they are recorded here, as B2's were.
Two notes needed no change: the replay's writer refuses a differing referenced row by ID, and a clash on another unique column of `agent_approvals` stops the replay with the database's own error instead; and the stream writes every row with foreign keys off, as it always has, so an intent drafted before its submission may precede the candidate row it names.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `PromotionApproval` on the owner's PC; `EngineParity7` and PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; `test_m1_account.py`; the owner's start of `PROMOTION_V2` and the promotions read model, which are B7's (§19); a live promotion, which needs the policy installed at the boundary.

The two failures are B0's and B1's. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- **`Migration7`'s steps outside Docker** (on the code at `8680edb`; `0009` and the helpers have not changed since): a recovery database migrated to `0007`, seeded with `tests.m6_migration seed-legacy`, `seed_m4` and the pilot state, migrated to `0008` and seeded with `seed-catalog`, then `tests.m7_migration static` on a restored copy and `live` on it, as the phase's steps run them. Both `VERIFIED`: the static step's downgrade is refused (`M7 agent history exists; downgrade refused; fix forward`); the live step migrated in 0.23 s after five attempts in 2.17 s and two in 0.48 s, held only `AccessShareLock` on its 21 protected relations, restored exactly, and found 20 declared changes. Before the repair it failed on the seed rows, on `agent_approvals`' triggers and on `research_promotions`' state check (decision 10). The database was seeded with this build's code, not with the M6 image the phase uses, and PostgreSQL was 16, not 17.
- **Each change matters.** In a copy of the tree, 33 checks were switched off one at a time (26 in Python, and 7 in `0009`'s views and grants, each on a database migrated with the switched-off revision), each file restored and compared afterwards, and the tests written for each were run. 30 failed at once, each on the assertion written for it, or, for an hour paired across a gap, in the comparison's own arithmetic. Three did not, each a gap in the tests, now closed: the main account's policy engine kept its policy with no run (no test drove that engine there), a variant at `PROPOSED` passed (the test asked only for a status outside the list), and a decision probe whose snapshot binds the run was counted for it (no test had one). With the added tests, all three fail too.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B6b diff at `ee550a6`; it ran ruff and `measures.compute` and `policies.problems` on synthetic input, and no database test) found no blocker, two minor points and six nits. Each was taken; each change that alters behavior has a test that fails without it (the round above):
1. *The evaluation identity saw, and a report copied, a tool name a model chose* (minor): the tool-call view showed `agent_tool_calls.tool_name`, which holds the model's requested name even when the dispatcher refused it as `UNKNOWN_TOOL`, and the measures let any name of the code's shape through. Now the view shows none for a refused name and the measures count only the catalog's names (§17, B6b decision 3), tested with a made-up name through B5's loop.
2. *A policy replay kept its binding over a live position of the reference* (minor), contrary to its own docstring: the reference's position names no policy, so the binding did not see it, and the policy engine would have left its trend exit unevaluated. Now any live position of another strategy makes it give way (decision 6), tested in the function and in a fixture engine.
3. *Segments were judged by the order of answers,* so an entry that waited made two spurious segments. Now by submission order (decision 2), tested with an entry that waits.
4. *The engine's own qualified codes counted as `UNRECOGNIZED`.* Now by the code before the colon (decision 4).
5. *A policy document whose ID is not a string raised instead of being invalid.* Now `invalid:id`.
6. *`Migration7` did not snapshot `evaluation_read`.* Now it does.
7. *The comparison admitted research replays,* which name no fixture although the labels say synthetic fixture, *rested "one fixture" on the generator's word, and did not check a run's variant against its report.* Now fixture runs only, the variant is checked (`VARIANT_MISMATCH`), and the documents say that the fixture's hash is what the generator was given (decision 7).
8. *A harness comment said the evidence file holds the documents;* it holds their hashes and paired statistics.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `Comparison` and `Migration7` on the owner's PC (the latter again, since B6b changed its helpers); `EngineParity7` and PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; `test_m1_account.py`; the forward control (B6c); a comparison of real agent decisions (M7-B and later); showing any of this to the owner (B7, B8).

The cloud session of B0 to B6b, on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `7b661a0` (after B6b). It had no Docker daemon. - M6's bridge-side files (`test_m6_shadows.py`, `test_m6_bridge.py`, `test_m6_runner.py`, `test_m6_staging.py`) in the bridge environment, on the final code: `62 passed, 77 warnings` in 323 s.
- Every other test file, M0 to M4, on the final code: `324 passed, 1 warning, 13 errors` in 23 min, as B6b's run. The 13 errors are `test_m1_account.py`, which needs the host `postgres`; they are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- Without a database, the whole `tests` directory, on the final code: `2 failed, 724 passed, 329 skipped, 1 warning, 305 errors` in 57 s: B6b's result plus B6c's one test without a database, and 17 of its tests in error for want of one (`Required M7 tests need a prepared isolated asl_m1_ database at migration 0009`, `Required B4 tests need the prepared isolated catalog and research fixtures`). The two failures are B0's and B1's. These are `NOT RUN`, not passed.
- **`Migration7`'s steps outside Docker,** on the final `0009`, as in B6b: both `VERIFIED`. The static step's downgrade is refused (`M7 agent history exists; downgrade refused; fix forward`); the live step migrated in 0.27 s at the first attempt, held nothing stronger than `AccessShareLock` on its 21 protected relations, restored exactly, and found 27 declared changes to M5 and M6 relations: B6b's 20, and B6c's seven on `research_jobs` and `research_results` (two columns of the jobs and one of the results made nullable, the `CONTROL` kind, the subject check and two guards). The database was seeded with this build's code, not with the M6 image the phase uses, and PostgreSQL was 16, not 17.
- **Each check matters.** In a copy of the tree, 73 checks were switched off one at a time (33 in `control_replay.py`, 25 elsewhere in the research code and the agent's experiment tool, and 15 in `0009`'s guard, grants and checks, each of the last on a database migrated with the switched-off revision), each file restored and compared afterwards, and the tests written for each were run. 70 failed at once, each on its own assertion or the guard's refusal. One switch was malformed and two did not fail, each a gap in the tests, now closed: a period after its run had stopped inside the last one started (no test had a run stop inside a period), and a period started on a store due for rotation (no test had one). With the added tests and the switch repaired, all three fail too, two of them on the guard's own refusal.
- ruff: `All checks passed!`; the OpenAPI check: `FastAPI OpenAPI schema matches`; the link check: 100 files, `0 broken link(s)`, with the three known warnings of plan 0003.

**The independent review** (read-only, of the B6c diff at `4f2991b` and `3d29d60`; it ran ruff and pure checks of the control identity, the payload check, the labels and the period arithmetic, and no database test) found no blocker, one major point, three minor points and three nits. Each was taken or answered (§17, B6c decision 10); each change that alters behavior has a test that fails without it (the round above):
1. *A period that starts late could replay part of its window, or none of it, and say otherwise* (major). A store holds only what main still kept when its staging began, and nothing recorded what came before. Now a period is replayed only from a store that covers its freeze, ends `INCOMPLETE` (`STORE_STARTS_AFTER_ITS_FREEZE`) otherwise, and each mark records `store_covers_from`; tested on a store whose coverage starts after a freeze.
2. *The period check depended on the session time zone* (minor): `interval '21 days'` is 503 or 505 hours across a change of daylight saving. Now 504 hours, in the check and in the code; tested in `Europe/Budapest`.
3. *The owner's dataset list showed control work* (minor), as shadow datasets. Now it leaves out a window sealed for the control replay alone; tested with and without `purpose=SHADOW`.
4. *A period whose advances kept failing never ended* (minor, inferred), and kept its store. Now it ends `NOT_FINISHED_IN_TIME` 7 days after its window's end, or after its own start when that is later, unless its own work is with the runner; tested. The same review corrected §19's line on M6's quiet tail.
5. *The late-result scan asked the store about control work canceled before it was sent* (nit). Now only about work that was sent; tested.
6. *`trial()` skipped any job without a hypothesis* (nit). Now it skips by the job's kind.
7. *A later comparison could mix cost models* (nit, inferred). Answered in decision 10: the period records `H1`; M7 compares nothing.

**`NOT RUN`, each with its command in COMMANDS:** `Comparison`, `ShadowSchedule` and `Migration7` on the owner's PC, and PostgreSQL 17 for these tests; the profile check on Windows PowerShell 5.1; `test_m1_account.py`; a control period over a real agent-driven run (M7-B, once the owner activates one); showing periods or marks to the owner (not in M7).

**What ran here** (`OBSERVED`; PostgreSQL 16, Python 3.13 from the locked environment, the production web build on Node, the preinstalled Chromium with Playwright 1.63.0):
- **The browser spec** through `http://web:4318`, each test at 390 and 1440 px, on the final build after the review's fixes and a fresh seed: `@decisions` `11 passed` (30.3 s); `@unavailable` on a database at `0008` `1 passed`; `@error` with the API stopped `1 passed`. Earlier runs had failed four ways, each fixed and rerun: after the fixes, the revocation test's new check found that it had read the "If revoked:" label alone, so it had compared the dialog with an empty text, which any dialog contains; it now reads the whole paragraph (the policy's text, then the dialog's, both checked). Before the review: a definition's ID and the predecessor's values clipped at 390 px on `/approvals` (§17, B8b decision 11); a draft refused `ACCOUNT_NOT_INITIALIZED`, since the fixture had no account (decision 12); and nothing on the experiment's page named its strategy (decision 5).

### The start dialog on a phone (2026-10-17)

The cloud session of B0 to B9d, on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `2508b18`, after run `m7-b9a`'s `BrowserResearch` failed on the PC (OBSERVED there, 23:40 UTC): M6's test "agents and system: four templates, ..." (`e2e/m6.spec.ts:705`) at its check "the dialog fits a 390 by 700 viewport". Its page snapshot shows the start dialog with seven templates (two disabled) and the note that the operating policy is not installed. The same test passed in run `m7-b8a` on B8a's web; B8b and B8c added three templates and that note (§17, B8c), and M6's `@research` selection did not run in the cloud after B8a (§18, B8b: the container's free space is below M6's research floor), so nothing caught it before the PC.

- *The fix.* The start dialog's content is given the class the research confirmation already uses (`max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)] overflow-y-auto`, M6 B8): it scrolls inside the viewport. No component was added or changed.
- *M7's own check.* M7's `@decisions` test now asks that the start dialog fits a phone after Market brief and after an entry-basis candidate is chosen. In this fixture the dialog is 685 px high at 390 px wide (measured on the build without the fix), so it fits M6's 700 px without scrolling; M7's check asks 600 px, about what a phone shows below its browser bars, so that it fails without the fix. Its helper checks the page's width, not clipping inside the dialog as M6's does.
- *Left as it is* (found by the fix's independent review, no blocker): the dialog's close button sits in the scrolling box and scrolls out of sight with it; Back and Escape still close the dialog. The other dialogs without the height limit are not checked on a phone by either spec.

### The fault runner's hold (2026-10-17)

The cloud session of B0 to B9d, on branch `claude/m7-b0-hjaahq` from `main` at `9119009`, after run `m7-b9a`'s `AgentFaults6` failed on the PC (OBSERVED there): its in-process part `VERIFIED`; its process part's case 1 (the agent worker killed while it waits) passed, case 2a (the runner killed once) timed out waiting for its job `RUNNING`, and the later cases did not run (`AgentFaults6-process-failed-20261017T011149323Z.json`). The job ran 3.7 s between two readings about 6 s apart. INFERRED: since ExecPlan 0025's speed-ups a screening of the case's two-day fixture ends before the harness can see it running; M6's passes of these cases ran before those speed-ups.

- *The hold.* `tests/m6_held_runner.py` runs the product's runner (`app.research.runner.main`, its loop, lease, heartbeat, fence and replay unchanged) with one addition: before the last cell of each attempt, a screening's second, it waits 40 s. The first cell has then reset the account, installed the candidate and replayed, so a kill lands inside an attempt that has written state and the retry's reset is shown, as before ExecPlan 0025. While it waits it keeps one session on the research database and reads the job's fence through it every second, as an open replay batch would: a restarted cluster ends the attempt (the session is lost), and a fenced or canceled job is refused there. `tools/m6/faults.psm1` starts the fault cases' runners with it (`python -m tests.m6_held_runner 40`) and records that in the phase record's `runner`. The cases, their waits and their checks are unchanged.
- *Why there, and not a larger fixture.* A longer job from more days was not chosen: how a screening's time grows with the days is not measured after ExecPlan 0025 (two days 3.7 s on the PC, 9.6 days 108 to 169 s), and the load of a day takes about a minute. A hold of a fixed length makes each case's moment independent of the job's speed. It is a fault injection in the runner's process, as `tests.m6_faults fetcher slow` is at the mock source, and the record says so.
- *Its tests.* `tests/test_m6_held_runner.py`, five tests without a database: the hold comes before the last cell only, once per attempt, with one session and its fence read about every second; a job of one cell waits before it; a refused fence or a lost session ends the attempt before its cell; a stopped runner does not wait it out. Each of four parts of the hold taken out once made a test fail (the fence read, the commit, the last cell, the session). They join `Build`'s static tests and its selection (`held_runner`) in `tools/profiles/m7.psd1`, and the profile check's expected list.
- *What it narrows.* The hold sits between cells: the first cell has committed and released the execution lease, and no replay batch is open. So the kills of cases 2a and 2b and the restart of case 4 land at that quiet point, never inside a replay batch as in M6's passes; a fault in the recovery from a kill in the middle of a batch is not exercised by these cases any more. The cases still show what they assert: the kill comes inside the attempt and its retry resets, the restart ends the hold's session, and 3b's job outlasts the bridge's stop. The record's `runner` says so.
- *Time.* About five or six minutes more for the process part (an independent review's count: case 1 40 s, 2a about 40 s, case 4 80 to 120 s with its small job, 3b three times 40 s), inside the phase's 32-minute window and 30-minute deadline against about 13 minutes observed before.
- *Found by the fix's independent review and taken:* a first version held before the first cell, without a session. The review found that a restart of the cluster could then fall between two lease renewals and leave the attempt to succeed on its first try, failing case 4's check that the restart interrupted something, and that kills would land before any cell had written state. Both are answered by the version above. A second review found that `Build`'s selection left the five tests out (`5 deselected`); `held_runner` is added to it. It also asked for the narrowing above to be said. *Left:* the hold's log lines go with the fault containers when the phase removes them.

### The boundary rehearsal's images (2026-10-17)

The same session and branch. Run `m7-b9a`'s `BoundaryRehearsal` (its first run on Docker, 01:12 to 01:27 UTC) failed on one check: "execution-worker does not run the image the boundary built; evaluation-worker does not run the image the boundary built" (`BoundaryRehearsal-failed-20261017T012747548Z.json`). Every other step held (OBSERVED on the PC): both models, the deployment, its rollback and the deployment again; the boundary refused while the pilot and then its workers ran; the boundary `COMPLETE` with both documents installed and unapproved, the second run `PRESENT`, the rollback refused after it; the synthetic owner's approvals; the policy's run `RUNNING`, bound to `AGENT_POLICY_V1`, with no failure line. The first boundary run's record names `execution-worker` `sha256:e1d045d92e94...` and `evaluation-worker` `sha256:a141cc868d99...`; the started workers run `sha256:e5c7e4833ec8...` and `sha256:9a1f78d20d3c...`, which the project's two names point to and whose images were created at 22:10:58 UTC, during the run's `Build`; the first run's two images were not on the host when read after the phase had ended. INFERRED, not confirmed: the boundary's second run built the same tree again and got new image IDs for the same content (an image created during `Build`, from its cached layers), so the names moved to its images, the first run's were left without a name, and the activation started the workers from the second run's images, as it should. The check compared them with the first run's record. A new ID and the same code identity do not prove the same content; the record fields below add what does: the image store, and each image's creation time and layer and configuration digests in both runs. The alternative, that something moved the names back to the pilot's own images after the boundary, is checked by name now as well.

- *The fix, in the rehearsal* (`tools/m6/rollout.psm1`): after the second boundary run, its built images, the names and their code identities are read and checked as after the first (each name the image that run built, of this run's code identity; the market worker's name unmoved), and the record lists which services got new image IDs (`steps.boundary_again.new_image_ids`) and which kept the same creation time, layers and configuration (`same_content`), with each run's `contents` and the daemon's storage driver (`docker_image_store`). Before the activation the two workers' names are read again (`steps.image_names_before_the_activation`) and must still be the last boundary run's images. The workers must then run the images the boundary built last. No owner script changes: `tools/m7-boundary.ps1` already checks, in each run, that the names are the images it built and that they compute the running API's code identity.
- *The owner's text.* `tools/m7-boundary.ps1`'s header, §13.2's item 4 above and OPERATIONS said a second run's builds "are cached". They now say that a second run may name new images and that the newest `m7-boundary.json` names those the activation starts. The procedure is unchanged: `tools/pilot-activate.ps1` starts the workers from the names.
- *Checked on stubbed Docker* (the B9d stub, a scratch script that is not committed, changed so that each boundary run gives new image IDs, as observed): the code before the fix gives the PC's message exactly; with the fix the phase is `VERIFIED`, also when both runs give the same IDs. A new fault, the names moved back to the pilot's images at the refused rollback, fails with "the image name of execution-worker moved after the boundary, before the activation". The 18 faults of B9d still fail, each with its message.

### The promotions of the API fixture (2026-10-17)

The same session and branch, from `main` at `55b59d5`. Run `m7-b9b`'s `PerformanceApi` failed at 07:19 UTC in its step `perf-api-routes` (`PerformanceApi-failed-20261017T071915478Z.json`, OBSERVED on the PC): `GET /v1/promotions` answered 500, the API's error "99 validation errors for PromotionsView ... items.N.template_id: Input should be 'PROMOTION_V1' or 'PROMOTION_V2' [input_value='MARKET_ASSESSMENT_V1']"; every route read before it answered 200. B6a typed `PromotionView.template_id`, which `app/research/views.py` reads from the workflow that wrote each promotion. M6's year of rows (`tests/m6_performance.py volume`) attached its 200 cloned promotions to the M5 measurement's assessment workflows. The product writes a promotion only from a promotion workflow (`app/agents/workflows.py`), and since B7 the database refuses any other (`asl_m7_promotion_guard`, migration `0009`); the fixture's clone, with triggers off, held rows real data cannot, and the API was right to refuse to describe them.

- *The fix, in the fixture:* each cloned promotion gets a copy of the fixture promotion's own workflow, for its candidate (`params.candidate_id`), with a fresh identity and order number. The copies are made before the M5 records, so the 50 newest workflows the routes read are still the M5 measurement's; their queue, start and finish times move with their creation time. The view and its type are unchanged. The cloned candidate reviews still sit on M5 assessment workflows; no typed view reads those workflows' templates today, and the fixture says so.
- *Checked here* on copies of the M7 browser fixture (scratch PostgreSQL 16; the M6 fixture itself is not seeded in the cloud, its screenings refuse below the 40 GiB research floor): with the old clone, 200 promotions on M5 workflows, the view through `PromotionsView` gives "99 validation errors", the PC's error; with the fix, valid, 100 items. The agent routes the step reads after it (runtime, the workflows page, the details of the 50 newest workflows and of five promotion workflows, approvals) validate through their response models; the 50 newest are the M5 measurement's. This covers the M7 fixture's `PROMOTION_V2` workflow only; the M6 fixture's `PROMOTION_V1` workflow, written by its own fixture code, is `NOT RUN` here and is what the PC's phase clones. The scratch scripts are not committed.

### The M6-B gate run `m7-g1a` and the held step's cost (2026-10-17 and 18)

**The run** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, prepared on `main` at `5b9987b`, 20:07 to 23:20 UTC, beside the pilot watch the owner started at 20:06 UTC). `Build` and `ResearchRunner` `VERIFIED`, the research lease's renewal fix among them, on PostgreSQL 17. `PerformanceResearch` `FAILED` (`PerformanceResearch-failed-20261017T231949771Z.json`) on one figure: the shadow advance over 21 days after the 302-hour warm-up, projected at 3,781.4 s against the job bound of 3,600. It is a projection from a measured advance over 2 days (245.9 s), most of it 38 stepped days charged at 87.03 s each (3,307 s), the rate of a day with a position. Every other figure it judges met its bound: a screening over 21 days 991 s; full tests over 14 and 21 days 1,270 s and 2,558 s; the shadow advance over 14 days 2,500 s; the idle dispatch at p95 4,808 ms against 5,000; staging 2,266 rows/s against at least 1,000, its largest batch 481 rows against at most 500. The run stops at the first failure: `DecisionSaturation` and `PilotCoexistence` `NOT RUN`. The pilot beside it: lease epoch 8 held throughout, no `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` row and no collection gap; retention failed twice and recovered at the next attempt both times; the watch ended itself at 23:20 UTC. The harness's `Clean` removed the run's nine Docker resources by exact name; the evidence stays under `.cache\m7\m7-g1a` in the PC session's worktree. The normal application was not addressed.

**What was done.** The same session and branch, from `main` at `5b9987b`, under ExecPlan 0025's authorization of 2026-10-09 and on the recommended option of the owner's unanswered card: the seven changes of [ExecPlan 0025 §5.9](0025-research-job-cost-and-search.md#59-the-constant-costs-of-a-held-step-added-2026-10-18-after-gate-run-m7-g1a), each leaving a replay's rows identical, with their measurement in its §14. No bound, cap, fixture or charging of the judge changed.

- *Tests.* `test_m3_throughput.py`: a statement classified once as every time; a plain copy is what `copy.copy` and `dataclasses.replace` make, and a class with a hook keeps `copy.copy`; the kept encoders write what `json.dumps` writes. `test_m3_unit.py`: a stable id is the `uuid5` of its kind and parts (a property test). Two existing tests are changed for the shared manifest, because sharing is the change: the copy test now counts the manifest as shared, and the reused-run test asserts that every copy of a kept run holds the kept manifest, that a fresh read does not, and, new, that with `verify` an edit of the shared manifest fails at the next read (`replay_cache_divergence:run`). Every other field is still asserted to be a step's own copy.
- *Checked in the cloud* (scratch PostgreSQL 16, not acceptance evidence; commands in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0025-the-constant-costs-of-a-held-step-2026-10-18)):

*The independent review* (read-only, of the whole diff; it ran pure Python snippets, no database) found no state in which a change makes a replay's rows, ids or trace differ, or makes the forward engine behave differently: the manifest is only read in `app/` and no run escapes a step, the evaluation's shortcut returns nothing only where every range would be empty, the stable id matched `uuid5` on 200,000 random names (surrogates raise the same error), and the encoders keep no state between calls. It found two faults, both fixed with a test that fails without the fix: the statement memory kept a kind worked out before `test_m6_runner.py` replaced `SELF_CONTAINED`, so that test's expected failures did not happen (it is now forgotten when `SELF_CONTAINED` is replaced); and the plain copy's test of a class asked for a positive dictionary offset, which an ordinary class does not have on Python 3.13, so it never applied (it now asks for any offset, and also leaves out `__getnewargs__`, `__getnewargs_ex__` and `copyreg` entries). It noted that the shortcut of the evaluation and the held check of `_inert` have no test of their own: they are held by the scenario and fingerprint tests, which compare with one step a transaction. The measurements above are of the fixed code.

### The M6-B gate run `m7-g1b`: the saturation feeder's jobs and a staging read that runs once (2026-10-18)

**The run** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, on `main` at `207deb7`, in the PC session's worktree, beside the pilot watch the owner started at 02:24 UTC and again at 02:31 UTC). The owner asked for `DecisionSaturation` before `PerformanceResearch`. `Build` `VERIFIED` (02:25 UTC); the change of order stopped the first watch at 02:25 UTC, because the runner looked for `Build`'s record under the wrong file name, and the PC session fixed that before the owner started the watch again. `ResearchRunner` `VERIFIED` (02:36 UTC). `DecisionSaturation` `FAILED` at 04:43 UTC (`DecisionSaturation-failed-20261018T044331806Z.json`), on its research side only. Inside the loaded thirty minutes (04:13 to 04:43 UTC) everything else it judges passed:

- the agent load: all 36 decisions and 4 briefs `SUCCEEDED`, the one beyond each budget ended on the daily budget, a decision p95 16.8 s and a brief p95 12.2 s, every submission answered, no lease lost;
- the policy engine: step p95 313 ms and p99 338 ms against 750 and 1,500, all 5,400 publications looked at;
- research: the runner 99.3% busy, no job lost its worker, staging at 1,719 rows/s with no minute without staging.

Its problems, both research-side:

1. *Staging beside a long job is not shown* (two of the record's problem lines). Every job ran 410 to 430 s, and the staging watch needs rows staged after a job's 660th second. The feeder's candidate was the reference rule at a hold of 29 hours, which B9 had INFERRED to run 620 to 1,070 s on the seven-day store and which had never been measured. The jobs were short before the held step's speed-up as well (INFERRED: at 10% to 13% less time, below 500 s).
2. *One `research_staging_failed` line* in the bridge's log (`OperationalError`, without its SQLSTATE). Staging went on in the next pass. The bridge logs only the class of the error, so the statement that failed is not known from the record. The owner was asked to have the PC session save the database containers' `ERROR` lines before the run's resources are removed.

`PerformanceResearch` and `PilotCoexistence` are `NOT RUN` on `207deb7`: the run stops at the first failure. The pilot beside it: lease epoch 8 held, no `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` row, no collection gap; retention failed four times and recovered twice from 02:31 UTC to the end of the phase. The owner chose on the thread's card not to read the application's incident rows for it, and these counts are reported with the run.

**The feeder's jobs** (test code only, `tests/m6_performance.py`). Every saturation job now runs a candidate that enters on the condition that holds on every fixture bar and has no other entry condition, with the widest stop the envelope allows (5 ATR) and no signal exit, at a hold of 8 hours (`_measured_candidate(..., held=True)`, `SATURATION_HOLD_HOURS`). It holds a position nearly always, so a job steps at every quote for almost all of each cell. The constant of the added condition still makes each job's candidate new. `PerformanceResearch`'s measured jobs keep the reference rule. `ResearchSaturation` on the M6 harness shares the feeder and gets the same candidate; it is not part of the M7 gate. No judgment, bound or fixture size changed, in either phase: the store stays at seven days (the [owner's decision of 2026-10-06](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-06--m6-b9-phase-content-images-saturation-length-job-measurement-row-counts-quiet-window-lost-store-regression-and-the-read-only-look), point 2), and the watch still needs rows staged after the 660th second.

Measured in the cloud (OBSERVED there, not acceptance evidence): the six cells of one full test replayed one by one on the seven-day saturation fixture (`tests/m6_performance._load_dense`, 1,815,038 publications), as the runner replays a cell, with the driver's batch of 4,000 steps:

| Candidate | DEV ×2 | VALIDATION ×2 | HOLDOUT ×2 | Job |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reference rule, hold 29 h (before) | 63.3, 63.6 s | 7.3, 7.4 s | 59.9, 60.2 s | 261.8 s |
| Held, hold 6 h | 151.4, 148.3 s | 54.9, 53.0 s | 64.7, 66.6 s | 538.9 s |
| Held, hold 8 h (chosen) | 153.0, 151.7 s | 51.1, 53.8 s | 66.3, 67.0 s | 542.9 s |

At that ratio for the whole job, a job of the chosen candidate is INFERRED to run about 820 to 870 s on the PC, 160 to 210 s past the 660th second; about 700 s if all the PC's extra time over the cloud's were outside the replay, which nothing measured shows. Whether a job ran that long stays the watch's reading on the PC. (Superseded by gate run `m7-g1c`'s own timings, in the next section: about 660 to 720 s.)

**The staging read** (product code, `app/research/staging.py`, and the bridge's log line). The likely failure, INFERRED until the database's log line was read (OBSERVED since, in the next section: it was this read): the first batch of the backlog reads its collection through `research_read.market_collections`, a security-barrier view that calls a collection published when one of its observations has a publication. `market_observations` has no index on `collection_id`, so the search reads the table up to the collection's first row. [ExecPlan 0023 §19](0023-m6-research-investigations.md#19-open-risks) recorded this read, and the double read of a new collection, as a risk for `ResearchSaturation` on 2026-10-08 ("two more staging reads go through a barrier view"), to be timed or made independent of the table's size before the activation. In the cloud, `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` of that read for a second collection behind the seven days took 285 ms with the database's cache warm and 317 ms after a restart of the database and a write to `drop_caches` (whose effect inside this container is not known), on an idle host: a sequential scan that removed 1,512,372 rows and read 49,622 pages (388 MB) outside the shared buffers, because a sequential scan larger than the buffers uses a small ring of them. A canceled statement under the research identity's 2 s raises `OperationalError` with SQLSTATE `57014` (OBSERVED in the cloud). On the PC the phase's measurement starts the main database container again just before the bridge's first pass, beside the stream, the engine, the agent worker and the store's job, so a read of 388 MB there past 2 s is plausible and not shown. The same read runs in the normal application for every collection the store does not hold: at the research activation, and after each market worker's new lease generation, which opens a new collection. A second read of the same kind, where a new store version starts (`staging.origin`), reads every publication inside the backfill once for each store version.

Chosen by the owner on the thread's card ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-two-staging-reads-that-run-once-get-a-20-s-bound)), its recommended option, after a card at 30 s was withdrawn on the review's third point: those two reads raise their own statement bound to 20 s (`ONCE_SECONDS`) with `SET LOCAL` for their one statement, then return to the identity's bound with `SET LOCAL statement_timeout TO DEFAULT`, as the source fetcher's write raises its own bound on main to 5 s. A read canceled under the raised bound ends its transaction and the raised bound with it. The store's transaction waits idle while the source is read, and the bridge identity ends one that waits 60 s: the schema's check and origin's two statements at most stay below that at 20 s each. If a read took longer than 20 s every time, each pass would repeat it, as it repeats one longer than 2 s now; OPERATIONS states the bound. A new collection is read once per batch instead of twice; its rows come from the same snapshot, so the batch is unchanged. The `research_staging_failed` line now names the SQLSTATE (the `app/market_data/worker.py` idiom). The card's other options were an index on `market_observations(collection_id)`, a migration of an M2 table that changes M7's database version and its deploy tools (the [owner's decision of 2026-10-06](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-06--m6-b9-phase-content-images-saturation-length-job-measurement-row-counts-quiet-window-lost-store-regression-and-the-read-only-look), point 4, puts such an index in a migration and pull request of its own, and puts a read that misses its budget to the owner before any fix); or accepting the line as a known finding. No other bound of the identity, grant or view changes.

- *Tests.* `test_m6_staging.py`: a new collection is read once, under the raised bound, which is given back (`SHOW statement_timeout` is `2s` after the read), and the batch carries the collection; where a new store starts is read under the raised bound, which is given back; a read canceled at the raised bound leaves the next transaction at 2 s; and, without a database, a read that runs once is wrapped in its bound and no other read is, and origin's second statement, when the backfill holds no publication, is wrapped as its first is. The three database tests need the catalog's secrets and are `NOT RUN` in the cloud; they run in `Staging` on the PC.
- *Checked in the cloud* (scratch PostgreSQL 16, not acceptance evidence; commands in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-the-gate-run-m7-g1b-and-its-two-fixes-2026-10-18)):

*The independent review* (read-only, of the uncommitted diff; it ran ruff, the database-free test and pure Python snippets, no database) found no blocker, two major points, four minor points and three nits. It found the reuse of a new collection's rows equal to the second read it replaces, `_once` inside the source's one transaction in every product path, and each new test failing on the code before the change. Its points:

1. *The PC estimate scaled a whole job by a ratio of the cells' replays* (major). A job also hashes its dataset and sets up and checks each cell. Those parts are timed in the cloud now, and the estimate is a range with its assumption (above): about 820 to 870 s, at the least about 700 s against the 660 s needed. Holds of 6 and 12 hours were not longer (table above), and the fixture stays at seven days by the owner's decision of 2026-10-06; the watch judges it.
2. *The research identity's bound is raised on main without a record* (major). OPERATIONS now states both reads and their bound, the code's comment names the raise on main it follows (the source fetcher's) instead of the store's sealing scans, and the card states what remains: a read longer than the raised bound would be repeated each pass, as a read longer than 2 s is now. The owner chose it on the card, which answers [ExecPlan 0023 §19](0023-m6-research-investigations.md#19-open-risks)'s risk for these two reads.
3. *The store's transaction could pass the bridge identity's 60 s idle limit* (minor): it waits while the source is read, and the schema's check with origin's two statements at 30 s each could reach it. The bound is 20 s; the card at 30 s was withdrawn and posted again at 20 s. A batch can still run into the quiet window by as long as those reads take when they take that long, against 0.3 s measured.
