7. *`SET LOCAL statement_timeout TO DEFAULT` gives back the session's value, not one a caller set* (nit): intended, the identity's bound as the docstring says; no caller sets one. Not changed.
8. *Test gaps* (nit): `bounds_around` no longer wraps at the first statement; origin's second statement has a database-free test; a read canceled at the raised bound has a database test (`NOT RUN` here; the same was checked in the cloud, table above).

### The M6-B gate run `m7-g1c`: a report with an entry not yet filled (2026-10-18)

**Before the run** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, at the owner's request): `m7-g1b`'s test containers still existed, and their database `ERROR` lines from 04:05 to 04:45 UTC were saved (`.cache\m7\m7-g1b-db-errors-0405-0445.txt` in the PC session's worktree). There was one, in the catalog database: `04:08:29.609 UTC ERROR: canceling statement due to statement timeout`, on `SELECT t.id, to_jsonb(t)::text AS body FROM research_read.market_collections t WHERE t.id = ANY($1)`, two milliseconds before the bridge's `research_staging_failed` line and in the phase's lead time. The read the previous section INFERRED, and which the 20 s bound covers, is the one that failed.

**The run** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, on `main` at `9917639`, in the PC session's worktree, beside the pilot watch the owner started at 07:25:45 UTC, one phase at a time in the order the owner typed). `Build` `VERIFIED` (07:26 UTC), `ResearchRunner` `VERIFIED` (07:26 to 07:30 UTC), `Staging` `VERIFIED` (07:31 to 07:33 UTC, with the previous section's new database tests). `DecisionSaturation` `FAILED` at 09:43 UTC (`DecisionSaturation-failed-20261018T094325884Z.json`), measured 09:13 to 09:43 UTC:

- passed: the agent load (all 36 decisions and 4 briefs `SUCCEEDED`), the policy engine (step p95 313 ms and p99 339 ms, intake p95 271 ms, all 5,400 publications looked at), the runner 99.6% busy with no job losing its worker, and no `research_staging_failed` line;
- failed: each of the three jobs that finished in the window (`0558c5d8`, 09:08:29 to 09:18:08 UTC; `3a330123`, 09:18:09 to 09:28:37; `6f69786a`, 09:28:39 to 09:38:28) ended `REPLAY_FAILED` with error type `TypeError` after 579 to 628 s. A fourth job was canceled at the phase's end, as designed. So no job ran past the staging watch's 660th second (the longest 627.6 s), and staging beside a long job is still not shown.

At the owner's answer "A", the PC session read the failed jobs' stored results from the run's research store, read-only (`.cache\m7\m7-g1c-failed-jobs.txt`): all three are full tests whose first four cells (DEV and VALIDATION, both cost scenarios) ended `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`, and whose fifth, HOLDOUT `H1` (2026-10-07 19:24 to 2026-10-09 05:00 UTC), recorded its reset and then the failure. The runner's log line names the error's type only. Then the run's 30 test resources were removed by exact name. `PerformanceResearch` and `PilotCoexistence` are `NOT RUN` on `9917639`. The pilot beside it: lease epoch 8 held, no `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` row, no collection gap; market incidents `RETENTION_FAILED` 2 (1 recovered), `CLOCK_UNCERTAIN` 1, `CLOCK_UNVERIFIED` 1.

**The cause** (OBSERVED in the cloud, reproduced). A sealed research dataset ends a microsecond after the newest row of its range (`app/research/datasets.research_range`), and on the saturation store that row is the last hour's final candle, available 300 ms after the hour. The two holdout cells (cost scenarios `H1` and `H2_STRESS`), the only cells that end at the dataset's end, see it; the PC's jobs failed on the first, `H1`, so the second never ran there. The held candidate, which enters whenever it may, accepts an entry on it, and the cell ends before a quote can fill it. The cell's report (`app/evaluation/worker.generate`) then holds a position with no fill by the as-of time beside filled ones, and the trade list sorted them on `first_fill_at or datetime.max`: a naive `datetime.max` against aware fill times, `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` at `app/evaluation/metrics.py:114`. The cloud's earlier replays set the window's end to the whole hour by hand, so they never saw that candle; a job sealed as the bridge seals it failed the same way at the same cell, and after the cell ran with the fix the database held that entry's position (SOL, its mandate at 05:00:00.300 UTC, no fill) beside eight filled ones. The reference rule's jobs on `m7-g1b` succeeded: that rule enters only on a crossing, which that candle did not give (INFERRED from their success). The same report builds a forward run's snapshots, so one whose as-of time fell between an accepted entry and its fill, with a filled position earlier in the run, would have failed the same way (INFERRED from the code; not observed).

**The fix** (product code). Such a position sorts last on an aware bound (`LAST` in `app/evaluation/metrics.py`); it has no fill there and is left out of the trade list, as before. A failed research attempt's log line now also names the innermost line of the engine's own code the error passed through (`raised_at`, `app.research.runner.failure_site`), never its message, which can carry data: the job's result keeps only its reason, and this failure's line took a reproduction. No judgment, bound, fixture, candidate or protocol changed.

The record's expected length of a feeder job (test code, `each_job_expected`) is corrected from this run: its three jobs reached the end of their fifth cell after 579 to 628 s on the PC, against about 491 s for the same parts in the cloud, so a whole job is INFERRED to run about 660 to 720 s there, not 820 to 870 s. That is close to the 660 s the staging watch needs: the next run may show staging beside a long job, or fail as not shown again. The store's size is the owner's (the [decision of 2026-10-06](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-06--m6-b9-phase-content-images-saturation-length-job-measurement-row-counts-quiet-window-lost-store-regression-and-the-read-only-look), point 2), and the hold of 8 hours already gave the longest job of the holds timed.

- *Tests.* `test_m4_evaluation.py`: a position not filled by the as-of time is left out beside a filled one, and the trade list and realized P&L are the filled one's (it failed on the code before the fix with the same `TypeError`). `test_m6_runner.py`: `failure_site` names the engine line an error was raised at, and nothing for an error raised outside the engine's code; and a failed attempt, run through the runner's own failure path from an error raised outside the engine, logs its reason, type and the engine line the error passed through, never the error's message. All three run without a database.
- *Checked in the cloud* (scratch PostgreSQL 16, not acceptance evidence; commands in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-the-gate-run-m7-g1c-and-its-fix-2026-10-18)):

| Item | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| A full test through `runner.run_once` on the saturation store's layout (seven days of part `a` from 2026-10-02 05:00 UTC, a first day of part `b` after it), the held candidate at 8 hours, the window set by hand to the whole hours | all six cells `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`; hash 9.4 s, cells 151.2, 154.2, 51.8, 55.3, 68.8 and 68.9 s | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The same job on the window `research_range` seals (2026-10-02 05:00:00.270 to 2026-10-09 05:00:00.300001 UTC), holdout `H1` only, the other cells left out | `REPLAY_FAILED`, the `TypeError` at `app/evaluation/metrics.py:114` from `report.build` in `generate`, after 69.8 s | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The same with the fix, both holdout cells (cost scenarios `H1` and `H2_STRESS`), the other cells left out | `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE`, 72.8 and 74.3 s; the run's last mandate (SOL, 05:00:00.300 UTC) without a fill | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The new metrics test on the code before the fix | `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| ruff, the OpenAPI check | `All checks passed!`, "FastAPI OpenAPI schema matches" | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The database-free pytest | `2 failed, 741 passed, 329 skipped, 340 errors` after the review's fixes: the same failures and errors as before the change (tests that need a database), and the three new tests pass | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The gate on the PC with the fix | | `NOT RUN` |

*Review* (independent and read-only, of the diff): no blocker; three points to fix and seven nits, each fixed or answered here (one was the merge of `main` then in progress, since committed).

1. *An estimate left at the earlier figure.* TESTING's description of `DecisionSaturation` still gave 820 to 870 s; it gives this run's 660 to 720 s now, and the previous section's estimate is marked superseded.
2. *STATUS overstated the replay with the fix.* On the window the bridge seals only the holdout cells were replayed, before and after the fix; the six cells ran on a window set by hand. STATUS and the table above say so.
3. *The log line itself was not tested.* A second runner test runs an attempt through the runner's own failure path (`_execute`) and reads the line: its reason, type and `raised_at`, and not the error's message.
4. *Nits.* `failure_site` skips a frame that names no absolute file (generated code), walks the traceback without reading source lines, and is otherwise unchanged; the dates of the sealed window and the holdout's two cost scenarios are named. The runner's other failure lines still give the error's type only (outside this change). The risk that a job runs no more than 660 s on the PC is the one the records state: the store's size is the owner's, and the feeder's hold already gave the longest job of the holds timed, so the next run shows whether a job runs long enough, and a store-size decision follows only if none does.

### The M6-B gate run `m7-g1d`: a renewal behind a slow batch (2026-10-18)

**The run** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, on `main` at `595e5aa`, in the PC session's worktree, beside the pilot watch the owner started at 11:17 UTC, one phase at a time in the order the owner typed at 11:00 UTC). `Build`, `ResearchRunner` and `Staging` `VERIFIED` (11:17 to 11:26 UTC). `DecisionSaturation` `FAILED` at 13:43 UTC (`DecisionSaturation-failed-20261018T134323817Z.json`), measured 13:13 to 13:43 UTC:

- passed: the agent load and the policy engine; all three jobs in the window `SUCCEEDED`, in 670 to 685 s each, with no `TypeError` (the previous section's fix); staging beside a long job, shown for the first time on any run: rows were staged after each job was more than ten minutes old; and the bridge logged no failure line;
- failed: one line of the research runner's log, which the phase counts as a failure (`_failed` in its pattern, `tools/m6/performance.psm1`). At 13:33:55.382 UTC the runner logged `research_heartbeat_failed` with `OperationalError`, and at the same millisecond the research store logged `ERROR: canceling statement due to lock timeout`, `CONTEXT: while locking tuple (6,3) in relation "work"`, on `SELECT id FROM research_runtime.work WHERE id=$1 FOR UPDATE`. The renewal was asked again at the next tick, and the job (`f672e851`) `SUCCEEDED`.

The pilot beside it: lease epoch 8 held, no collection gap; two `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` rows during `Build` and `ResearchRunner`, under the watch's stop limit (more than four pairs in thirty minutes); one retention failure. At the owner's message of 14:34 UTC the PC session planned `PerformanceResearch` and `PilotCoexistence` on `m7-g1d`, for information, while the fix was made, beside a new watch the owner starts. The watch was not up for 15:07 UTC; it started at 15:20 UTC. Under the rerun below only `PerformanceResearch` runs on `m7-g1d`, its parts from 16:07 UTC (about 18:20 UTC by the PC session's estimate), and it counts toward the gate if it is `VERIFIED`; `PilotCoexistence` runs on `m7-g1e`. Neither result is part of this record (`NOT RUN` at its writing).

**The cause** (OBSERVED in the cloud, reproduced). Each replay batch holds its job's row shared from its open to its commit (`queue.fence(..., hold=True)` in `FencedReplay._open_batch` and `flush`, `app/research/runner.py`), a second or less by design. The job's renewal thread (`renew` in `runner.execute`, every 3 s) takes the row for update in `queue.heartbeat`, on the admin identity, whose lock bound is 2 s (`runner.engines`). Under the saturation load one batch held the row longer than that, and the renewal timed out at the bound (SQLSTATE `55P03`). [ExecPlan 0025 §13](0025-research-job-cost-and-search.md) decided that such a renewal is asked again at the next tick; that happened, and the 15 s lease was not lost. But the line it logs is one the phase must count, because every research failure is named `_failed`. A test that holds the row shared for 3 s and renews behind it failed on the code before the fix with the PC's error: `LockNotAvailable`, "while locking tuple ... in relation "work"", on the same statement.

**The fix** (product code). A renewal waits up to `RENEWAL_LOCK_SECONDS` (5 s, a third of the lease) for the row, with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` in its own transaction (`app/research/queue.py`): the bound ends with that transaction, and every other statement of the identity keeps 2 s. A batch that holds the row longer than 2 s and less than 5 s now only delays the renewal. One that still holds it after 5 s times the renewal out as before, and the renewal is asked again at the next tick. The lease stays safe mainly because, while batches run, the replay renews it itself between them (`_open_batch`, every `RENEW_SECONDS`, 1 s), and that renewal holds no batch while it waits, so it waits at most for the renewal thread's short transaction; the thread is the backstop. After one of its renewals times out, its next one still starts inside the lease: about 3 s after the last renewal, the wait of 5 s and about 3 s more, about 11 s of the 15 (INFERRED from the code and the loop's timing; the heartbeat file's write and the storage reading every 30 s add a little). A job's end now waits for its renewal thread up to `RENEWAL_LOCK_SECONDS` + 2 s (it was 4 s), longer than one renewal can wait. No judgment, bound of the phase, fixture, candidate or protocol changed.

- *Not chosen.* Accepting a renewal that was asked again as no failure, which would change the phase's judgment and could hide a renewal that never succeeds; a shorter batch, which ExecPlan 0025 §13 also left out and which costs replay time that `PerformanceResearch`'s projections leave little room for (`m7-g1a`'s shadow advance missed its bound by 5%).
- *Tests* (`test_m6_runner.py`, on the research database). A renewal behind a batch that holds the row for 3 s waits more than 2 s and less than `RENEWAL_LOCK_SECONDS`, renews the lease, and leaves the identity's 2 s bound in place after it, read on the renewal's own pooled connection; it failed on the code before the fix, and with a session-wide `SET` in place of `SET LOCAL` it fails on that bound (`'5s' == '2s'`). The test of a renewal behind a long batch now holds the row for `RENEWAL_LOCK_SECONDS` + 5 s, so its first renewal still fails with a lock wait and is asked again (with the earlier 6 s, the renewal would wait the batch out).
- *Checked in the cloud* (scratch PostgreSQL 16, not acceptance evidence; commands in [COMMANDS](../COMMANDS.md#execplan-0026-the-gate-run-m7-g1d-and-its-fix-2026-10-18)):

| Item | Result | Label |
|---|---|---|
| The new renewal test on the code before the fix | `LockNotAvailable`, "while locking tuple ... in relation "work"", on the renewal's `FOR UPDATE` | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The renewal tests with the fix, after the review's fixes (`-k "renewal or heartbeat"`) | 4 passed in 45.04 s | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The new test with `SET` in place of `SET LOCAL` (a check of the test, reverted) | failed: `assert '5s' == '2s'` | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| `test_m6_runner.py` on the research fixture database | 31 passed, 1 failed: `test_real_worker_process_loss_one_retry_and_stale_fence`, whose child process cannot claim on this container's disk, as on 2026-10-17 | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| ruff, the OpenAPI check | `All checks passed!`, "FastAPI OpenAPI schema matches" | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The database-free pytest | `2 failed, 741 passed, 329 skipped, 341 errors`: the same failures and errors as before the change (tests that need a database), and the new database test's error | `OBSERVED` in the cloud |
| The gate on the PC with the fix | | `NOT RUN` |

**The rerun** (PROPOSED for the PC; [the owner's go-ahead of 14:34 UTC](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18--the-owners-go-ahead-to-speed-up-m7-and-the-gates-rerun-on-the-renewal-fix)). A new run `m7-g1e` on the commit that merges this fix: `Build`, `ResearchRunner` (the renewal's tests), `Staging`, `DecisionSaturation` (the failed phase) and `PilotCoexistence`; its evidence is the phase records, because `Report` refuses any run that started a gate phase (`tools/m6/report.psm1`; found by the PC session after the merge, OBSERVED in the code). `PerformanceResearch` stands from `m7-g1d` when it is `VERIFIED` there: between the two commits the product differs by the renewal's lock wait and how long a job's end waits for its renewal thread, which none of its figures include (INFERRED from the code), and it does not judge the runner's log lines (its failure pattern is the bridge's staging lines, at the start of `Invoke-M6PerformanceResearch`). Otherwise it runs on `m7-g1e` too. It did not end `VERIFIED` there; the owner's decision of 19:31 UTC, in [the next section](#performanceresearch-on-m7-g1d-stopped-by-the-pilot-watch-2026-10-18), replaces the sentence "Otherwise it runs on `m7-g1e` too."

*Review* (independent and read-only, of the diff): no blocker; five points to fix and seven nits, each fixed or answered here.

1. *A class that does not exist.* The batch path is `FencedReplay`'s, not `ResearchDriver`'s.
2. *The plan for `m7-g1d` contradicted itself.* Only `PerformanceResearch` runs there now; `PilotCoexistence` runs on `m7-g1e`.
3. *No step ended `m7-g1e`'s watch.* A watch still running during the application update would read the recreated services as a change of the pilot and stop the run. COMMANDS now creates the end file after `Clean` and waits for the watch's last line before the update.
4. *STATUS left out the pilot's rows.* It names the two `PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` rows and the retention failure now.
5. *The test's last check could pass without checking anything.* The pool may hand the read another connection than the renewal's. The test empties the pool before the renewal and asserts one connection in it before the read; with a session-wide `SET` in place of `SET LOCAL` it now fails.
6. *Nits.* A job's end waits for its renewal thread longer than one renewal can wait; a renewal that hit the lock bound "timed out" (a refusal is something else in the runner); the new test's docstring says which renewal logged the line, and the test asserts the batch's thread ended; the lease's safety rests mainly on the replay's own renewal between batches, and the loop's timings are approximate; the saving in DECISIONS is labeled an estimate and the update's commit is named; DECISIONS cites the thread's message of 13:06 UTC that put a rerun of only the failed check to the owner; COMMANDS changes into the worktree first and gives the watch 16 hours.

### The boundary, M7-B and M7's close (2026-10-19)

**The boundary** (OBSERVED on the owner's PC, reported by the PC session). **M7-B** (OBSERVED in the application's database by read-only checks, reported by the PC session; each action the owner's, in the application, and step 3 from a file the owner double-clicked):
1. `AGENT_POLICY_V1` approved by hash `cf2519333007ad4bc37cee250a0eab0be178b9e0cfd5eac163a66899ec20fcb6` at 10:58:59 UTC; `EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2` by hash `55340dff5e17d80627b373de5be2b7946f482c32e4140e36b437b95838637169` at 10:59:06.
2. Experiment `a8b3ae07-0fe8-40b7-a59f-1a485c3dda43` (`FORWARD_PAPER`) drafted at 11:03:43 UTC; its manifest `b74dbf62027d6707f69de190ba1b9afedb314a031fc19424b8b7f2c817294128` binds both documents and was approved at 11:04:30 (`READY`). Its cost scenario was not reported here; the owner was pointed to `COST_SCENARIO_HYPOTHETICAL_H1`.
3. `tools/pilot-activate.ps1 -ExperimentId a8b3ae07-…` exit 0 at 11:07:13 UTC: both identities enabled and both flags set; both workers recreated on the boundary's images (`execution-worker` `c07a5b2fa9fb`, `evaluation-worker` `eb7a1f93b6e0`, started 11:07:10); the lease held; the run `READY`, version 2.
4. Start, requested at 11:06:05 UTC, before the workers came up. The run read `RUNNING`, version 3, at about 11:16:40. Until then the new execution worker replayed the publications since the pilot's worker stopped at 10:16:18, a 51-minute backlog, and deferred the start at each step (`RUN_START_DEFERRED`, `MARK_UNAVAILABLE_BTC`, the replayed quote older than 5 s while the feed read `CONNECTED`). VERIFIED in the code: a pending start makes every publication a full step (`app/execution/engine.py`, `needs_step`, `run.pending`); each step reads the quotes as of its publication against the clock (`app/execution/worker.py`, `process_batch`); a held asset without a usable quote makes the valuation invalid (`valuation`, `MARK_UNAVAILABLE_<asset>`), and `_apply_start` defers while it is. The dust is such an asset, so the start waited until the replay reached the present. It fails closed; here it cost about 9.5 minutes. INFERRED: a longer downtime costs more, and steps slower than the publications arrive would never catch up.
5. The decision probes did not run. The owner started the capability probes at 11:06:21 UTC (run 6) while the run was still `READY` and the Agents page did not yet offer the decision probes (`agents-page.tsx` waits for the policy's run to be running); Enable at 11:06:34 moved the runtime to `ENABLED`; at 11:06:37 the agent worker's probe loop canceled run 6 after 3 calls, because a capability run needs `PROBE_ONLY` (`app/agents/probes.py`, `progress`, `_cancel`). At 11:13 UTC the owner reported their Codex usage used up.
6. Enable: the runtime reads `ENABLED` (`OWNER_ENABLE_ENABLED`) since 11:06:34 UTC, on profile `9a7e664d9186` with a verified capability record (OBSERVED); that it is run 5's is INFERRED from the code (`matching_verification`).

**What runs now** (VERIFIED in the code; OBSERVED on the PC where named). The scheduler starts no decision workflow until a decision probe run is `VERIFIED` for the approved profile and the policy (`app/agents/decisions.py`, `_verified`, `DECISION_SUITE_NOT_VERIFIED`); a failed or missing suite never latches (`app/agents/probes.py`, `_finish_decisions`). Other agent work starts: by 11:15 UTC two `EVENT_INVESTIGATION_V1` workflows and one `RESEARCH_V1` had ended `FAILED` with `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (OBSERVED; the usage limit as the cause is INFERRED from the owner's 11:13 report, the stored error text unread). Nothing traded (OBSERVED at 11:15 UTC); no decision workflow could start, so no trade intent was submitted (INFERRED from the code). The engine values the dust and protects without a model. Every M7 service runs `09f4490`'s images; by design the market worker, the agent runner, egress and PostgreSQL are unchanged (OBSERVED at 11:18 UTC).

**M7's close** ([DECISIONS](../../DECISIONS.md#2026-10-19--m7-closes-without-the-decision-probes-and-the-first-live-decision)). At 11:18 UTC the owner closed M7 without the decision probes and the recorded live decision cycle of M7-OD8 (a); the same words are read as closing M6 too (an interpretation, see DECISIONS). Rule 14 of the repository instructions: the M7 release on `09f4490` is deployed and running, with its own readiness check (2026-10-19, above); the owner used the pages for every in-application step above (the approvals, the experiment, Start, the probes and Enable), and the owner's reports are the only browser evidence: a structured browser check after the deployment was not run. The PC session reports the test runs cleaned (`m7-g1e`'s `Clean` removed 65 resources by exact name, above) and removed `m7-g1e`'s worktree after copying its evidence to `.claude\worktrees\m7-b0\.cache\m7\from-m7-g1e`.

**Follow-ups:**
- The decision probes: "Start decision probes" on the Agents page once the usage resets (the owner's; at most 40 calls); decisions then start on their triggers.
- The first live decision cycle and the walk-through of §13.4, with M7-OD8 (a)'s follow-ups, among them the new run's checkpoint date, which the owner names (not yet named).
- M6's walk-through ([ExecPlan 0023 §13.3](0023-m6-research-investigations.md#133-the-live-walk-through)).
- A start after a worker's downtime (step 4): a pending start could skip a stale publication's full step while nothing is exposed but dust. Not designed.
- The usage-limit refusal's pattern ([ExecPlan 0030 §4.5](0030-agent-models-and-effort-per-role.md#45-the-usage-limit-block)); the stored error text of the failed runs and workflows is still unread.

## 19. Open risks

- **Model latency is not known to this plan.** A decision with a review may take longer than the proposed expiry allows, or cost more calls a day than the subscription gives. The first is read before the boundary, the second observed in M7-B.
- **No real model has been sent a generation-2 or generation-3 step.** M6-B's probes are the first for generation 2; the capability probe is the first for generation 3. A refusal there changes hashes after acceptance.
- **The quiet window delays every bar decision by six minutes or more.** It exists for the evaluation worker's hourly capture and stays.
- **The forward engine changes.** The reference path is held identical by tests, but the engine file is shared, and after the boundary every rebuild of the workers carries M7's code. The intake sits in three places of the step (before fills, directly before entries and after them), takes no lock it must wait for, and is absent from an engine bound to the reference; `EngineParity7` holds the reference's statements per step to B1's (§18, B3).
- **A new read path in each direction** across the line M5 drew. The execution identity reads agent-written rows as requests it validates; a compromised agent identity could still submit requests inside the policy's sets. The limits, the review guard and the risk gate bound what such requests can do. The agent identity reads financial rows of one run through views.
- **Account data leaves the PC** to the model provider (M7-OD6). It is a paper account; the profile's data scope states it.
- **Thin evidence.** One position per symbol, two entries a day at most and a paper account give few trades. Reports will say "insufficient sample" for a long time. No return threshold is set.
- **After the pilot there is no forward run of the reference.** The control replay is a deferred replay with its labels, and it runs only while research is active.
- **A reused reason value.** `TREND_EXIT` on an agent's exit must be read with the mandate's exit rule. The report's trade list maps it to `AGENT_EXIT` (B4) and a position's page names it (B8b); the orders list and a reader of the raw table show the recorded origin and could misread it.
- **The boundary is one sitting with several steps** on the owner's installation, after weeks of the pilot. It is rehearsed in isolation. An open drawdown latch stops it.
- **A probe run of the capability suite ends decision work in flight,** as it ends research today, because one of its probes raises the epoch. The operator procedure says to run it with no decision workflow open.
- **Share locks starve a waiting epoch update while they overlap.** On PostgreSQL 16 (B0) and 17.11 (B3's §18) share holders which overlap without a gap starve a waiting update for as long as they overlap (the cases' bound); agent commits alone do so, as today, and one engine alone does not, so B3 keeps the hold. The owner's Disable waits for agent commits today and, under the hold, for an engine step too; it is tried three times, then answered `503 TRY_AGAIN`, never left as an error. A Disable during a stream of overlapping agent commits can therefore need the owner to try again.
- **An owner's cancel of a workflow is not immediate at the engine.** It acts when the agent worker ends the workflow, a second later, and not while that worker is down. Disable and the run's Pause are immediate.
- **M7-B depends on M6-B's research activation,** because the one capability run holds the research probes.
- **The numbers of §5.2 and §5.7 are proposals without a measurement.**
- **The decision suite's budget rests on a script.** Scripted, it takes 26 of its 40 calls; a real model that retries a refused tool may take more and fail CP-27 for its budget. A failed suite blocks every decision until the owner runs it again; M7-B measures the calls.
- **A decision is not shown its risk headroom.** The snapshot says so (`RISK_HEADROOM_NOT_SHOWN`); a model may submit an entry the risk gate then refuses, and that submission still counts toward the policy's limits (§17, B3 decision 6).
- **The plan rests on `main` after the performance pull request.** §2 was read before that merge.
- **Two sessions write the same status documents.** Whichever merges second merges `main` and keeps both entries.
- **The M5 and M6 guard functions read tables by bare name without a fixed search path** (found by B1's review). A session that may create temporary tables could make one stand in for a table such a guard reads. `0009`'s functions fix their path; the older ones are M5's and M6's and are left for a migration that changes them, or a `REVOKE TEMPORARY` on the database, which is the owner's call because it changes every identity.
- **Generation-3 owner starts reached the web in B8c** (§17, B6a decision 15; B7 decisions 2 and 6; B8c decisions 2 and 3). The owner starts a brief, a decision and an entry-basis request and the decision probe on the Agents page; a candidate held only by a consideration request is not offered there, and SYSTEM starts its entry-basis request once the policy is installed.
- **An M6 test depends on the time of day** (found by B7's run, §18). A test clock started between HH:59:30 and HH:06:00 UTC runs 8 minutes ahead to stay out of the quiet window, and `test_m6_agents.py`'s source-refresh test then finds the fetcher's new evidence before its workflow's cutoff and fails. `AgentOffline6` can fail the same way when that test starts in the window; a rerun outside it is the workaround. Not fixed in M7.
- **A research suspension reaches a submitted entry only at the agent worker's next pass.** The owner's revocation raises the permission epoch, which the engine checks in its step; a shadow's integrity failure suspends the candidate without it, and the engine reads no research table. The worker withdraws such an entry (`UNAPPROVED`, §17, B6a decision 18) at its next pass; until then, and for as long as the agent worker is down, the engine can still take it if its entry condition holds while the draft is valid (30 minutes at most). Its position keeps its terms, as after a revocation.
- **A candidate's signal is computed twice.** The snapshot shows it from the agent's view of the candles at the cutoff, and the engine evaluates the entry condition again on its own finalized bars. Both read the same stored bars of the same decision bar; where they could differ (a bar corrected between the two), the engine's evaluation decides and the snapshot's is labeled as computed at the cutoff.
- **The comparison has run on scripted decisions only.** Its three runs are one synthetic fixture whose brief, coordinator and review are written rules, not models, so they show that the comparison works, not that a role helps, and say so. A comparison of real decisions needs agent-driven runs in isolation (M7-B and later).
- **"One fixture" rests on the generator's word.** A report's `replay.fixture_sha256` is what its generator was given, not a hash of stored rows; the comparison refuses differing or missing values but cannot tell a wrong one. The phase's runs build the fixture and pass its hash in one process.
- **The control replay starts each 21-day period flat** (§17, B6c decision 1). A position of the reference open at a period's end is not carried into the next, so the periods together are not one run of the reference over the agent run's window, and a period is short beside the protocol's sample minimums. Its labels say so.
- **A quiet tail can hold an M6 shadow open, and its store kept.** A window becomes final only when a publication after its end is staged. When nothing more is published inside it after the last mark, a shadow is not advanced again. The store's day cap spares a window it has staged whole, so the shadow stays open, keeping its store and possibly holding up the next rotation, until its owner stops it or the store reaches its byte cap (corrected after B6c's review; this line said the day cap ended it). The control replay closes such a window and ends a period not finished in time (§17, B6c decisions 6 and 10); M6's shadows are left as they are. It needs the feed to have published nothing between a shadow's last mark and its window's end, as when the PC or the feed was down then. The same holds for a shadow whose final advance keeps failing.
- **While a control advance runs, the research status reads `running` and names no job** (§17, B6c decision 7). The owner sees the runner busy without seeing why; a screening the agent queues waits behind it, as behind a shadow's advance.
- **Rolling the code back past B6c with a control job queued stalls the bridge.** M6's dispatch reads the job's candidate, which a control job does not have. `0009`'s downgrade refuses while any control record exists, but a code rollback that keeps `0009` (the mixed-version case) would need the queued control jobs canceled first.
- **The cloud session cannot run M6's research browser test now.** Its fixture's screenings need 40 GiB free (M6's envelope), and the container had 29.5 GB (§18, B8b). B8b's changed promotion texts are covered by unit tests until `BrowserResearch` runs on the owner's PC.
- **Left as M6 left them,** unless a batch touches them: the M4 and M5 request bodies' coercion, the read models' missing indexes, the research gate conditions of M6-B.
